# Fidelity — how far this model sits from a real machine

One row per thing that differs. **A row that is written honestly is finished work.** Closing the
gap is a separate decision, taken on the row, never implied by it.

Columns: what it is / what a real machine does / what this model does / the distance / **on
purpose?** / **how sure the real-world figure is**.

**Every `WINDSCALE` reading in this file was taken on a preset that no longer exists.** WINDSCALE
was renamed CALDER HALL in `56bdde6`, which in the same commit gave it `feedT` 310.9 and `sgDesP`
1.448 — its physics changed, not just its label. The readings are left under the old name on
purpose: moving them would attach numbers from one plant to another. None of them has been
re-measured under CALDER HALL, and until one is, a `WINDSCALE` figure says nothing about the
preset on the board today.

`ON PURPOSE` is one of:

- **YES** — a deliberate cut, usually the design pillar's "unless it is heavy to compute" clause,
  or a game constant. Nothing is owed.
- **FIT** — the shape is real, the level is a number solved to make one preset land. Honest, but it
  is not a measurement of anything.
- **ERROR** — the model is wrong and nobody has said why. **Nothing new may be built on an ERROR
  row without saying so out loud in the reply.**
- **DRIFT** — nobody decided this. It fell out of something else and has been treated as a decision
  since. Not necessarily wrong; just never chosen. Do not write `YES` on a row unless a decision was
  actually taken.

`SURE` is confidence in the **real-world** side, not in ours: **high** = textbook or well known,
**medium** = from memory of published figures, **low** = not checked against anything.

Nothing here is frozen. A better measurement beats any row on sight — re-measure, rewrite the row,
say what moved. That is not a scope question and never needs permission.

**The "this model" column is a record, not a reference.** Testing is against real-world physics
only: the "real machine" column, conservation, analytic solutions, published data. A figure the
simulation once printed cannot pass or fail anything, because the code that printed it has had bugs.

**The target is BEHAVIOUR, not a decimal.** This will never simulate a plant one-to-one. A job that
ends by chasing the last few per cent of a published figure has failed, not succeeded. Where a row
or a check compares a decimal, ask first whether the published source states a behaviour instead,
and compare that.

---

## Read this first, if nothing else

This is a **game about reactors**, not a reactor simulator. The four biggest honest distances:

1. **The presets are INSPIRED BY real machines, never replicas of them.** They exist to show the
   variety the simulator can build and to give the probes something to fly. A preset is judged on
   flying, closing its books and behaving as its family does; any figure off its namesake's (size,
   pressures, MWe, flows) is a deliberate cut, not a gap.
2. **The reactor physics is genuinely reactor physics** — point kinetics, a nodal core, a published
   DNB correlation, real steam properties. That half is strong.
3. **The pressure network is real physics too** — a momentum-and-mass solve, and the books close to
   zero. It carries **fluid inertia as one lump per run** (`I = L/A`, marched only): no travelling
   wave and no pipe-wall compliance, so a surge reads about 20 % high. BWR/4's coolant flow reading of **0.64** is a BUILD defect on that
   preset's drawing, measured 10/09/26 — see the pump sizing section.
4. **Time is compressed hard.** Xenon and samarium run 100× real speed (`XE_CLOCK`) and the
   compartment's heat capacity is scaled so a session can see it.

---

## Plant size — the presets are inspired by real machines, and nobody picked the sizes

**A preset is inspired by a real machine; it is never a replica.** Most real plants are not
documented well enough to copy, and chasing them stalled development. A preset shows one kind of
thing the simulator can build; its namesake says what kind. Every per-plant figure below and in
"Steady state against its family" is therefore **on purpose**: the row records what is given up,
it asks for no work.

**No preset states a power.** A `PLANTPRE` row is plumbing and a lattice; `D.power = latRating()`,
so the megawatts are an OUTPUT of the drawn core. Nobody chose one since 23/09/26: `PEAK_M` (solved to land the
stock PWR on 1198 MWt) is gone, the two ceilings carry sourced margins, and the stock PWR rates 842 MWt. Every row below
is whatever its own drawing happens to rate at, on a board and a mass budget sized for the old 1198 MWt anchor.

| what | real machine | this model | distance | on purpose? | sure |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| STOCK PWR | a 4-loop PWR is about 3400 MWt | 842 MWt (1198 until 23/09/26, pinned by `PEAK_M`) | ~1/4 size | YES: the board is the ship; no longer pinned | high |
| ...its containment | a large dry PWR containment holds ~15 m3 per MWt at ~0.4 MPa design; a compact one (NuScale's CNV, 6.9 MPa) is rated for what a break brings it to | 757 m3 of gas for 1236 MWt, so ~50x short on volume. **Since 21/09/26 its liner is rated for `sgDesignP()` (6.9 MPa)**, not `MAT_PDES` 0.5: 20-365 mm by span, 344.5 t of steel (was ~36 t), weakest cell bursts at 24.2 MPa (was 1.76). A 60 s steam-line break now peaks at 5.51 MPa with no breach (at 0.5 MPa a wall cell burst at 4.56 s and the wave wrecked the core at 4.96 s: BUILD); with protection armed it scrams at 0.12 s and peaks at 6.62 MPa against a 6.66 MPa drive. With protection armed the hot-leg break scrams at 0.12 s; its water now fits its cells to 1.3 % (it was 14.9x), and its worst gas cell reads 35.5 MPa against an 11.76 MPa drive: a pocket the liquid solve squeezes (row **...water pushed into a full body**), MODEL | volume 50x short, bought back with wall | YES: the board is the ship | high on the measurements |
| NUSCALE | 250 MWt per module | 694 MWt | **2.8× too big** | YES: inspired by | high |
| BWR/4 | 3293 MWt | 1059 MWt | ~1/3 size | YES: inspired by | medium |
| BN-600 | 1470 MWt | 1647 MWt (869 until its own 6.9 mm pin, 23/09/26) | 1.12× | YES: inspired by | medium |
| EPR | 4590 MWt | 965 MWt | ~1/5 size | YES: inspired by | high |
| RBMK-1000 | 3200 MWt | **730 MWt** (23/09/26: batch D's exact gamma cell, and 8 fuel slots in 100 given to control channels; 756 on 22/09/26, 775 on 20/09/26, was 1037) | ~1/4 size, and it is the only row whose size is no longer borrowed: the lattice states the real machine's cell (250 mm pitch, 80 mm bore, 18 rods at 13.6 mm, 7 m high) and `latRating()` answered 756 MWt in 175 m³ on 22/09/26, 4.32 kW/L against the published 4.2; on 23/09/26 its mean q' is 14.7 kW/m against 14.2 (`rbmk.js coef`). What is short is the plan area, not the cell: `LQ = 10` fixes the drawing at 400 channels against the real 1661, so the core is 5.64 m across against 11.8 | the rating is an output and was not aimed at; the 400-channel ceiling is the drawing's own limit | high |
| MSRE | 8 MWt — it was an experiment | 644 MWt (23/09/26) | **80× too big** | YES: inspired by, for the fluid | high |
| CALDER HALL | 182 MWt (Nuclear Engineering, Dec. 1956) | 150 MWt (23/09/26) | −18 % | YES: inspired by (drawn near the real core 19/09/26) | high |

None of this is a fault. It matters only when a number below is read as if it came off a real plant.

## The reactor on the board (plan-reactor-ui phase 6, 25/09/26)

**The box is the vessel now, so the picture's proportions follow the drawn core.** Before this every core
drew a fixed 9x12-cell box and one capsule of the same size whatever its kind, so a CALDER HALL pile and a
PWR looked alike and a ratio the drawing states could not be read off the board. The vessel's diameter and
height are now computed in `src/data/pipenet.js` and read through one door - `vesselDiaM()`, `vesLowerM()`,
`vesUpperM()`, `vesCylHgtM()`, `vesselHgtM()`, `vesselShellMass()` - and `layout.js` sizes the core and
rod-drive boxes over `MPC` (0.4667 m) from that door. A preset whose vessel outgrows its board grows the
board (`D.gw/D.gh`). The allowances no source states are game figures and are named as such below.

| what | real machine | this model | distance | on purpose? | sure |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| the vessel's inside diameter | a Westinghouse four-loop PWR: a 147.25 in core barrel in a 173 in ID vessel (NRC HRTD 3.1 rev 0909, ML11223A212), so ID/core 4.39/3.38 = 1.30 (MIT OCW 22.06: barrel 3.76/3.87 m in a 4.39 m ID vessel) | core diameter + 2 x (reflector, drawn in cm + barrel 0.055 m + downcomer 0.26 m), the last two off that reference. STOCK PWR **3.29 m ID on a 2.48 m core (8.9 cm reflector drawn), ratio 1.326 against 1.299, +2.1 %** (`vesgeom.js pwr`) | +2.1 % on the ratio; the absolute ID is small because the drawn core is | **allowances**: reflector drawn (BUILD); barrel and downcomer sourced. The check's 5 % band | high on the source and the check |
| the vessel's height | the same four-loop: 13.36 m assembled over 3.66 m active = 3.65 (MIT OCW 22.06) | lower plenum 1.50 + core + upper plenum + two 2:1 elliptical heads (ID/4 each). Upper plenum = the rod travel (the active height) + 0.8 m normally, but a core that boils in its vessel carries separators and dryers instead (4.0 m, DOE QTR 2015 ch. 4) and takes its rods in from below; a tube core is its channel stack + reflectors + shields, no vessel. STOCK PWR **8.91 m on 2.48 m active, ratio 3.590 against 3.650, -1.6 %** (lower 1.50, upper 3.28) | -1.6 % | **allowances**: lower 1.5, upper +0.8, separators/dryers 4.0 are game figures (stated); the 2:1 head and the rod-travel rule are the pressure-vessel standard | high on the check, medium on the unsourced allowances |
| the vessel's wall and mass | the wall is set by pressure and the code, thicker in the head | `vesselWallSuggest()` = max(25 mm, `wallSuggestMm()` off Barlow at the design pressure), head 1.6x; `vesselShellMass()` = cylinder + two 2:1 head plates at steel density, and it reads `vesCylHgtM()`, so the plena are weighed (before, the shell was core + 2 x 0.55 m) | none on the law; the Barlow wall checks are unchanged | the wall/head law is the existing one; only the geometry it stands on moved | high |
| **the box is the vessel** (decision 7) | a machine's box on a plan follows the machine | `machineW/machineH` for a core = ceil(vesselDiaM or vesselHgtM / MPC) cells; a rod-drive rider is as wide as its head. Board before -> after: STOCK PWR 60x34 -> **62x44**, NUSCALE **62x42**, BWR/4 **69x45**, BN-600 **76x40**, EPR **83x42**, RBMK-1000 **108x46**, MSRE **62x44**, CALDER HALL **78x71**, DUAL **74x82** (two cores). Vessel boxes in cells: STOCK PWR 8x20, NUSCALE 6x18, BWR/4 7x21, BN-600 6x16, EPR 9x18, MSRE 8x20, CALDER HALL 25x45, DUAL 6x18 each; RBMK-1000 is a tube core, 13x18 (`vesgeom.js box,0-8`) | the boards are larger; the 9x12 fixed box is gone | **BUILD**: the real vessel's proportions (decision 7) | high on the measurements |
| ports re-anchor and stranded runs re-lay | a nozzle is bolted to a face and a route is drawn round the machines; a redraw that swallows a route would leave it cut | on a resize `portReanchor()` returns `moved` and `grown`, and `relayStranded()` sends any run the new box overlaps back through `runLay()`, never edited by hand. `vesgeom.js relay`: lengthening the fuel column 30 % grows the box 20 -> 23 rows and **every vessel and drive port stays on a traced connection (22 of them)**; injected, a nozzle dragged 5 cells off its shell fails the check | none - this closes the core half of the backlog line 08/09/26 (the box grows under routed pipework); its pump half is still open | **BUILD, checked** | high |
| **the drive box** (decision 6) | rod drives stand on the head; a drive's housing is the rod travel and the mechanism (motor, gearbox) stands over it. A bottom-entry bank's drives hang under the vessel | `ROLE.rods` rides the head, height = ceil((active height + 1.0 m mechanism)/MPC), width = the head. **Redrawn 25/09/26:** one housing per bank, filled from its entry end as far as that bank is in (`csRodZ`), an amber tick at its demand (`csRodZDem`), its % and AUT/MAN (`csBankAuto`) under it; red when jammed, dark red on a scram. The reactor draws the absorber where the solve has it: a thin rod down the centre of each fuel column, under its dots, as strong in each node as that node's coverage `csNCov` (followers `csNFol`), so a bank shows only in the rings its rods are drawn in (26/09/26, `bankShares()`; checked by eye on STOCK PWR: no rod in the centre rings or the outermost), on the fuel cells, never between them; the part not yet in waits in its guide tube past the entry end on the bank's main ring (the ring holding the largest share of its absorber). `c.entry === 1` puts the drives under the vessel and the rods enter from the bottom | none; it keeps the channel-water path, the scram gear and the jam | **allowance**: the 1.0 m mechanism stack is a game figure (stated) | high |
| **each reactor looks like its kind** | a water vessel is a tall capsule with domed heads and drive nozzles; a boiling vessel carries separators, dryers and a steam dome; a tube core is a vault with a top plate and channel heads; a gas core a thick vessel; a sodium core a thin low-pressure vessel with cover gas; a salt core a vessel with a graphite matrix | `symAt()` picks the kind by `coreKindOf(cD)` = tube / boil / gas / salt / sodium / water, off the drawing only (`c.tube`, the coolant family, `coolBoils()`, `MODER`, `separates()`, `c.entry`) - never a preset name. **Redrawn 25/09/26 (`coreVessel()`):** the vessel to scale, one metre the same both ways, a 2:1 top head ID/4 deep and a hemispherical bottom head (row "the core's damage, on the board"), the core at its drawn diameter and height between the plena, the reflector at its drawn cm. The water is tinted on the pipes' own temperature law (`coolTCol()`): the core plane by plane at the ring-weighted `csNTc`, the upper plenum at the top plane's, the downcomer and lower plenum at the bottom plane's; over the collapsed level the vessel goes dark and a cyan line marks the surface. Internals: barrel and core plates on every kind; upper support plate, support columns, guide tubes and incore thimbles on water; separators and a dryer on boil; cover gas on sodium; graphite edges on salt; a 6-unit wall on gas, none on the low-pressure sodium and salt. A tube core keeps its vault. The 25/09/26 hashes of the old picture are gone and were not re-taken | presentation: the plena read the bottom and top planes, not the inlet and outlet nodes; the core's water shows one temperature per plane, so a radial coolant shape shows only through the fuel dots | **BUILD** | high on the geometry (it is the one `vesselDiaM()`/`vesCylHgtM()` door); checked by eye on STOCK PWR, BWR/4, BN-600, RBMK-1000, not on NUSCALE, EPR, MSRE, CALDER HALL, DUAL |
| **the core's damage, on the board** | a melting core's damage is where the melt is: burst and oxidised pins in the hot uncovered upper core, melt running down, a pool and crust in the lower head, a lower head that heats, creeps and fails | **25/09/26:** each node keeps its own marks (stage wash, burst slash, melt square, emptied slot, pool bar, blockage), the wash capped at 0.45 so the dot stays readable and dropped where the fuel has gone. The whole-box melt flicker (a fixed bottom 38 %) and the whole-box damage hatch are gone. Under the core: the pool `csPl*` at its own height off `eLhGeomA()` (the one function `eLhStep()` uses), bright over its solidus and dark red to orange under it, a crust line on top; the lower head's wall glows from the Draper point (798 K) to orange at 1400 K off `csHdTi`; creep life `csHdLife` as up to 8 crack marks and a HEAD CREEP tag; `csHdFail` as a hole with the melt running out while the pool holds any. Checked by eye with those figures written into the page's view only, not off a run that melts | **the pool sits in a hemisphere of radius R in `eLhStep()`, and the box, the shell mass and the height check size the vessel on 2:1 heads R/2 deep (`vesCylHgtM()`, `vesselShellMass()`).** A Westinghouse PWR's lower head is hemispherical, so the pool's geometry is the real one. The picture draws the bottom head as that hemisphere and takes the extra R/2 out of the straight lower plenum under the core (decided 26/09/26: drawing only), so below the core the picture is not to scale and the geometry door still carries the 2:1 head. **MODEL** (two geometries for one head) | the drawing is presentation; the head mismatch is not deliberate | high on the reading path; the melt picture has not been seen off a real run |

## The core on OPERATE and on the bench (plan-reactor-ui phases 7-8, 25/09/26)

**The readings come off the engine's own functions, and the bench reads and draws more.** The rows above
and below carry the physics; this records what the OPERATE core view now shows and what the bench gained.
Checks: `core.js 0`, `entry.js`, `lattice.js`, `paintsig.js`.

| what | real machine | this model | distance | on purpose? | sure |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| the radial offset split | a flux leaning to one side reads its offset off equal-area rings | `eCoreOffsets()` splits the rings at equal area, not by ring index (rings are worth 2i+1 unit cells, so a flat core used to read -50 %). `core.js 0`: a flat flux reads **radial offset -1.1e-16** (tol 1e-12); a J0 profile splits inner/outer **0.7858 against the analytic 0.7871** (tol 1 %). AO stays (top - bottom)/(top + bottom) weighted by POWER | the index-weighting error is gone | **BUILD, fixed** | high |
| PEAK Fq | a core's F_q is the local power peak (flux x fuel), not the flux peak on a ring that holds no fuel | `eNodePeak()` takes the power peak and a never-fuelled ring is excluded (`T.coreFracR`). `core.js 0`: a uniform core reads the peak **exactly 1.0** (tol 1e-12) and a ring with no fuel is **never the peak** | the flux-peak reading is gone | **BUILD, fixed**; F_q itself stands at 2.3725 and F_dH at 1.4560 (row "peaking factor `Fq`") | high |
| the reactivity ledger rows | the board reads every term the engine steps | `RHO_ROWS` gained EXCESS and GRAPHITE (the engine's `csRho` already summed both). `core.js 0`: STOCK PWR rows sum to NET to **3.0e-10 pcm** (tol 1e-9) | none | **BUILD, fixed** | high |
| fill level, void and leak | the OPERATE fill draws the collapsed level; void is a share of the coolant (<= 1) and a leak is its own figure | the fill now draws `csLvl` (was the loop inventory `(inv-88)/12`, a level's label on a number that was not one); VOID FRACTION shows the share `csVf` clamped 0..1 and LEAK shows `csVLeak` separately; the DNB wobble keys on `csNDnb` (was fuel temperature); FUEL TEMP reads `csTfHot` against the local limit with a FUEL MEAN row added; the three `PROMPT_F` readers move to the per-core prompt share (0.3 % today) | the mislabelled readings are gone | **BUILD, fixed** (help text for FILL LEVELS and FUEL TEMP updated) | high |
| the axial power strip and the AO trend | a tall core's xenon wave shows as power sloshing top to bottom over hours, which no 180 s ring holds | the strip is the plane powers off the live flux and fuel (`axialPowerOf`, the same reduction the bench section uses, rule 7); the trend is `vizTraceLong` on the `ao` channel, the last **45 min** of sim time decimated to the panel (`AOLONG_MIN` 45); the bench's axial harmonic margin is `derived().xeWave` | none | **BUILD** (decision 4) | high |
| **part-length banks, rod entry and axial fuel zones** (8.3) | a bank's absorber may be shorter than the core; a bank may enter from the top or the bottom; a core may carry axial blankets and zones of different enrichment | `bankLen` (a bank shorter than the core), `entry` (1 = bottom), `axFuel` (a per-node excess column, no tick state) are core columns and inspector knobs. `entry.js`: a bottom-entry bank's coverage **mirrors** the top-entry one node for node (0.0) and its AO reads the **mirror** (+54.170 % against -54.170 %); a part-length bank centred on a symmetric core reads **AO 0.0** (injected, full-length half in, -54.170 %); an axial zone of the same fuel changes nothing (0.0 pcm), a blanket reads its own worth (7845 pcm, symmetric top and bottom) | none | **BUILD** (decision 5) | high |
| enrichment per zone and the burnup knob | a core may zone enrichment and hold any burnup | `fuelEnrW()` is live (the cached `M.enrW` is gone); the inspector carries ENRICHMENT per zone, ASSEMBLY PITCH, BURNUP and the absorber's diameter and count. `lattice.js` checks that the enrichment knob at a `FUEL` row's own enrichment is a no-op (**kKnob == kPlain to 1e-9**) and that k-inf is monotone in it | none | **BUILD** (decision 5) | high |
| the bench's derived readouts and thresholds (8.1-8.2) | a reactor engineer reads k-inf, k-eff, migration length, H/M, worth per bank, the shutdown margin with the highest-worth bank stuck out, critical boron and a separate Doppler coefficient | `coreFig` now carries `kinf, keff, migM, HMratio, aoD, fz, fdh, bankW, sdmStuck, dopplerLaw, dopplerFit`, each off the one engine function (rule 7); the bench shows AXIAL PEAKING, CHANNEL PEAKING, AXIAL OFFSET, K-INFINITY, K-EFFECTIVE, MIGRATION LENGTH, H TO HM, BANK WORTHS, STUCK-ROD MARGIN, CRITICAL BORON, DOPPLER LAW and DOPPLER FIT. **Since 26/09/26 the stuck unit is one rod cluster, the most worthy one, as NUREG-1431/1433 LCO 3.1.1 state it** (was the most worthy bank, 8 clusters): `rodSlotWorth()` (`core2d.js`) ranks one drawn slot's quadrant copy by its own rod area per ring, first order on the rest flux; the core has no azimuth, so a cluster is priced smeared round its ring; a cluster in ring 1 outworths the same in ring 12 (`rodworth.js stuck`, fault seen to fail). **Since plan-bank-curve (26/09/26)** `derived().rodW` is solved (`rodStuckOf()`): for the 3 clusters the ranking puts highest, the bank's rod part with all banks in less that one, on the hot rest, against all banks in; the largest. BANK WORTHS are each bank alone solved on the hot rest (`bankWorthOf()`). Both are cached per predicted core (`coreWorths()`, `design.js`). Each bank alone and the stuck cluster lie between 0 and all banks in on STOCK PWR and CALDER HALL (`rodworth.js exact`, min-max; the old bench's first-order-on-the-rest-flux banks against its slope-measured all-in seen to fail). Bench STUCK-ROD MARGIN is the bank sdm (xenon- and samarium-free) less that cluster: STOCK PWR +4621 (cluster 506 pcm), NUSCALE +10316 (1671), BWR/4 +6551 (465), EPR +3339 (399), RBMK-1000 +7803 (718), CALDER HALL +10523 (368). **Since plan-bank-whole (26/09/26)** the stuck cluster is the whole change of the hot rest, all banks in less the cluster against all in, and each bank alone the whole change from banks out, no clamp (row "the bank's integral worth curve"): STUCK-ROD MARGIN STOCK PWR +6337 (cluster 578 pcm), NUSCALE +10200 (1478), BWR/4 +8198 (634), BN-600 +25068 (1422), EPR +4748 (471), RBMK-1000 +8763 (780), MSRE +138542 (1900), CALDER HALL +11134 (412); BANK WORTHS STOCK PWR 3895 / 3170 / 1811 / 867 pcm. PEAKING FACTOR warns at F_q **2.50** (the Westinghouse four-loop limit - one number now, where the bench amber was 2.6 and the design warned at 3.0); SHUTDOWN MARGIN and STUCK-BANK MARGIN red under **200 pcm**; CRITICAL BORON amber outside 0-1500 ppm | none new on the figures; the F_q limit is published | **BUILD** (decision 5); the 200 pcm sdm and the 1500 ppm boron ceiling are planning figures, not read at source | high on the F_q limit and the readouts; medium on the sdm/boron thresholds |

## Neutronics

| what | real machine | this model | distance | on purpose? | sure |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| point kinetics | 6 delayed groups | 6 groups, implicit Euler, 4 substeps per 20 ms; asymptotic period after a +50 to +200 pcm step within 0.6 % of the inhour equation on the model's own groups (`tests/physics/kinetics.js`, 18/09/26) | none worth stating | — | high |
| Boron on a plant with two reactors | each unit has its own coolant chemistry: its own boron, dialled to hold its own core critical | **closed 19/09/26.** A core's boron worth is the boron in its own water (`ST.bBy` at the core's node, advected like everything else); commissioning dials each circuit's water for the first core it cools (`eCoreDialBoron()`). The plant figure `SC_BORON` and its demand stay one: the charging system moves every core circuit by the same step. `core.js 8`: DUAL core 1 moves 0.004 pcm over 3 s with rods and boron held (1.8 pcm and 0.26 % heat before, when every core read core 0's figure). A consequence that is real: unborated or differently borated water reaching a core (an injection tank's `tankFluidB`, a dilution) moves that core's reactivity as it arrives; core 0 already read its own water through `SC_BORON`, every other core does now | two cores on one circuit share one water, so the second is not dialled | — | high |
| delayed-neutron shape of a plutonium core | Pu-239 has β ≈ 0.0021 against U-235's 0.0065 and its own group abundances (Keepin 1965), so a MOX or fast core has less margin to prompt critical per pcm | `DNG` in `design.js` states both Keepin (1965) shapes, and both sites in `step.js` read it; a `FUEL` row names its `dng` (absent = U-235 thermal) and MOX PLUTONIUM carries Pu-239. `kinetics.js`: both rows digit-for-digit, inhour roots at +100 pcm 54.87 s (U) and 63.64 s (Pu) at the same beta and Lambda (injected U-shape for Pu reads 54.87 and fails) | **closed 20/09/26:** the shape alone is worth 16 % of period at +100 pcm. No preset uses MOX, so nothing flies differently | **MODEL**, closed | high |
| decay heat after shutdown | **ANSI/ANS-5.1-1979 Table 7**, thermal fission of U-235, 23 exponential groups; after infinite irradiation and on the 200 MeV/fission the standard is normalised against, P/P0 is **6.591 %** at shutdown, 6.159 at 1 s, 4.750 at 10 s, 3.102 at 100 s, 1.901 at 1e3 s, 0.954 at 1e4 s and 0.485 at 1e5 s — fission products only. ENDF/B-VIII.0 `n-092_U_235.endf` MF=1 MT=458 puts the recoverable fission-product decay energy at **12.830 ± 0.07 MeV** (beta 6.500, gamma 6.330) against 194.077 MeV recoverable less neutrinos, which is **6.365 %** once the 7.5 MeV capture gamma the model carries is added to the total. JEF Report 17 ch. 9: ANS-5.1 sits within about 4 % of the Tobias experimental standard | **re-anchored 20/09/26.** `E_DEC_ANS` is the standard's own 23 α/λ pairs typed in; a group's amplitude is its α/λ over their sum, scaled so the total is `1 − PROMPT_F`. Each group is stepped by an exact exponential (`eCoreDecayStep()`). Until 20/09/26 it was an 11-group fit of Way-Wigner for 3 years at power, which read 3.767 % at 10 s and 2.318 % at 100 s against the standard's 4.750 and 3.102 — **21 % and 25 % low** — and 0.341 % at 1e5 s against 0.485, 30 % low | the sum is held at **6.500 %** against the standard's 6.591 (−1.4 %) so the rest point closes on the fission partition, and that −1.4 % is the whole error of the fit from 1 s to 3.5 days (`tests/physics/decay.js`, tolerance 5 %, injected ×1.1 reads 8.5 % out and fails). **U-239 and Np-239 since 24/09/26:** two decay states per core (`csU239`, `csNp239`), 0.474 and 0.419 MeV a decay (ANS-5.1-1979 eqs. 14–15), half-lives 23.45 min and 2.356 d (NUBASE2020), fed by R, the U-238 captures per fission off the law's own rates at the core's burnup (STOCK PWR **0.639**, inside the NRC's 0.6–0.7), the chain solved exactly over the tick; their heat comes out of each core's prompt share (`PT.corePrompt`), so the rest still closes on 1 (`decay.js`: 1e-16). Np-239 matches its Bateman chain to 1e-13 at 1 h to 3.5 d; the sum against the standard's fission products plus the chain stays inside 5 % from 1 s to 3.5 d, and with Np-239 given no energy the 1-day check fails (Np-239 is 17 % of decay heat at 1 day). Still missing, both raising it: neutron capture in fission products (the G factor, +3.7 % at 1e3 s and +7.1 % at 1e4 s on FRAPCON's table; a **stated cut**, no time-since-shutdown state carries it), and any isotope but U-235 thermal (a **stated cut** on the NRC's own recommendation to take 100 % U-235 thermal, ML021720702 sec. 3 item 2) — MOX and U metal presets carry the U-235 curve. Two panel readouts and the design-side radiation estimate still read the fixed prompt share (0.3 % at R 0.64) | the normalisation YES, it is what makes heat = 1 at rest; the actinides closed; G and the other fissioning isotopes stated cuts | high on the standard's groups (two independent transcriptions agreeing digit for digit, sum 13.183 MeV) and on ENDF's evaluation |

| control bank worth | a bank's worth grows with how much absorber is drawn (clusters, rodlets, rodlet bore, B-10 enrichment) and falls in a fast spectrum, where every absorber is grey, so a fast core carries many more rods of enriched B4C | **24/09/26:** the bank's fast part is weighed by the share of fission in the fast regions alone (`fastShareOf()`), not fast plus epithermal: MSRE's full bank 77 054 → 17 931 pcm (row "the bank's integral worth curve"); MODEL. **Since 22/09/26** `bankRho()` (`src/data/lattice.js`) prices the drawn bank fully in: Wigner rational self-shielding `A = S/4 · Σl/(1+Σl)` on the drawn rodlets (S, V, l = 4V/S), thermal part `A_th/(Σa V)cell` (utilisation), fast part one-group at critical `A_f/(νΣf V)cell`, blended by `fastOf(mth)` = (1-mth)³, which stands on the fitted `MOD_HALF`. `coreConst()` spreads it as a volume average (`rodA = -ρ/wMean(cov)`); the flux weighting is `coreSolve()`'s. **Since 26/09/26 each ring carries only the rods drawn in it** (`bankShares()`, `core2d.js`): a bank's absorber in ring i is its drawn rod area there over the ring's area share, `XNR²·N_b,i / ((2i+1)·ΣN)`, so a ring denser in rods than the core average reads coverage over 1 and a ring with no rod reads 0; the diffusion solve, not a kernel, carries the rods' effect to the next ring. It was a triangle kernel over `rinf = max(2.2, XNR/NB)` rings with each touched ring normalised to 1: STOCK PWR carried absorber in 13 of 14 rings where the drawing puts rods in 9. | the bank's integral worth curve | a bank's integral worth is S-shaped against insertion, and in a loosely coupled core the flux runs from a bank that enters from one end, so its first half is worth little (the Westinghouse manual's differential and integral worth curves, Figure 2.1-20, read) | **Hot since 24/09/26 (decision 1):** `rodCurve()` solves the core at rest on its own xenon and pellet and coolant feedback at each tenth of the bank's travel (`coreHot()`, `restSolve()`: Newton on flux and eigenvalue, the xenon absolute, the feedback as `F φ` so the coolant's axial tilt is in), once, after the burnup shape, which is taken hot and bank-withdrawn. A rod-held core's rest bank is found as the engine finds it: the rated excess plus every node term weighted by the rest flux squared (`coreRestRho()`), Illinois to the engine's 0.01 pcm from the book's own linear estimate (`rodX0Of()`); the linear book alone ignored how the peaked flux weighs xenon and feedback. Design against engine, the same state solved twice (`core.js`): **BWR/4 +315, RBMK-1000 +207, MSRE −152, CALDER HALL −104 pcm** on the bank's curve (GAP; on the cold curve by the linear book they were −1 015, −1 059, −339, −900, the fault the check is seen to fail on). What is left is what the design's rest does not carry and the engine's does: void (BWR/4 −854, RBMK-1000 +743 pcm), the graphite's own temperature (RBMK-1000 −216, CALDER HALL −148, MSRE −29) and a circulating fuel's precursor loss (MSRE 125). Rest banks: BWR/4 0.81, RBMK-1000 0.44, MSRE 0.34, CALDER HALL 0.42 on the design; 0.82, 0.53, 0.32, 0.30 in the engine. Before it, **24/09/26:** `rodCurve()` (`core2d.js`) solved the cold core's flux with the bank at each tenth of its travel and weighs its absorber by that flux, the measure the engine reads its bank by; `rodS()` interpolates it, and the rest position, the shutdown margin and the xenon window read it (the analytic `x − sin 2πx/2π` S-curve is gone). A rod-held core is brought critical by bracketed Illinois on the bank (`eCoreRodCrit()`), its position carried into every later settle. The bank's fast part is weighed by the share of fission in the fast regions alone (`fastShareOf()`): since phase 3a it had been fast plus epithermal, which put 56 % of MSRE's bank on the 30 keV formula (77 054 pcm, now 17 931) and bent `latMig()`'s blend. Full bank worth: STOCK PWR 4 222 pcm, BWR/4 5 086, RBMK-1000 4 441, CALDER HALL 16 657, MSRE 17 931. Rest bank: boron-held cores at the lead bank's bite; BWR/4 0.88, RBMK-1000 0.72, CALDER HALL 0.68 on the design's cold curve. **26/09/26, plan-bank-curve, MODEL, landed:** the curve had priced the bank as `rodA · impW(cov, φ)` on the flux the bank itself depresses, rodA times the eigenvalue's slope dλ/d(rodA) (Hellmann–Feynman), not its change, and it fell as the absorber grew (CALDER HALL at absorber density ×0.03 → ×3: 4629 → 3639 pcm). The engine booked its parts the same way (φ² on the live flux), and so did `coreRestRho()`. W·K is symmetric, so λ − λ_b = −1e-5 ⟨φ_b, (ρ − ρ_b) φ⟩_W / ⟨φ_b, φ⟩_W exactly: every part, design and engine, is now weighted by the base flux times the live one (`mixWA()`/`mixW()`, one body; `T.phiB` solved in `coreConst()` on the node terms no part books - poison grading, ring loading, burnup shape, axial loading - copied to `PT.corePhiB`, the tick's weights in `SX.coreMixW`), and the parts sum to the eigenvalue's own change. `rodCurve()` reads the bank's rod part at each tenth (`rodPartAt()`); `coreHot()`/`coreRestRho()`/`coreBase()` take one rod state (`rodSt()`: a depth per bank, one cluster held out). Checks (`rodworth.js exact`, `mono:pwr`, `mono:calder`, each fault seen to fail): cold, all banks in, the mixed parts against −1e5(λ_in − λ_b) to 1e-15 on STOCK PWR and CALDER HALL (φ² reads 3570 against 7707 and 3676 against 12489 pcm); the engine's parts at hot rest, all banks in, against its own eigenvalue off `PT.coreLamB`, every preset chunk run (STOCK PWR 2e-7, CALDER HALL 2e-15; φ² reads 11840 against 14627 and 4844 against 13804); the most worthy cluster at 1e-4 of its absorber against first order on φ_b, 0.999994 and 0.99984 (on the all-in flux 2.24 and 31.8); absorber density ×0.03, 0.1, 0.3, 1, 3 - all banks in at hot rest falls, STOCK PWR −5486 → −19656, CALDER HALL −10095 → −15188 pcm off the base, and the rod part fully in grows, 1930, 3691, 6014, 9062, 11873 and 8825, 10788, 11860, 12704, 13361 pcm (the slope measure: 1888 … 5239, 4171 and 4664 … 3662). Design rest bank against the engine's, on the bank's curve (`core.js`, GAP, the terms above): RBMK-1000 −58 → +22, MSRE −27 → −148, CALDER HALL −115 → −17 pcm (0.203 against 0.198 of travel, was 0.503 against 0.310). **26/09/26, plan-bank-whole, MODEL, landed (decision: at hot full power, the curve keeps its sign):** the curve had read only the bank's rod part, and as the bank goes in the xenon and the feedback redistribute with the flux, so their parts move too. The bank's worth at x is now the whole change between the two hot rests, `r0 − coreRestRho(T,x,0)` = 1e5 (λ_x − λ_0) (`rodWholeAt()`, `restAt()`, `core2d.js`); BANK WORTHS and the stuck cluster read the same way; `rodPartAt()` is gone and no `Math.max(0, …)` clamp is left on the curve or the bank worths. Checks (`rodworth.js`, each fault seen to fail): the bank fully in on a fresh curve against the two hot rests' own eigenvalues (`T.lamH`) to 1e-9, STOCK PWR 10916 and CALDER HALL 13480 pcm (the rod part alone reads 9072 and 12704, `exact`); the curve strictly rises over its travel on every preset whose bank draws no followers, STOCK PWR, NUSCALE, BWR/4, EPR, MSRE, CALDER HALL (`rise:N`; the same curve with the absorber's sign turned falls); the bank fully in grows with the absorber, STOCK PWR 2056, 3996, 6725, 10896, 16176 and CALDER HALL 9129, 11313, 12524, 13480, 14222 pcm at ×0.03 … ×3 (`mono:`); each bank alone and the stuck cluster in [0, all in]. | a ring's own composition | a node's k∞ is its own composition's: fuel in a water gap is over-moderated in an under-moderated lattice and runs hot beside it; a ring past the fuel's edge holds less fuel and more of whatever surrounds it; zones of different enrichment differ by their own k∞. In one-group diffusion the node term is (k∞ − 1) times its absorption over the core's | **since 24/09/26** (`ringRhoOf()`, `ringCnt()`, `lattice.js`): each ring's term is 1e5 (k∞,ring − k∞,core), both off the moderation law and scaled by their own fuels' published k∞ over the law's (`kScaleOf()`), replacing `−LAT_NF (1 − occupancy) + enrRho0` (`LAT_NF` 22 000 pcm, the one fitted number, deleted). A ring's slots are counted off the drawing (`latRevolve()`, conserved to 1e-12), its empty part taken as the core's own moderator: the blocks where the drawing has any, the coolant where it has none. A ring is narrower than a slot (0.67 of one on STOCK PWR), so its own blocks and control channels alias the drawn pattern (MSRE ring 0 read −131 000 pcm, a uniform lattice ±10 000 ring to ring): **its occupied slots take the core's own shares of fuel, blocks and channels** (user decision 24/09/26), its zone mix and its empty part its own. Its thermal absorption is Wigner's rational on the pellet's own chord, taken at the Maxwellian's mean speed at the neutron temperature (a black surface takes n v̄/4; 1.57 × 2 200 m/s at 565 K, `vBarOf()`); the uranium inside the black core leaves the thermal book. The front moves in at φ v̄/(4 N) on the core's thermal flux at half the rest burnup (`thPhiOf()`, `fuelSecs()`); each ring's worth is its law with and without (`poisonAt()`), graded and meaned as before (`corePoison()`). | the moderation law | a lattice's k∞ is fast fission × resonance escape × η f: water lattices are built under-moderated so the MTC is negative (Westinghouse Technology Systems Manual sec. 2.1.6.2, GE BWR/4 Technology Manual ch. 1.7, both read 24/09/26), and graphite moderates far less per volume, so its lattice peaks at a far larger moderator ratio | **the fit is gone, 24/09/26.** `latLaw()` (`lattice.js`): four regions in series on the cell-homogenised book. Over U-238's 1 MeV threshold: the Watt spectrum puts 0.699 of fission neutrons there; fission and capture are ENDF/B-VII.1's kT = 1.42 MeV Maxwellian averages (Pritychenko & Mughabghab 2012 Tables XVIII/XIX, read), scattering a black nucleus 2π(R+ƛ)² with R = 1.25 A^1/3 fm (hydrogen the n-p 2.9 b), removal by inelastic and by ξ over the band's lethargy (`nucT()`). Fast: the 30 keV book. Epithermal: narrow-resonance escape on ENDF/B-VII.1 resonance integrals, U-238 by Hellstrand with Blomberg's √T broadening and Sauer's Dancoff (a bore's rods see each other through its coolant; an open lattice smears its blocks among the rods). Thermal: η f at the neutron temperature on Westcott g, diffusion-theory cell utilisation. The level is each FUEL row's published k∞ on its own reference drawing (`kInfOf()`, row "the fuel excess"). `MOD_A/B`, `MR_PEAK`, `ETA_STOCK`, `MOD_HALF`, `FAST_RHO`, `modEta*`, `modTherm`, `AV_*`, `AM_K`, `modAbs`, `absK` deleted. `lattice.js law`: the formula on the IAEA 2D region-1 constants to 1e-9 (k∞ 1.125); U-238 RI against ORNL/TM-6376 Table 4.1's transport calculation, six rods at 300 and 1089 K, all inside Hellstrand's 2 % (no-broadening fault fails the four hot ones); cell utilisation to its homogeneous limit 1e-9 and a black pellet under it; STOCK PWR, BWR/4, EPR and NuScale all gain k∞ with water; STOCK PWR peaks at water over fuel 5.55 against its drawn 1.60, a graphite lattice at 142 (26×). A dry PWR lattice reads k∞ 0.81 (1.36 before the threshold region). Fast fission over threshold: STOCK PWR 4.6 % of k, CALDER HALL 2.8 % (ε ≈ 1.05 LWR, 1.03 natural U–graphite, as commonly quoted) | the level is FIT per fuel; the shape between lattices is the law's. U-235's epithermal absorption counts its 1/v tail from 0.5 eV at infinite dilution: STOCK PWR carries 28 % of its fission above thermal | law, level FIT | high on the laws and the read data; medium on the black-nucleus scattering (a textbook approximation, ~20 % at 2 MeV) |
| RBMK stability | the real RBMK-1000 was stable at full power under its automatic regulators and unstable below about 20 %. INSAG-7 annex I-3: the fast power coefficient measured above 50 % power ran from -4e-4 to +0.6e-4 beta_eff/MW (the positive end only at a void coefficient of +5 beta_eff), negative at the design working point, positive at low power. **Converted on the machine the measurement was MADE on** (beta_eff 0.005, 3200 MWt) that is **-6.40 to +0.96 pcm per % of rated**, and absolute pcm per % of rated is intensive for the same cell, so a smaller core of the same design is judged against the same band. **INSAG 2.1 also states the BEHAVIOUR, verbatim: "the fast power coefficient remained negative under normal operating conditions. At the time of the accident, the void and power coefficients of reactivity were both positive."** The pass condition at 100 % is therefore the SIGN, which is what the source states and a harder thing to get right than a decimal inside a band 7.4 wide. **The intensive argument was re-derived at the source 20/09/26 and it holds**: a_N in beta_eff/MW scales as 1/rating for the same cell, so a_N x rating is invariant and pcm per % of rated is the right common unit; the model's own beta 680 pcm does not enter the comparison at all, which is why the row "delayed-neutron fraction against a real beta_eff" does not touch this one | **redrawn on the real cell 20/09/26** (rows "Plant size", "a fuel slot may hold moderator"): 250 mm pitch, 80 mm bore, 18 rods at 13.6 mm, 7 m high, 775 MWt. `aV` +2150 pcm per unit void on the published band (row "the void worth's published target"), `aG` −2.88 pcm/K off the thermal book on fresh fuel (row "moderator temperature coefficient"). The fast coefficient is read about the operating point with flow, drum AND core inlet held, because the drum and the feed cannot follow inside a circuit transit (`rbmk.js coef`). **As it stands 24/09/26, on the moderation law and the hot rating (467 MWt): the user's 21/09/26 ruling restored as `aFfit` −3.2 pcm/K on the LWGR row, a stated FIT added to the law's own Doppler (−1.11) in `restFeed()` and read everywhere the Doppler is, so the core carries −4.31 pcm/K against the −1.2 INSAG-7 table II-I measured: 3.6 times it. Sized with the zero crossing at 50 % of rated on the hot rating (the law alone left +4.65 pcm/% at 100 %, no crossing; −5.3, sized on the cold rating, left the hot core negative at 20 %). `rbmk.js coef`: −3.33 pcm/% at 100 % (void +6.86, Doppler −10.27, moderator +0.08), +6.27 at 20 %, crossing 51 %; the void-zeroed fault fails. `rbmk.js low` (on the hot rating): the frozen-rod kick at 100 % decays, e-folding 15.6 s. That test had zeroed `coreKXE` in its growth phases, which drops the xenon worth and threw the frozen core to ~5.8 times rated: it now holds the xenon, and its fault multiplies the void slope five-fold about the same operating point, because the 20 s kick sees the slow coefficient (inlet following power, −7.5 pcm/% with void +2.8) as much as the fast one; that fault reads NaN, the run-away breaking the reading, as HEAD's doubled-void fault did (not chased). The flight to 20 % misses its 60 s hold (n 0.181 against 0.200 ± 1 %, rods 0.56, xenon −3 428 pcm against −2 649 at 100 %); HEAD misses it too (0.162). Class: MODEL (the law moved it), the FIT a stated behaviour fit, not a drawing.** Before that, **as it stood 22/09/26, after batch D (block coefficient off its law, block temperature off the drawing, samarium, 100× clock): −3.20 pcm/% at 100 % (void +7.04, Doppler −10.39, moderator +0.14, graphite 2 s share −0.005), +8.18 at 20 %, zero crossing 60.5 % of rated, and the frozen-rod flight at 100 % decays, e-folding 28.3 s; `rbmk.js` passes 46 of 47, the 20 % flight the one gap. Class of the move: MODEL (the laws), none of it a drawing change.** Before it, **as it stood 21/09/26: -5.198 pcm/% at 100 %, +7.356 at 20 %, zero crossing 51.6 % of rated, and the frozen-rod flight DECAYS at 100 % (e-folding 12.43 s, injected fault - void doubled - grows at 13.18 s). `rbmk.js coef` passes every check.** That is the real machine's behaviour and it is bought with one stated FIT on `aF`; the history below is what it took to get there and what is still owed. Readings on the way: **+1.941 pcm/% at 100 %** (void +7.18, Doppler -5.38, moderator +0.14) after the channel flow split, the graphite conductance and the two-phase riser bore landed, was +3.965 before them; **+3.166** after the pellet conductivity law (void +7.20, Doppler -4.18, moderator +0.15), which moved it the WRONG way for the right reason. Letting the inlet follow the power instead of holding it gave **-2.15 pcm/%** at the +1.941 point. At 20 %, fast: the real machine's sign throughout, PASS, injected void-zeroed fails. `rbmk.js low` grew at **3.36 s**, then **2.88 s**, and now decays. The 20 % flight still does not arrive: n 0.0142 at demand 0.2, the regulator at its 0.10 floor, the deliberate poison clock (row "xenon poisoning"; 100× since 22/09/26, where the same flight reaches n 0.090), not a defect | **21/09/26, THIRD pass — the behaviour is now RIGHT, bought with one stated FIT, and the decision was the user's: the preset exists to feel like an RBMK and to differ from the other eight, not to match a decimal.** `COOLANT.LWGR.aF` is **−4.0 pcm/K** against INSAG-7 table II-I's measured **−1.2** on Chernobyl Unit 4. `LWGR` is read by the RBMK-1000 and nothing else, so the FIT moves one preset; checked, the other eight read bit-identical (`core.js` 0/2/7 unmoved to every digit). Swept before choosing: `aF` −2.8 crosses zero at 79 % of rated, −3.4 at 64 %, **−4.0 at 52 %**, −4.8 at 39 %. −4.0 was taken because INSAG's own behaviour statement is stable above 50 % and unstable below, so the crossing lands on the sentence. **`aF` was the right knob and not merely the cheap one:** the positive void coefficient IS what makes this preset an RBMK, and closing the coefficient on `aV` instead (1284 pcm needed, against a published 2000–2500) would have bought the same crossing by throwing away the thing that makes the machine different. The measured result: **−5.198 pcm/% at 100 %**, **+7.356 at 20 %**, crossing **51.6 %**, and `rbmk.js coef` passes every check including the injected one. **The frozen-rod flight now DECAYS at 100 %, e-folding 12.43 s**, with the void coefficient doubled as its live fault injection (grows, 13.18 s). **But the flight was failing for a different reason than this row said, and that is the more important finding.** Standing xenon down alone makes it decay even at the old `aF` −1.2 (39.9 s), because the flight lets the inlet and the flow follow and both are stabilising; with xenon live the stock PWR and the EPR grow on the same test. So the growth was **the xenon clock (400× then)**, not the RBMK's feedback (row "xenon poisoning"), and this row's old claim that the machine "grows where the real one decays" was measuring a clock shared by every preset. The `aF` FIT is what buys the STATIC coefficient's sign and crossing — the quantity INSAG actually publishes, and what a player reads off the bench — and it is not what fixed the flight. Both are said because both are true. **Still open:** `aG` now reads −2.88 on its law against INSAG's +6.00 measured on a burnt core (row "moderator temperature coefficient"), and the 20 % flight cannot arrive while the poison clock stands. **20/09/26, second pass: the target is 0, not +0.96, the distance is the whole 1.95 pcm/%, and the coefficient is now SPLIT and every term inverted.** `tools/coefsplit.js` reads the four terms off the same rest pass and then solves, term by term, for the coefficient each would have to carry for the total to reach zero (a secant, because the flux solve reads the reactivity field and the terms are weakly coupled - doubling `aV` moves another term by 0.55 pcm/%): void +7.180 at `aV` 2150 on d(void)/dP 0.003340 needs **1622 pcm**, below every published figure (2000-2500); Doppler -5.383 at `aF` -1.2 on 4.486 K/% needs **-1.677 pcm/K**, past the published -1.5; moderator +0.144 at `aM` +5.635 needs **-74.0 pcm/K**, no published figure and the wrong sign for 4 % of the moderating; graphite's 2 s share +0.008 needs **-637 pcm/K** against a published +6.00. The terms close on the total to 0.0e0 and each is separable to its own coefficient. **No term reaches a negative coefficient inside its published band, and by construction the four ARE the coefficient, so that is the whole answer.** **Step 2's arithmetic:** taking INSAG's two mapping points as a LINE in a_w (an assumption, labelled one) puts the mapping's own zero crossing at **+4.31 beta_eff** and this drawing at +4.30, so a_N here is **-0.01 pcm/%** and the honest magnitude target is 0. **Step 6, the crossing:** the model's coefficient falls monotonically and never crosses in the range it can run - 20 % +15.23, 40 % +9.33, 60 % +5.86, 80 % +3.57, 100 % +1.94, 120 % +0.74 pcm/% before the pellet law was fixed, and **+16.40, +10.48, +7.03, +4.77, +3.17, +1.98 after it** - so it is unstable at EVERY power, where the real machine was unstable below about 20 % and stable above 50 %. **One mitigation, measured:** the model's two boundary conditions BRACKET the published value - inlet held +1.94, inlet following -2.15 - and INSAG states neither, so which one its measurement was made under is not knowable from the source. It does not rescue the behaviour: the frozen-rod flight grows with a 3.36 s e-folding, which is faster than any circuit transit, so the held-inlet coefficient is the one that governs. **The old distance of 0.98 pcm/% against +0.96 is struck.** 20/09/26, first pass: the whole void chain was ruled out as its cause. `rbmk.js void` computes d(alpha)/dP test side from published correlations only - Saha-Zuber departure on the St 0.0065 branch (Pe here is over 3e5 for any liquid conductivity between 0.5 and 0.7 W/m/K), Levy's profile fit, Zuber-Findlay with C0 1.13 and the churn-turbulent V_gj, IAPWS-IF97 saturation properties and IAPWS R1-76 surface tension - on this drawing's own held inlet, flow and pressure: **0.00308 per %, where the engine reads 0.00315** (re-measured after the riser bore landed and the core came back to 6.99 MPa; it was 0.00408 against 0.00371 at the old 8.07 MPa), so the engine is within 2 % of the correlations. `tools/voidsplit.js` splits the engine's own slope by mechanism, each frozen at its 100 % value: flux reshaping **0.0 %**, subcooled void **+0.7 %**, the axial pressure profile **-0.4 %**, the channel flow split **-4.8 %** flat; with all four stood down the engine reads 0.003858 against the analytic 0.003848, **0.3 %**. The four candidates carry at most 5 % between them and the rest is what the correlations themselves give. The same analytic on the REAL RBMK-1000's published channel (1661 channels of 0.00816 m2, 18 rods of 13.6 mm in an 80 mm tube, 7 m heated, 10 417 kg/s, 3200 MWt, 14 K subcooling, exit quality 0.145) gives **0.0033 per % at 7.0 MPa and 0.0042 at 8.07** - the real machine's own channel is as steep. **A fast coefficient at the +0.96 target with this drawing's `aV` 2150 pcm and Doppler -5.38 pcm/% needs 0.0025 per %, and no correct void model gives it.** What is left is `aV` or the Doppler term. Ruled OUT by measurement, 20/09/26: the **axial quadrature** - refining the mesh from 10 to 80 planes outside the checkout moves d(alpha)/dP only 0.003302 to 0.003218, -2.5 %, so 10 planes resolve the boiling knee; **`aV`**, which is on its published band by construction; **Doppler** - **and that clearance is WITHDRAWN 20/09/26**: the fuel rise was checked against the model's OWN law with the model's own literals, which is not a check. Solved on Fink's UO2 conductivity instead the rise is 213.3 K against the flat law's 276.9, so Doppler was **29.8 % OVER-credited**. **The pellet law is now FIXED** (row "the pellet rise reads one conductivity") and the coefficient moved the WRONG WAY with it: Doppler -5.38 -> **-4.18**, total +1.94 -> **+3.17 pcm/%**, e-folding 3.36 -> **2.88 s**, and the term inversions harden to `aV` 1284 (published 2000-2500) and `aF` -2.203 (published -1.1 to -1.5). `aF` -1.2 pcm/K did sit inside its published band and the driver it multiplies was wrong and is now right - and `aF` itself has since been taken OFF that band deliberately, to -4.0, for the behaviour (see the third pass at the head of this cell); and the **drift velocity**, now built and worth -1 % here (its own row). d(void)/d(power) at a held inlet is **0.003340 per % flux-weighted** where the +0.96 target allows 0.0025, but it is now measured to be RIGHT: see the reference above. The heated length's own drop is **0.095 MPa**, not the 1.86 MPa this row used to quote inlet-to-drum — most of that loss is the inlet throttle and the pipework, which sit on the edge and not in the core — so the flashing is worth 2.1 % of exit quality, and d(void)/d(power) went the WRONG WAY by 0.4 %. **No candidate is left in the void chain, and 20/09/26 that stopped being "nobody knows": the slope is checked against published correlations twice over, once on this drawing and once on the real machine's own channel, and both say it is right.** **The source was read directly on 20/09/26 (IAEA Safety Series 75-INSAG-7, Annex I) and it settles two things.** First, the quantity: INSAG says, verbatim, "Measurements of the FAST power coefficient of reactivity, characterizing the change in reactor reactivity in response to a change in power, showed that when a_w increased from -(0.2-0.4) beta_eff to +5 beta_eff, a_N changed from -4 x 10^-4 beta_eff/MW(th) to +0.6 x 10^-4 beta_eff/MW(th). However, these data were valid only for power levels of more than 50% N_nom." So the check compares the RIGHT quantity and the earlier guess that the band was a minutes-scale coefficient is **wrong and struck**. Second, it is a MAPPING from the void coefficient to the power coefficient, not a band of scatter: this drawing's `aV` 2150 pcm is **+4.3 beta_eff** on the real machine's beta_eff 0.005, which sits at the +5 beta_eff end, so the honest target is the POSITIVE edge **+0.96 pcm/%**, and the whole-band pass condition is lenient by its own width. The pass condition was NOT tightened - a check is not re-aimed around a number - but the note now carries this. The same annex's Table II-I gives Chernobyl Unit 4's fuel temperature coefficient as -1.2e-5 per degC and its graphite coefficient as +6e-5 per degC, the two figures this model already uses as `aF` and the `aG` target, so those are confirmed at the source. **20/09/26, second pass: there is nothing left to fix inside published data, and that is the finding.** The four terms are the whole coefficient, every one of their coefficients is on or inside its published band, the void slope is checked twice against correlations and once against the real machine's own channel, the fuel rise is now checked against an independent property law and is 30 % too GENEROUS, and no term can reach a negative total without leaving published data. The model is internally consistent, its inputs are published, and it still does the opposite of the machine at every power it can run at. **A hand reconstruction says the same of the real machine**: 2150 pcm x 0.0033 per % on the real channel gives a void term of +7.1 pcm/%, and 14.2 kW/m through Fink's pellet with `aF` -1.2 gives a Doppler of about -4.1, so INSAG's own table II-I coefficients assembled on INSAG's own channel give a POSITIVE fast coefficient of about +3 pcm/% where its own mapping gives 0. Either the measured a_N was not taken at a held core inlet, or one of the two published statements is not what it looks like. | the drift velocity in the void correlation | Zuber & Findlay (1965): alpha = x / (C0 (x + (1-x) rho_g/rho_f) + rho_g V_gj / G). The second term in the denominator is the velocity the bubbles make against the mixture, and in churn-turbulent bubbly flow V_gj = 1.53 (sigma g (rho_f - rho_g) / rho_f^2)^0.25, about 0.18 m/s for water near 7 MPa. Without it a channel reads the same void at every mass flux | **built 20/09/26.** `eDriftFluxA()` and its inverse `eVoidQualA()` now carry the drift; `eVgjA()` evaluates V_gj once per core per step off the node's own saturation state. Surface tension is a new curve property `sigK` on the IAPWS R1-76 law (`sigmaA()`), stated on water and **zero on every other curve**, so a fluid with no published surface tension gets no drift and reads exactly what it read before. `props.js` checks sigma against IAPWS R1-76 table 1 at 298, 373, 473 and 573 K to 1 %, that it vanishes at the critical point, and V_gj at 7 MPa against the published 0.18 m/s (reads 0.187); an exponent 10 % off fails the 473 K check | on the drawn RBMK-1000 the drift term is **4.2 % of the C0 term at rated flow, 8.3 % at half flow and 20.8 % at a fifth**, so it is a low-flow correction and it is now there. Core-average void 0.274 to **0.270**, d(alpha)/dP 0.003302 to 0.003269. It was measured BEFORE it was built that this could not close the RBMK stability row, and it did not: the disturbance's e-folding went 3.27 s to 3.29 s. **23/09/26 (batch Q):** a boiling rod bundle's pool on this law swells 0.023 per cm/s of j_g at 4 MPa and 0.026 at 7 MPa against Anklam & White's 0.0109–0.0132 (ORNL CONF-810806-8 eq. 6, THTF, `level.js swell`): a bundle asks a drift velocity about twice the churn value. **Closed 24/09/26 (batch K phase 3b):** the one drift law channels and pool both read is Zuber-Findlay's form with K 2.9 in a rod bundle, a FIT to that band (`E_VGJ_K`, 1.9x the churn 1.53). Read first: Anklam, Miller & White (NUREG/CR-2456, 1982) found the churn DFM's V_gj too low in the THTF bundle and Yeh's void correlation the best they tried; Yeh by hand swells a pool more than the churn law at the check's 8-10 cm/s, so it was not taken. `level.js swell` 0.0113 per cm/s at 4 MPa, 0.0125 at 7 MPa: PASS. RBMK-1000's core void 0.315 to 0.310, d(void)/dP (coefsplit) BWR/4 0.003238 to 0.003176, RBMK-1000 0.003148 to 0.003139; both re-flown 120 s, every check passing | **MODEL, closed.** C0 stays a constant 1.13; a pressure- and flow-dependent C0 (Dix, Chexal-Lellouche) is not built and is not claimed | high on Zuber-Findlay's form and on IAPWS R1-76; medium on K, a FIT to one bundle's swell (THTF, 3.5-8 MPa, low flux) carried to every flowing channel, with no slug or annular form |
| the channel flow split in a boiling core | parallel channels hang between the same two plena and take the same drop, so their flows split on the momentum relation over the WHOLE channel resistance. In an RBMK that resistance is the single-phase inlet throttle valve: the drawing states 1.00 MPa for the throttle and the lower water line, against 0.063 MPa of two-phase friction over the heated length, about 15:1 | until 20/09/26 `csChW` weighted each ring as 1/phi, the two-phase friction multiplier alone, as if the heated length were the channel's whole resistance. Built 20/09/26 (`eCoreStep`, `src/eng/core.js`): the weight is 1/sqrt(dp_throttle + dp_friction phi_i^2/phi_bar^2), normalised, with dp_friction the axial pass's own friction term (`E_AX[2]`) and dp_throttle whatever the stated core drop (`PT.coreDp`, `COOLANT[].dpCore`) has left over. A core that states no drop keeps the old law exactly, which is its right limit. `rbmk.js chan`: the weights carry the whole flow to 1e-12, every channel takes the same drop to 1e-12 (one channel put 5 % out fails it), a uniform void gives a flat split to 1e-12, and zeroing the stated throttle brings the old spread back | on the RBMK the ring flows went from **0.548 .. 1.310 of the mean, a factor 2.39, to 0.979 .. 1.012, a factor 1.034** - what a 15:1 resistance ratio allows.
