It was measured BEFORE it was built that this could not close the RBMK stability row, and it did not | **MODEL, closed.** Shared by every core, so it reaches BWR/4 too; a PWR's channels barely boil and its split was flat under either law. **Gravity is NOT in the split**: a hotter channel is lighter and that pushes its flow the other way, worth about 0.037 MPa against this drawing's 1.00 MPa throttle. Named, not built | high on the momentum relation; the 1.00 MPa is the drawing's own stated figure for a real RBMK's inlet throttle and lower water line |
| ...and what it does once it IS drawn as a direct cycle | no steam generator and no pressurizer; main circulation pumps of 1.5-2.0 MPa; feed pumps discharging somewhat over drum pressure through the regulating valves | the preset carries a drum per loop, no pressurizer, the governor on `SINK.loadDem`. Re-measured 20/09/26 on the redrawn core (row "Plant size", 775 MWt): coolant pumps bought at **1.236 / 1.253 MPa** (2.498 / 2.373 before the riser bore was fixed on 20/09/26, 2.84 / 2.64 on the 1037 MWt drawing) passing 1289.5 kg/s each; the feed pump bought at **9.367 MPa** into 6.9 MPa loops, down from 14.63 when `circHeadOf()` was taught the drum fence and from 10.45 when the feed-line bore was fixed (see the distance). The drawing states `rodSpd` 0.4/7 (row "RBMK-1000 power regulator"), and the rod banks are priced for it | coolant pumps now **1.24 MPa against a real MCP's 1.5-2.0, about 18 % under the low edge**, where they were +39 % over the midpoint and +57 % before the redraw. Nothing was tuned: the riser bore was fixed on physics and the head followed. The feed train still throttles most of its head away, which a real one does not. **CLASSED 20/09/26 (`tools/pumphead.js`, all nine presets), and the class is MODEL, not BUILD.** The feed pump's bought head splits into a `loopHeadAt()` base plus a `PUMP_MARGIN` term. `PUMP_MARGIN` behaves everywhere. The whole anomaly was the base term: 0.018-0.050 MPa on all eight other presets, **5.169 MPa on RBMK**. The cause is `circHeadOf()`, not the drawing. On a direct cycle the feed shares the primary's CIRCUIT, so the feed pump falls through `loopMap().partLoop` to `circHeadOf()`, which summed every run of that circuit at its own duty - the whole recirculation the drum stands at the top of. `loopHeadOf()` already cuts at `drumFence()`; `circHeadOf()` did not, and 20/09/26 it was taught to, using the `inLoop()`/`loopNodes()` walk that was already there for the same purpose. Base term **5.169 to 1.135 MPa**, bought 14.63 to 10.45, discharge 21.6 to 12.65. `tools/pumphead.js` on all nine before and after: **every other preset's bought head is bit-identical**, because only a drum fence triggers the branch and only the RBMK has one. **The residual 1.135 MPa is CLASSIFIED 20/09/26, and it is MODEL, in the BORE SUGGESTION.** It is three runs: `feed:efwpl-efwteer` 0.577 MPa at a 59 mm bore on 32 kg/s, `feed:efwteeb-feedl` 0.271 and `feed:feedl-freg1b` 0.223, both 203 mm on 374 kg/s. All three run at **15.0-15.2 m/s**, where the SAME runs on STOCK PWR are 138 mm and 635 mm and run at **3.0 m/s**. The cause is one predicate: `runBoreSuggest()` sizes a liquid run at `runOnLeg(r) ? circCoolOf(ci).vLeg : V_LIQ`, and `runOnLeg()` asks only whether the run's CIRCUIT has a core on it. On a direct cycle the feed shares the primary's circuit, so a feedwater line is sized at the main-coolant-leg velocity the coolant row states - `vLeg` 15 m/s for LWGR - instead of a feed line's 3. The bore is therefore sqrt(5) too small and the drop 25x too big. `circHeadOf()`'s pricing of what IS drawn is right: recomputed by hand run by run it reproduces 1.1354 MPa exactly. **FIXED 20/09/26.** The drum-fence walk was tried first and is WRONG: it cuts at the feed regulating valve, so the two runs carrying the pump's whole 1289 kg/s into its suction fell outside it and were re-bored 754 -> 1686 mm at 0.7 m/s. The predicate that is right is the FLOW: `runOnLeg()` now asks whether the run's duty IS `legDutyKgs()`, which is an identity and not a threshold - measured across all nine, every leg reads a duty ratio of exactly 1.00000 and the nearest non-leg reads 0.29. RBMK feed runs 59 -> **132 mm**, 203 -> **454 mm** x2, all three now at 3.0 m/s; the feed base head **1.1354 -> 0.0573 MPa**, bought 10.445 -> 9.367. It reaches more than the feed line, and the siblings are the same bug: the pressurizer **surge line** and the **HPI injection line** were bored at `vLeg` on every preset and are not recirculation legs either (STOCK surge -> 224 mm, HPI 102 -> 239 mm, at 3 m/s against a real PWR surge line's 356 mm and ~1.2 m/s, so the direction is right and the distance is smaller). Relief runs are untouched - `runReliefBore()` short-circuits them to the valve's own bore. BWR/4 is bit-identical: it is drawn through steam generators, has no drum and so no run of it changes class. **The MCP residual is classified too, and it is a deliberate CUT in the drawing.** Priced run by run at rated flow the drawn loop is 1.1475 MPa on the cold leg (of which the drawing's own stated 1.000 MPa core throttle is K 11.56 of the run's K 13.3, leaving 0.15 MPa of pipe friction), 0.048 + 0.033 on the hot legs and 0.008 on the suction. The real MCP's 1.5-2.0 MPa also pays for group distribution headers, 1661 individual channel throttle valves, pressure and suction headers and downcomers - kilometres of circuit this board draws as four runs. The 0.26-0.76 MPa shortfall IS that missing pipework, so it is a cut, not a pricing defect. The MCPs are MODEL too, and they are FIXED. `loopHeadOf()` broke pump0's 2.498 MPa into 1.148 on the cold leg (of which 1.000 is the drawing's own stated inlet throttle) and **1.342 MPa on the two hot legs**, which carried 1289 kg/s of a 193 kg/m3 mixture through a 377 mm bore at **60 m/s**. `runBoreSuggest()` sized every run at `circDesRho()`, which knew only liquid and vapour and handed a two-phase riser the circuit's LIQUID density. It now asks `circHotRho()` for a boiling circuit's hot side, which is the mixture at the design exit enthalpy - the same split `loopHeadOf()`'s own `runHotSide()` already made for the pressure drop, so this is a second caller of one idea and not a second idea. RBMK risers 377 to **754 mm**, MCP head **2.498 to 1.236 MPa**, and the core node came back from 8.07 to **6.99 MPa**, which is where a real RBMK channel sits (inlet header ~8.3, after the throttle ~7.6, outlet ~7.0, drum 6.9) | **MODEL on both halves, one fixed and one stated.** The feed pump's is fixed: a shared pricing law that skipped a fence it already had. It moves exactly one preset because only one preset has a drum fence - a defect can be in shared code and still reach one drawing. The MCPs' is fixed in `runBoreSuggest()`. It reaches every boiling circuit, so it moves BWR/4 too, and there it is a clear gain: BWR/4's 120 s void fraction went **0.786 to 0.365** against a published 0.25-0.55, closing that row's own check, and its four settle failures went to none. The cost is on BWR/4's first seconds: the enlarged riser holds four times the water and the plant now rings through a swing of about 40 % of rated over the first 2 s before settling, so its 3 s rest check goes 3.14 to **400 pcm** while its 120 s march is clean. That is the "BWR/4 as a direct cycle" backlog item - its drum IS the vessel - and it is named, not chased. The seven non-boiling presets are bit-identical, checked on `tools/pumphead.js` across all nine | high on the MCP band and on the hot-leg arithmetic; medium on a real feed pump's discharge; **high on the MODEL class for both halves** - the feed pump's rests on eight presets reading bit-identical before and after, the MCPs' on the 60 m/s the drawn riser runs at |
| RBMK core inlet pumping | an adiabatic pump leaves all of its shaft work in the water: between the drum and the core inlet the enthalpy must rise by at least v (p_in - p_drum), the isentropic figure, and a real pump exceeds it | **built 20/09/26.** `ePumpWorkA()` (`src/eng/transport.js`) hands every pump's shaft work to its discharge node as an enthalpy source on the flow the solve passed: w (p_out - p_in) / rho_suction / `PUMP_ETA`, with `PUMP_ETA` 0.85 the hydraulic efficiency of a large single-stage centrifugal pump. It goes through `eSrcAdd()` like every other source, so `SC_ENRES` closes and no mass moves. The expression is the INCOMPRESSIBLE one, so a machine whose discharge density is more than 2 % over its suction gets nothing. Commissioning's tube fit (`eSettleUA()`) now sizes the generators for the core's heat AND the primary pumps' work, or the plant sits about 1 % hotter than it was commissioned for | the RBMK's pumps now leave **3.179 kJ/kg** in the water against an isentropic minimum of 2.701, where 0.96 used to arrive (the figures fell with the pump heads when the riser bore was fixed, its own row). `rbmk.js boil` checks the pumps' own first law, w (h_out - h_in) = P_shaft, to **3.4e-4**, and the drum-and-core circuit's first law closes to **0.03 %** with the MCPs' 5.0 MW counted (`rbmk.js rest`, `drum.js 5`). The 0.36 kJ/kg still between the drum mixing statement and the core inlet is the two loops' drums leaving their downcomers at slightly different enthalpies - a drum asymmetry, not a pump term, and not chased. **A gas circulator gets no shaft work at all**: v dp overstates a compressor 6.8x on CALDER HALL's CO2 (442 kJ/kg against the isentropic 65 at its 1.94 pressure ratio), so the incompressible form is refused there rather than applied wrongly | **MODEL, closed for liquids; deliberately not built for gases.** Shared code, so it moves every preset: the tube fit absorbs it on the seven with steam generators, and BN-600's 120 s heat balance goes 1.053 to 1.072 on a check that already failed on the tree before this. The compressible form c_p T ((p2/p1)^((gam-1)/gam) - 1)/eta is NOT built, and Calder Hall's circulators really do put a few MW into their gas | high on the law; `PUMP_ETA` 0.85 is a class figure for a large centrifugal pump, not this machine's own |
| the flow work on a node that is nearly empty | a node's internal energy is U = m·u with u = h − p·v at the contents' OWN pressure and specific volume; contents too few to fill the volume at the solved pressure cannot hold that pressure's p·V | **since 21/09/26** `eAdvectStep()` books U = m·h − p_c·V with p_c = p·min(1, m/(V·ρ(p,h))), the contents' pressure where they fall short of the solved state (exact for an ideal gas at fixed T), and `pAdv` carries p_c tick to tick so the ledger books what the update did. A node drained past u = 0 (the 273.15 K datum) lands at u = 0 and the difference is a booked clamp. Before: `turbb` (0.006 kg in 11 m3) took V·dp of a 0.0024 -> 3.27 MPa jump and read h 5.9e6 kJ/kg, `condt` flashed to 7.72 MPa and both radiators failed on OVERPRESSURE, while `SC_ENRES` read 1e-7 because it booked U at the same solved pressure. After: worst h over 9 s of the STOCK PWR steam-line break 3028 kJ/kg, condenser peak 0.037 MPa, `SC_ENRES` 5e-8; STOCK PWR 120 s energy closes to 4e-12 and its heat balance reads 1.013 | the contents' pressure is the ideal-gas scaling of the solved one, not a solved EOS p(ρ, u); the u = 0 floor is a guard on an explicit donor at CFL ~1, not a law | YES on the scaling (a solved p(ρ, u) per node is heavy) | high on the measurements; not injected: no check fails on the old law |
| RBMK scram and the displacer | INSAG-7: every RCPS rod driven at 0.4 m/s, 18–21 s for the full stroke; a 4.5 m graphite displacer hangs under each absorber on a telescope with 1.25 m of water above and below it at full withdrawal, so a scram first drives the bottom water out and adds reactivity in the lower core, by an amount that depends on the power shape | `SCRAM` MOTOR DRIVEN, `rate` 0.4/7 of travel a second, mass 20 t (the gravity row's rods and drives, a servo in place of the latch; no measured figure); ARCHPRE RBMK takes it. `FOLL.tipLen`/`tipGap` are fractions of the core's height: GRAPHITE DISPLACER 4.5/7 and 1.25/7, WATER and BORATED STEEL a gap of 0 (their behaviour before, BORATED STEEL's 4.0 nodes now 0.4 of the height); `follHi()` (core2d.js) is the one expression the engine and the design copy read, and the bench draws the gap. `rbmk.js scram`: stroke 17.5 s; the bank's bottom node fills with graphite after **3.13 s** (1.25 / 0.4 = 3.125); from full withdrawal on a bottom-leaning flux (AO −0.09, a xenon tilt) rods and followers add **+17.3 pcm (0.026 β)** in the first 3.1 s, on a top-leaning one (AO +0.34) −18.9 pcm; injected, a water follower, the rise is gone (−5.1). **Since 24/09/26 the follower is a material** (`FOLL[].mat`, `folRhoOf()`): its worth is the whole core's k∞ off the law with every rodded bore's contents replaced by it, a control channel's bore (its water if piped) or a fuel slot's rodlet bores, the follower's own absorption self-shielded on Wigner's rational region by region, its 1/v part exact through the epithermal band; spread over the bank's own reach. The displacer is graphite at the block row's density: **+1 530 pcm core-wide on RBMK-1000** (`lattice.js fol`), `tipRho` 1 562 a node; the borated steel ASTM A887 304B6, 1.7 wt% B (Sun et al., Materials 2021, read), on the 316 row: BN-600 −2 231 core-wide (was −420). Filling a whole 80 mm bore solid, even plain steel takes 10 000 pcm out of RBMK-1000: a real follower is far thinner, and BN-600's borated-steel follower is BUILD (the real machine's is not read) | stroke 17.5 s against 18–21 (the drive speed is the stated figure); INSAG-7 gives no magnitude for the positive scram, so +77 pcm is not measured against anything | the displacer's worth off the law, no FIT left; the geometry and the speed are the published ones; the follower fills its whole bore (no follower diameter drawn). WINDSCALE rides the same displacer row (ARCHPRE HTGR) and moves with it; not re-flown beyond 60 s | high on geometry and speed |
| rod drive speed | a PWR bank steps at most about 72 steps/min over a ~228-step stroke, roughly three minutes end to end | **closed 20/09/26.** `ROD_SPD0` is 1/190 of travel a second, a 190 s stroke. The knob stays and only its reference moved: `D.machines[core].rodSpd` via `rodSpdOf()`, the bench `ROD DRIVE SPEED` input and `FIG.rodSpd`, priced at `nbank·ROD_BANK_T·(rodSpdOf(c)/ROD_SPD0−1)` — the old 83 s drive now costs ~11.5 t per bank instead of nothing. `SCRAM[].rate` untouched (a scram is a separate mechanism); RBMK keeps its own `rodSpd` 0.4/7 (INSAG-7). `knobs.js rod`: 1/`rodSpdOf()` is 190 s to 10 s, and the mass term is 0 t at the reference and positive above it | the stroke matches the published figure. CALDER HALL no longer holds rated on it: the bank walks out to the `arLo` 0.10 floor and power fades to 0.06 in 120 s (BUILD — the preset's steam side is half-drawn and its commissioning point needs more withdrawal than the floor allows; the tune does not read drive speed, observed not changed). STOCK, MSRE, RBMK-1000 hold rated; `kinetics.js` passes | decided 20/09/26: the reference is the published stroke | medium |

## Heat and thermal limits

| what | real machine | this model | distance | on purpose? | sure |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| the axial pressure profile in a channel | a heated channel loses pressure up its length to the weight of the column, wall friction and the acceleration of a mixture that is getting lighter. Saturation temperature follows the pressure down, so water saturated at the bottom is superheated against the local saturation higher up and flashes with no heat added | **built 20/09/26** (`eCoreAxialA()`, `src/eng/core.js`). Ten plane pressures per core, gravity + Darcy friction (Blasius under Re 1e5, Filonenko/Petukhov over it) + acceleration, homogeneous two-phase multiplier, liquid density at the plane's own temperature. Anchored so the ten planes average `ST.csPCore[c]` exactly, so the pressure network is untouched and the profile adds no pressure. The march now reads `T_sat`, `h_fg`, ρ_g/ρ_f, the drift, the Jens-Lottes superheat and W-3's pressure at each plane's own pressure. Checked (`rbmk.js axial`): the anchor holds to 1e-12; with the flow stopped and the void flattened the profile is 45202 Pa against IF97's 45275 Pa of column weight; the friction share is 36486 Pa against 36427 Pa by hand at the same Re; the flashing is 0.00298 against IF97's 0.00315 | RBMK-1000, the deepest case: **0.095 MPa over the 6.30 m the ten plane centres span**, of which 0.052 MPa friction at Re 3.7e5, against the **1.00 MPa the `LWGR` row draws across the core**. That is not a defect in either: the drawn figure is the whole inlet-throttle-plus-pipework loss, which sits on the EDGE upstream of the heated length, and this is the heated length's own. Flashing is worth **2.1 % of the channel's exit quality**. The 5.3 % the flashing check sits under IF97 is structural: the march carries `h` as `c_p·T`, so its `dh_f/dp` is the coolant row's secant `c_p` times `dT_sat/dp` | — | high: every term is checked against IF97 or against the correlation by hand |
| departure from nucleate boiling, water | a pressurised core's local CHF ratio on W-3 (Tong 1967, 1000–2300 psia); a boiling core's critical POWER ratio over its boiling length (GEXL, CISE-4): the power at which the worst channel dries out, over the power it makes | **closed 24/09/26 (batch K phase 2), no constant.** A pressurised core: W-3 in the paper's own units, Biasi past quality 0.15, below W-3's mass-flux floor G_f = 1356 kg/m²s CHF = (1 − G/G_f)·q_pool + (G/G_f)·q_W3 (row history: plan lowflow-chf, 23/09/26; Zuber × Griffith (1 − α) × Ivey-Morris, α held at 0.8, a FIT), every margin capped by its plane's counter-current flooding limit. A test-side rebuild in psia, lbm/h/ft², inches and Btu/lbm agrees to 3e-6 (`core.js 0`). A boiling core (`dnbLaw:"cpr"`, the BWR and RBMK rows): CISE-4 (Gaspari et al. 1974 as INL/EXT-06-11725 eqs A24–A28, SI), x_c = a·L_B/(b + L_B), per plane off the plane's own pressure and the ring's G, the lattice's heated equivalent diameter, perimeter factor 1 (the cell has no unheated wall); the ring's rises scaled by λ at held flow and inlet, CPR the least λ at which a plane end reaches x_c over the boiling length below it (`eCprRingA()`, bisection to 20, 50 steps); a node reads its ring's last-pass CPR from the crisis plane upward. `cpr.js`: cise (the formula typed here, 2e-16; the two branches of a meet at G* to 1.1e-4; b 0.553 m at 7 MPa, G 1000, D 12 mm), cpr (a uniform channel against the closed form λ = (a h_fg + h_sat − h_in)/((b + L) q'), 1e-6; fault: L_B from the inlet), pwr, bwr (fault: CISE's D in mm reads 0.241), rbmk, boil, salt, gas. Rest readings, every law raw: STOCK PWR DNBR **2.46** (Byron/Braidwood UFSAR Table 4.4-1: > 2.0 at nominal), NUSCALE 3.02, EPR 1.82, DUAL 3.02, BWR/4 MCPR **2.23** (crisis at ring 0, plane 7), RBMK-1000 CPR **1.40** (SRS-43: over 1.0; Chernobyl-4 1.115 on the Soviet correlation, Adams et al. 1991), no node departed at rest on any. A rest pass (dt 0) latches no departure: it read CPR as low as 0.77 on RBMK-1000's unsettled first passes and latched 180 nodes into the settle. The ring search costs ~1 % of an RBMK tick | BWR/4's MCPR 2.23 over the 1.2–1.6 a BWR runs at (HRTD 1.8: operating limit 1.2–1.5; Browns Ferry 2 cycle 23 COLR MCPR_p 1.54–1.57): it is rated off the bought surface flux (row the rating margin), not off its own crisis law. CISE-4's own validity range was not found; CISE-GE's 300–1400 kg/m²s is the only one stated | BWR/4's operating margin: YES, a cut, the rating is not asked of its crisis law. PWR and RBMK: no distance found | high on W-3 and the pool law; medium on CISE-4 (read at a secondary source, its D exponent 1.4 checked by the b it gives) |
| ...its level | the design limits: W-3 1.30 (Tong 1967); WRB-1/WRB-2 ~1.17; WRB-2M ≥ 1.14 (AP1000 DCD Tier 2 §4.4, ML071580898); a BWR's MCPR safety limit ~1.07 (NRC HRTD GE BWR/4 Technology Manual 1.8, ML11258A297) | **closed 24/09/26 (batch K phase 2).** `dnbrK`, `PT.coreDnbrK`, `eCoreDnbrFit()`, `P.dnbrK` and every `COOLANT[].dnbr` are gone. `P.dnbr0` / `PT.coreDnbr0` are commissioning's rest reading of each core's own law, and the RPS setpoints are rebuilt off it (`engRpsSets()`). One table, `DNB_LIM` (design.js): W-3 1.30, CPR 1.07, sodium, salt and gas borrow W-3's; LO DNBR and its latch read each core's own. The readouts name the law (DNBR or MCPR, `uiCrisis()`) | the non-water laws borrow W-3's 1.30: no published limit exists for them | YES on the borrowing | high on the limits |
| ...sodium, salt, helium | no published DNB correlation exists for these | first-principles margin ratios, not correlations | honest, unvalidated | YES, nothing better exists | high |
| clad temperature at a boiling node | BWR exit about 570 K, just above saturation; PWR hot-channel clad 618–623 K | re-read 10/09/26 after the field inlet, its lag and the `LWGR` re-pin: **BWR 570.5 K**, **PWR 621.8 K**, **RBMK 568.3 K** (08/09/26 read 571.1, 621.8, 567.0). `coreStep()` is a real two-leg series now — a fixed solid conductance (pellet, gap, clad wall) and a live coolant film — and the film is the better of a single-phase flow term and Jens-Lottes (1951) nucleate boiling, `25·(q″/1e6)^0.25·exp(−p/6.2)`. The bareness penalty moved off the node's total void onto `vLeak`, the vessel's own shortfall of water: bubbles are a bonus, an uncovered rod is not. **Superseded for BWR/4 from 10/09/26**: after the loop-head work that preset rests at `flowNet` 0.567 and `n` 0.507 at 8 s, so 573.8 K is no longer a reading at its old operating point — hottest clad 567.2 K against saturation 567.3. STOCK PWR at the same instant reads 613.5 K at `n` 0.900. Neither is a like-for-like re-read and the row's own figures are not rewritten on them | a few K, inside the correlation's own scatter. **Re-read 10/09/26** at 8 s after `coreInH()` landed: **BWR 573.8 K** against saturation 566.0, **PWR 621.3 K**. RBMK is not readable — that preset is excursing by 8 s, so its 567.0 K is superseded by nothing and stands as a `dT0` 30 figure | — the ERROR is closed. The `max()` names no fluid: sodium, salt and helium all put the nucleate wall above their own single-phase one (1207 vs 998 K, 1767 vs 1063, 2005 vs 1015), so a non-boiling coolant keeps the flow term untouched. Jens-Lottes is a **water** fit and would be an extrapolation on boiling sodium, which no preset reaches | high on the correlation, high on the measurement |
| heat transfer past departure | post-CHF film boiling, water, 1–3 kW/m²/K; the wall rewets when it falls under the minimum film-boiling temperature T_min, which falls toward saturation as the pressure rises | `E_DNB_FILM` 0.10 of the **single-phase coolant film** (22.7 kW/m²/K on the stock BWR) = 2.3 kW/m²/K. **Rewet since 24/09/26 (batch K phase 2):** a water core's departed node holds film boiling while its can is over Groeneveld & Stewart's T_min at its plane's own pressure (TRACE V5.0 eqs 6-128–6-131: 557.85 + 44.1 P − 3.72 P² K to 9 MPa, above it the 9 MPa superheat falling linearly to nothing at the critical pressure; `eTminWA()`), 656.6 K at 15.5 MPa, 38.7 K over saturation where the old fixed 150 K asked 768 K. Sodium keeps its 150 K (`E_DT_LEID`), no published T_min being found for it. `cpr.js tmin`: T_min by hand at 1, 7, 15.5 MPa, 0; a departed node 1 K over T_min stays, 1 K under rewets (fault: the fixed 150 K rewets it) | inside the band; T_min on its published law | sodium's rewet: YES, nothing published found | medium on the film band, high on T_min |
| clad oxidation in a blocked channel | runs out of steam and self-limits | never runs out | oxidation is unlimited | YES, the "heavy to compute" clause, invoked out loud | high |
| ...and the correlation's own ceiling | Cathcart-Pawel (ORNL/NUREG-17, 1977) is measured 1273–1773 K. Urbanic & Heidrick (J. Nucl. Mater. 75, 1978) is measured 1323–2123 K, two branches split at 1850 K (the alpha-beta change). The H2 and the heat follow ALL the oxygen the metal takes up, not only the oxide layer. NRC ML021680052 reads Urbanic 3 % under Cathcart-Pawel at 1473 K | **rewritten 21/09/26.** `eOxRateA()`: Cathcart-Pawel's total-oxygen weight-gain law, 0.3622·exp(−39940/RT) (g/cm²)²/s, to `E_OX_TSW` 1773 K; Urbanic-Heidrick 29.6·exp(−16820/T) to 1850 K and 87.9·exp(−16610/T) above, (kg Zr/m²)²/s. All three are carried as ZrO2-equivalent thickness, so the ECR is the licensing ECR. Baker-Just is gone. The Cathcart-Pawel branch used to be the oxide-LAYER law (2.252e-6·exp(−18063/T)), which leaves out the oxygen dissolved in the metal: it under-made H2 and heat by 26 % at 1473 K. `ZR_H2` is now exact, 2·2.01588/91.224. Checked (`clad.js ox`): weight gain at 1473 K, 100 s against the published law; Urbanic/Cathcart 0.932 at 1473 K against NRC's 0.97; no temperature 1073–2100 K is answered above its law's data (fault: a switch at 1850 K goes red at 1774 K); H2/Zr = stoichiometry to 1e-9 over a bared core in steam | **1.** The rate constant steps DOWN to 0.640× at 1773 K (the H2 rate to 0.80×). The two measurements disagree there; the code reproduces both. **2.** The law is asked from `E_OX_T0` 1073 K, 200 K under Cathcart-Pawel's 1273 K floor (a GAP in `clad.js ox`). **3. closed 23/09/26:** the can melts (row "molten Zircaloy dissolves the fuel...") and the reaction runs on the node's own Zr metal inventory, molten or not; Urbanic-Heidrick is still the rate law for molten Zr, the only one this row reads | **1** no, it is the data. **2** DRIFT, not weighed. **3** YES: no separate law for a molten Zr pool's oxidation | high on the laws (read at NRC ML021680052 and the KNS 2006 CATHENA restatement of Urbanic-Heidrick, not at the original papers) |
| what a failed pin releases | NUREG-1465: a burst clad vents the GAP (5 % of the noble gases and of the volatiles, none of the refractories) and then stops; a molten pellet lets go of the early in-vessel share on top (PWR: noble 0.95, iodine 0.35, Ba-Sr 0.02; BWR iodine 0.25). The groups move differently: noble gases are insoluble and leave with the steam; halogens and alkali dissolve and stay in the water; refractories stay in the vessel. TMI-2: essentially all the noble gas reached the building, about 1e-5 of the core iodine did | **rewritten 21/09/26; the dose-fit `E_RELK` is deleted.** Three groups, each carried as one nuclide's equilibrium mass at rated power (Xe-133, I-131, Ba-140; yield × fission rate / λ). `eFpRelease()`: per node, NUREG-1465's gap share × the clad's failure (1 from ECR 17 %) + its in-vessel share × the melt, weighted by the node's rest flux; the released kg is one-way (`csFpRel*`). Noble gas and volatiles land in the core's water as hydrogen does (`fpNBy`, `fpVBy`) and ride `eAdvSp()`, the one donor law hydrogen now also rides: a gas with the vapour, a dissolved species with the water (the steam at 1/1e4 of it). Noble gas also takes hydrogen's bubble rise (`eGasRise()`, both callers). At an opening the noble gas rides the plume, the volatiles stay in the water it pours (`roomFpW`) save the flashed steam's 1/1e4. The room's airborne fields ride `eRoomAdvect()` and the gas moves beside hydrogen. A salt core's inventory is in its salt from commissioning. Checked (`clad.js fp`, `fb`): gap 0.05/0.05/0 and melt 1.00/0.40/0.02 to 1e-12, one-way; books (fuel + water + room + booked) close to 3e-16 of inventory over 6 s with a hot-leg pipe shot; building air after 6 s: 94.5 % of the released noble gas, 2.9e-5 of the released volatiles, 99.997 % of the volatiles in water; noble gas and hydrogen leave the same openings in the same share to 1e-9 until the clad makes hydrogen of its own; both faults (fractions zeroed) go red | **1.** Three nuclides stand for the whole inventory; their yields, half-lives, gamma energies, the NUREG-1465 fractions and the 1e4 partition are as commonly quoted, **not read at source**. **2.** The released mass does not decay: a tracer at its release-time mass. **3.** Refractories never leave the vessel; with a melt-through they should go with the corium. **4.** No NUREG-1465 table exists for sodium, salt or gas cores: they use the PWR one. **5.** The coolant-activity terms that already fed `SC_RELEASE` (SGTR, relief, tank burst) stay as they were; `SC_RELEASE` no longer hears from the fuel | **1** no, sources to read. **2, 3, 4** YES. **5** YES, a separate model of coolant activity | medium on the fractions, high on the ordering and the books |
| the decay heat the released mass carries | a core that has let go of its volatiles has handed that share of its decay heat to wherever they went | **deliberate cut, 21/09/26.** The core keeps ALL its decay heat; the released fission products are a tracer and the energy books do not see them | a few per cent of decay heat in the wrong place after a melt, none before one | YES, decided in the batch H plan | high that it is small before a melt |
| the absolute dose scale | a dose is Sv from a source term, a geometry and a time | **not claimed, 21/09/26.** The crew dose stays a 0–100 game quantity. What is physics is its SHAPE: the room's fission products weigh in as gamma power over rated (λ·N_A/M × mean gamma MeV per kg), on an r⁻² kernel with the board's own attenuation from the crew's box (`PT.radAirK`, the same law the core's kernel uses); moving 1 kg from the nearest cell to the farthest divides the dose by the kernel's ratio exactly (`clad.js fp`). The mean gamma energies (0.045, 0.38, 0.18 MeV) are as commonly quoted, not read at source; the kernel is centred on the crew's box, not on each crew cell | the whole absolute scale | YES, decided in the batch H plan | high on the shape, none claimed on the scale |
| **where the hydrogen the clad makes goes** | it is barely soluble in water and fourteen times lighter than air, so it comes out of solution, collects under every high point and in the pressurizer steam space, and goes out of the valve on top with the steam. It was clamped to the tick's highest concentration unbooked, and on a shot pipe with every pin burst it left 0.9717 of what was put in where the noble gas left 0.9776; now both 0.97791. `clad.js h2`: water + booked + what left by the openings closes to 2e-15 over 6 s (fault: the old clamp, 6.5e-3). 08/09/26. `s.h2By` still advects with the mass, and the gas is carried by two terms. `h2RiseStep()` drifts it up every open path at `H2_RISE` 0.25 m/s, on the path's own area over the donor node's own volume, conserving what it moves. A steam nozzle (`ed.gasAt`, below) hands over the node's gas at the vapour's own concentration `c/x`, capped at what the node holds. Measured on the stock ship: the pressurizer's concentration went 6.7e-8 → 2.8e-6 kg/kg over 20 s of ordinary circulation, and a stuck PORV then takes hydrogen out at 8.6× the mixture's share | the drift is added on top of the flow rather than solved with it, so a bubble in a downcomer is never dragged under (drift-flux), and it rides the edge's own `C` = A/√K, which understates a real bore by √K. So filling a pressurizer by drift alone takes hours; in an accident the loop is two-phase and bulk transport does it in seconds, which is the real mechanism and the one that carries the game | YES — chosen 07/09/26. A torn pipe still passes its own MIXTURE, deliberately: a hole in a wall is not phase-selective and a homogeneous mixture sample carries its proportional share | high that it is insoluble and buoyant and that TMI-2 vented it through the PORV; **medium** on 0.25 m/s, where published bubble drift is 0.2–0.35 m/s |

| ...and the bang when it lights | a stoichiometric hydrogen-air cell burning at constant volume (adiabatic, isochoric) reaches about +2900 K and eight times ambient pressure | **product path rebuilt 23/09/26 (batch Q phase 7.1).** Each burnt kg of H2 now makes 8.936 kg of steam in the cell and takes its 7.936 kg of O2 out of it; oxygen is its own species in the mixture (the oxygen over or under dry air's share, at its own molar mass and NIST c_p), so a burnt cell loses the moles it should. Molar masses are IUPAC's, so 2 H2 + O2 and 2 H2O weigh the same (O2 was 32.000, 6e-5 off). The gas keeps its energy through the species swap and gains the heat of reaction at constant volume onto its own species energies: LHV less the lost moles' RT, products less reactants at 298.15 K. Both feed the same `q` the hydrogen deflagration does, so the fire and the blast cannot disagree. Measured on a BN-600 hot-leg burst: a spray flash in the first second, 96 kPa, air to 1065 K, then a pool fire holding the bay at ~615 K. **Re-measured 11/09/26 on the one-field room, to 20 s (the longest run that fits 10 s):** peak `s.roomPMax` 3.4 kPa, air 1525 K, 3 / 6 / 9 cells burning and 28 / 171 / 256 kg on the deck at 1 / 5 / 10 s; the holding figure was not reached in the window. The flash pressure fell because the per-cell gas is isothermal (row: the gas is isothermal). Not a preset failure; nobody has classified it. **20/09/26: a longer window is owed and its length is now stated.** No sodium-specific time-to-quasi-steady figure was found, but the general pool fire literature puts the rise to a quasi-steady burning rate at one minute at the fastest and commonly ten minutes or more, driven by pool heating and edge effects; sodium's oxide skin and diffusion-limited surface argue for the same or longer. The window owed is **300–900 s**, 15–45× what was run, and it has to chunk through `engSnap()`/`engRestore()` to fit the 10 s script budget | the fire is there; the aerosol is not — see the rows under it | — | high on the behaviour |

Now: every plane, covered or not, carries the steam made below its top S = max(0, Σ csNQl − W_in (h_f − h_in))/h_fg (`E_LVS`) and the pool's own Zuber-Findlay void at its middle on that steam (`E_LVA`); a wetted node whose march quality is ≥ 1 takes that pool void as its void target (the dry part stays 1); the crisis margin below W-3's floor is the pool law of row "departure from nucleate boiling"; and where S exceeds the inflow the plane has a counter-current flooding limit (`E_LVF` = S_max/S, the node's margin min(CHF/q, S_max/S)): Wallis √j_g* + √j_l* = C_w with j_l carrying S − W_in, C_w² = 1.22 (L_B/D_he)^0.12 (ρ_g/ρ_f)^−0.032 (1 + 0.055 Bo − 4.08e-3 Bo²) (Park et al. 1997 as Chun, Moon & Yang, KNS 2000, rewrote it; their eqs. 9, 16, 17 and Table 1 read at source), Bo held to their data's 4.25-10.0, D_he = 4 A_flow H/A_heat, L_B from the bottom of the first plane making steam (FIT: they start it at the onset of saturated boiling), solved closed-form in √S. One take-up cap, `eTakeCapA()` (transport.js): the water a node holds and the water arriving on the solved flows, together carried at most to the surface's own temperature, m·(h(T_s) − h)/dt + Σw_in·(h(T_s) − h_in), asked in the direction of the offered heat and none once they are past the surface (signed 24/09/26, batch F: the sum of absolute values let a node already past its surface read capacity, which put an exchanger's outlet past its far inlet; row "how many exchangers in series"). The metal wall reads it; the core and its wet channel columns take through it (`eSrcTake()`, surface the hottest can / column, none for a dissolved fuel) and what the water refuses goes to `csQRef`/`csQRefC` and back into the cans / columns on the next pass, by each can's last heat out. A stage passes what both streams' nodes take, on both sides (`eSgHeatStep()`), a radiator what its fluid takes toward its panel, a condenser's steam side what it gives toward its cooling water. Feed heat, heaters, pump work and the sodium-water heat have no surface and are not capped. The surface's own collapse to film boiling is the clad's (rows departure from nucleate boiling, heat transfer past departure). Measured first: the old gate destroyed 0 kJ on a severed hot leg (60 s) and with feed stopped (240 s). `dryout.js`: take (the cap by hand on IF97, holdup and arrivals, 1e-5, the engine's steam tables' own interpolation; fault: arrivals left out), stage (a 1 g shell: primary gives what the shell takes to 1e-12 of the wet duty; fault: the old gate), book (core with its vessel emptied: offered = taken + refused every tick, 3e-16; fault: refused heat dropped), core (pins' first law with the refused heat handed back, inside the Newton inversion's own 1e-6 K; cans 0.72 K/s mean, 1.97 K/s hottest against NEA/CSNI/R(2000)21's 0.4–1.0; fault: taken off the row and not put back). The plan's holdup-only cap bound at rest on gas nodes (Calder Hall's generator) and on a condenser's cooling-water outlet, so the arrivals are counted. Rest at 6 s: STOCK PWR, NUSCALE, RBMK-1000 bit-identical, five presets within 5e-10 in core heat, MSRE 1.4e-3 (the old wall cap bound 29 times on its flowing salt nodes; MSRE also swings on rounding, backlog 21/09/26). The 28 % is two publications differing, and the quantity that feeds melt and dispersal, h(T), matches the table within its own 1 % (0.7 %, 1.9 % at 3120 K). Above 4500 K the liquid law is outside Fink's range and nothing clamps it. At rest the core's gas stands at 210–406 C against the row's own 140 → 336 C programme. Unclassified. **The readings below are 21/09/26's**, when it flew: **re-measured 21/09/26, 120 s from commissioning, no orders given**: **it flew**. Drawn 19/09/26: 9.42 × 6.40 m, 1696 bars of 29.4 mm, 137.4 t U (the sheet says 120 t), net excess 3518 pcm. Heat 0.9975 of commissioned, heat balance 1.0002, no red annunciator, mass 2.2e-15, energy 1.2e-11. 200.1 MWt (+10 %), circuit pressure at the CO2 store **0.7909 MPa** (100 psig = 0.7908, 0.0 %; the core node, a circulator's head downstream, reads 0.8485), gas in **471.8 K** (198.6 C, +58.6 K), channel gas outlet **597.3 K** (324.2 C, -11.8 K), gas flow **1539 kg/s** (+73 %), boiler **1.435 MPa** (-0.9 %), **48.0 MWe** (+14 %), efficiency **24.0 %** (+3.9 % on the sheet's 42/182), circulator head **0.351 MPa** against the whole real circuit's 0.038. The real plant took its gas down to 140 C through its L.P. circuit, which is not drawn: **BUILD, a deliberate cut** (plan narrowed 21/09/26). The rating's +10 % is MODEL on the row "heat deposited outside the fuel". The circulator head: flow alone (1.73² = 3.0x) accounts for a third of the 9x; the rest is **unclassified**, not diagnosed. MWe and efficiency: the superheat is ruled out by sign (priced off IF97 20/09/26: the model's saturated drop at 1.448 MPa to its condenser at 0.85 is 686.6 kJ/kg, the real 313 °C / 210 psia drop 815.6, 18.8 % MORE), so the +3.9 % efficiency is the single H.P. conversion carrying all the heat the real plant split with its L.P. circuit, BUILD with the cut above. Two more causes, measured: (3) the capped re-walk (cause 2 below) ran once, then the shells were settled and moved the capped tubes' duty: they took **61.86 MW of the core's 61.54**. `engSettle()` now walks the rest and the shells again until the capped stages miss by 1e-4 or less (`eSettleCapMiss()`, `eSgWant()` the one target the fit, the walk and the read share). (4) T-avg counted the CO2 store's dead leg (1.3 kg of cold gas) from the tick its slosh flow passed its reference throughput, which was the reference solve's round-off, **7e-10 kg/s against the loop's 335**: **+0.21 K in one tick at 2.4 s**. `engBuild()` now zeroes a reference throughput under 1e-6 of its circuit's. Now **−0.005 K, 0.0016 pcm, heat 5.3e-6**; the boiler-tube fault moves T-avg 0.48 K and fails. The store's flow-work fault does not fail, on HEAD either (recorded, not chased). **Closed 21/09/26** (`calder.js`, `core.js 7`): 3 s with rods and boron held, **T-avg -0.0025 K, 0.0097 pcm, heat 1.6e-5** (was +0.93 K, 2.3 pcm, 4.9e-3 on the same tree before the repair). Two causes, measured, neither the one this row used to name: (1) `eMassSeed()` seeded every node's flow-work pressure `pAdv` at the node's pressure, while the tick's own law puts a part-filled node's contents at p·m/(ρV). On the CO2 store (level 50) that is 0.791 against 0.395 MPa, and tick 1 took V·Δp = 19.8 MJ out of 176 kg of gas: **-112.9 kJ/kg, the store 108 K colder in one tick**. One law now (`ePAdvA()`, `src/eng/transport.js`) for seed and tick; the store holds to 4e-11 kJ/kg. (2) The rest settle pins T-avg at the row's Tref, and Calder's boiler sat there on its NTU ceiling (`E_SG_NTU_MAX` 4): it took **197.09 MW of 200.59**, so the tick warmed the loop 1 K until the tubes took it all. Tubes that reach the ceiling after the rest settle now latch, and the rest is settled again with the loop walked to where they take the core's heat (`eSettleCapT()`, `src/eng/tick.js`); at t = 0 the core gives 200.585 MW and the tubes take 200.625. The Shomate-against-row-`c_p` calibration this row blamed is real (the row's rise is 0.20 % over Shomate's integral) and moves no rest figure: the settle lands the loop on its own laws either way | 0 | **MODEL, closed.** (3) reaches a stage on its ceiling only, CALDER HALL alone; (4) reaches the T-avg reading of any circuit carrying a dead leg, and nothing else that reads a reference throughput (the core inlet's weights sum it, round-off included). (1) reaches every preset: it removed a first-tick step on the `hpi` tank of 5.9 kJ/kg (6.1 on BN-600, 2.2 on MSRE), and moves the chaotic 120 s marches of BN-600 and MSRE (backlog 21/09/26). (2) reaches only CALDER HALL: no other preset's tubes latch, and their rest figures are identical to the committed tree's | high: conservation, measured tick by tick, each cause fault-injected in `calder.js` |

The run through it is named `steam` by a walk from the shell's steam nozzle through exchanger paths (`ihxSteamFace()`, layout.js), the path's face nodes are vapour nodes (`ihxStreamSteam()`), the stage law prices the vapour on its own T(h) (row "Steam generator effectiveness") and the turbine reads the steam it gets (row "a turbine's work"). Rig `tools/sandbox/net.js` `netSh`: the stock plant's hot leg through a 2000 kW/K superheater ahead of its generator. At 8 s the turbine inlet stands at 590.7 K against 555.3 K saturation and a 604.2 K hot inlet; the steam gains w·Δh = 73.06 MW against the stage's 73.07 (7e-5); the primary loses exactly what the steam gains in the same tick; 297 MWe against 295 without it. Calder Hall's gas on the law alone (CO2 at 336 C, 100 kg/s, against 20 kg/s of saturated steam at 1.448 MPa, UA 100 kW/K): steam out 112.5 K over saturation, under the gas inlet. `hx.js sh` | CALDER HALL draws no superheater: **BUILD, a deliberate cut** (decision 24/09/26); no preset draws one. | gap conductance | h_gap moves with burnup, gas and temperature, a few to tens of kW/m²K; the gap closes in service. Open gap: gas conductivity over the gap width plus the surfaces' roughness (Ross & Stoute 1962). Xe and Kr conduct ~25× worse than He, mixed by NUREG/CR-7024 eq. 4.1-4 | **rewritten 21/09/26.** `H_GAP` 5.7 kW/m²K is the gap at each node's REST point with its own rest gas, by construction (`clad.js fg`: 0 W/m²K off at every node); on a core with no gas out at rest that is all helium. Only gas let go SINCE rest poisons it. Away from rest the gap is `k_mix(T_gap)/(δ + δ_r)`: δ + δ_r = k_He(T_gap,rest)/H_GAP less the pellet's growth over the clad's (UO2 on FRAP's strain law, NUREG/CR-7024 eq. 2.5-1; Zircaloy diametral 6.721e-6/K, eq. 3.5-9; the metal rows on their `alpha` plus a step at each phase change), never under δ_r = 1.5·(2.0 + 0.5) µm. The gas is the rod's own He + released Xe/Kr (FRAP A·T^B, Table 4.1-2). Built as its own column (`PT.coreGGap`, `pinRes().gap`); the pellet + clad wall stay in `PT.coreGSolid`. Checked: poisoned gap = H_GAP·k_mix/k_He to 4e-16; the pellet rise follows the series resistance to 2e-15; a closed gap reads 47 kW/m²K | **1.** δ_r and the roughnesses (FRAPCON defaults 2.0/0.5 µm) are as commonly quoted, **not read at source**. **2.** No solid-contact term (Ross-Stoute h_s): a closed gap is gas-only, so under a real one. **3.** A closed gap does NOT load the clad: no PCMI hoop strain, the burst curve prices pressure only. **4.** The gap gas is at the node's clad-side temperature. **5.** Zircaloy's expansion is its alpha-phase slope at every temperature; FRAP goes to 9.7e-6/K above 1273 K. **6.** The rest gap is taken as H_GAP, not built from an as-drawn cold gap | **1** no, a source to read. **2, 3** YES, a gas gap is the mechanism asked for; PCMI needs a clad stress model this has not got. **4, 5, 6** YES, small | medium |
| a pellet past `tdmg` | UO2 lets its fission gas go by diffusion to the grain faces, climbing steeply above ~1500 K (Booth; Turnbull's D). The gas raises the rod's pressure, so the clad bursts earlier on NUREG-0630's curve, and it poisons the gap, so the pellet runs hotter and lets go of more. Natural U metal changes alpha to beta at 942 K with a volume jump | **built 21/09/26.** Per node, `ST.csNFg` is Booth's reduced time ∫D/a² dt, advanced only while the pellet is over its fuel row's `tdmg`; Booth's sphere gives the released fraction (never over 1). The inventory is the core's burnup (`coreBurnupOf()`, `D` knob `burnup` ?? `burnupSuggest()` = half the fuel row's discharge `bu`) × 0.30 stable Xe+Kr per fission (0.85 Xe) × the node's rest flux. A rod at rest has already let go of what its residence time in the core (burnup × heavy metal / rated power: STOCK PWR 769 d, BN-600 1069 d) gives at its rest temperature, so the rest point is steady. A ring's released gas raises its rods' pressure over the fill (ideal gas over the CLAD row's own `pFill` and `vFree`: Zircaloy 2.2 MPa and 6 %, steel 0.1 MPa FIT and 0.754), and sets the gap's mixture. Checked (`clad.js fg`): nothing 50 K under `tdmg`; Booth to 1e-9 at +300 and +400 K; at 1200 K clad on a 15.7 MPa core a ring with its gas out bursts (NUREG-0630 1030 K) and one without does not (1477 K); a fault that stands the release down turns both red | **1.** Not read at source: Turnbull's D = 7.6e-10·exp(−35000/T), the 5 µm grain, 0.30 atoms/fission, 0.85 Xe, the 6 % free volume, each row's `bu`, U's 1.06 %/0.70 % phase volume steps. **2.** Release is driven by the node's MEAN pellet temperature; the centre is hotter. **3.** No athermal release and no gas already out at rest: a rod starts with its fill alone. **4.** Metal rows release nothing (their gas story is swelling, not diffusion). **5.** The burnup sets the gas and nothing else — no reactivity, no decay heat (see `burnup`). **6. closed 23/09/26.** The SFR archetype draws BN-600's own pin: 6.9 mm, 0.4 mm `STEEL 316` wall (standing for ChS-68, no open property set), rod pitch scaled with the pin so the drawn fuel and sodium shares stay the lattice's. `vFree` 0.754 of the bore: the 653 mm plenum over the 1030 mm fissile column plus the 1.8 mm hole and the pellet's gap (IAEA-TECDOC-1569); the model's fuel fills the bore, so the hole is carried as free volume and NOT taken out of the fuel mass (11 % high). `pFill` 0.1 MPa He is a FIT (target: well under burst at rest). Burst on Hunter & Fish's two points (HEDL-SA-645, 20 % CW 316 at 10 °F/s: 8.96 MPa → 1589 K, 44.7 MPa → 1450 K), stored as onset = published less the damage law's own lag at 5.6 K/s (70 K), so a 5.6 K/s ramp fails at the published temperature (FIT on `E_BURST_TAU`/`SPAN`). Checked (`clad.js ss`): cp and k against ANL-75-55 to 2e-16, the rod's pressure the ideal gas over its free volume to 4e-16 on the engine's `R_GAS`, every can 409 K or more under burst at rest (hottest rod 10.4 MPa, 134 MPa on the old Zircaloy figures), both ramps fail at 1589 and 1450 K, Zircaloy's curve on the steel can fails the check. The can's absorption: 316's own thermal and 30 keV capture (Fe, Cr, Ni, Mo added to `NUC`/`FAST_G` from NIST scattering lengths and masses and Pritychenko & Mughabghab 2012 Tables IV/XI) over Zircaloy's, blended on the lattice's own fast share (`cladAbsOf()`): fast ratio 1.03, thermal 30.7 | **1** no, sources to read. **2, 3, 4, 5** YES. **6** closed; the 10.4 MPa rest rod pressure is the release law's, not the fill's | medium on the law, low on the figures in 1 |
| the feedwater heating | a steam plant bleeds part of its own steam off the turbine into six or seven feedwater heaters at falling extraction pressures. Each bleed has already done the work down to its own stage, none of it reaches the condenser, and the water arrives at the boiler near 490 K | ONE open heater at the shell's own pressure. `feedHeatKW()` puts `fed·(h(T_FEED) − h_in)` on the feed nozzle, priced off the flow-weighted enthalpy the transport lands there (`feedInHBy`); `feedBleedKgs()` takes `q/(h_g − h_in)` kg out of `s.turbWk` and out of what reaches the condenser (`riseCond()`). The bleed does **no shaft work at all** | measured on STOCK 09/09/26 the bleed is **33.0 % of the steam raised**, 196 of 594 kg/s, against a real PWR's 25 to 30 %. Charging one bleed the FULL turbine work made `COOLANT[].eff` absorb what a staged bleed would have earned: PWR .33 → .504, BWR .33 → .496, LWGR .31 → .466, SFR .40 → .633, MSR .44 → .697, HTGR .42 → .623, so **those are no longer isentropic efficiencies**. Before 09/09/26 the heating was free and funded 465 MW on STOCK, 39 % of core power, out of nothing | YES for the single stage — a staged cycle is a second network. The `eff` figures are **FIT** | high on the 25 to 30 %; the per-family split is not measured |
| the plant line (`#plant-line`) | the figure a plant states is what its generator makes at rated: a PWR turns 32–33 % of its heat into electricity overall (Lamarsh 3rd ed. sec. 4.5, p. 140) | **closed 23/09/26.** It read `P.rated × P.eff`, where `P.eff` is `COOLANT[].eff` × a turbine weighting: a FIT turbine factor (row above), not a cycle efficiency, so the line overstated every water plant by ~50–70 %. Now `plantLineText()` (`shell.js`) reads `P.mwe0`, `eMWe()` stored at the end of `commissionGen()`, the same door the control room reads; an edited, uncommissioned drawing shows MWt only. `plantline.js`: the line equals the generator at the restored rest on all nine presets, and a 10 % fault in the stored figure fails. `P.eff` had no other reader and is deleted | the line: none. The plant behind it: stock PWR **30.1 % gross (253.6 MWe of 841.7 MWt) against 32–33 % overall** | none on the line. The cycle gap is row **the feedwater heating**. Still reading `rated × eff` as an electrical figure, not changed (the ask was the line): the turbine inspector's RATED OUTPUT (`inspector.js:460`), `radTRated(d.eff)` (`inspector.js:503`, `layout.js`), `ratedEff()` (`layout.js:501`) | high |
| ...and what that bleed did to the signal the rods steer on | the load signal a rod controller weighs against core power is on the SAME basis as it, so holding a steady load manufactures no mismatch and the temperature programme is met at rated | **fixed 12/09/26.** `turbShare()` divided `s.turbWk` — which is NET of the bleed, `netOut.turbWk − bleedAll` — by `P.steamRef`, which is the GROSS rated raise, and `buildRodAuto()` adds `TPROG_SPAN·max(0, n − tfrac)` straight into the error its PID nulls. So the bleed WAS the mismatch, and the rods held it as a standing temperature error for as long as the plant ran. `turbShare()` now adds the bleed back — the gross-against-gross basis the commissioning governor's own flow match already used | measured on STOCK PWR through `plantPreset(0)`, dice off, 600 s: raised 599.8 kg/s, bleed 210.8, `turbWk` 389.0, so `n − tfrac` **0.332**; `TPROG_SPAN` 18 × 0.332 less the 0.8 K `AUTOROD_DB` predicts **−5.18 K** against **−5.20 K** measured, flat, with TAVG DEV (4 K) lit and unclearable on a plant holding level 50.0 % and never tripping. After: **−0.01 K** flat from 150 s, `n` 0.944 → 1.000, **386.1 → 406.1 MWe**. EPR −0.06 K. BWR/4 is unmoved, and identical to HEAD — a boiling core steers on flow (`buildFlowAuto`) and never reads `tfrac` | DRIFT, now closed — nobody chose a net-over-gross ratio. **What is NOT closed:** the feedforward still enters the error the PID nulls with no washout on it, so a GENUINE standing mismatch would still park a standing offset. It reads ~0 on the three presets measured | high on the arithmetic and on the measurement; **medium** on the real machine, where the load signal is taken off turbine first-stage pressure and calibrated at rated rather than derived from a flow |

## Plumbing and flow — the solved network

This is the best-founded part of the model. It is a real momentum-and-mass solve, not a flow
scalar, and the rows below say so. **Do not read the sizing section under it as a verdict on this
one** — they are different code and different jobs.

| what | real machine | this model | distance | on purpose? | sure |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| what drives a flow | a pressure difference and the fluid's own inertia | `w = C·√(2·ρ_upwind·Δp)`, absolute pressure, relinearised each solve | no gap worth stating | — | high |
| a tank's gas charge | an accumulator's nitrogen expands fast on discharge, closer to polytropic (n ≈ 1.2–1.4) than isothermal; it cools and loses pressure quicker than p·V = const says | `p·V^n = const` with `TANK_NPOLY` 1.4 on all three gas-law sites (`eTankLvlA`, `eTankCapA`, `eTankPA`), fast-blowdown adiabatic nitrogen. **Closed 20/09/26 (`tests/physics/accum.js`), re-read on a fixed 10 s sim point 20/09/26:** STOCK PWR HPI accumulator through a cold-leg break reads **−2.4 % off the hand n=1.4 law in pressure** at the same swept volume and **−2.8 % in delivered mass** at the same end pressure (isothermal was +48.2 % / +60.6 %). Against n=1.2 it reads −19.9 % / −24.5 %, which is the published band, not a gap. The stroke is not one pour: the charge stalls around 3 s with 3424 kg still in the tank and pours again as the loop falls further, so the distance depends on WHERE it is read — the 2.0 % / 2.3 % first written on this row was read wherever the test's 8 s wall-clock guard cut that march, which is machine speed, not physics; the read point is a sim time now and two runs agree to 1e-9. **RE-READ 21/09/26 after the circulating-water circuit and the seed walk landed: −5.0 % in pressure and −5.5 % in delivered mass** (−4.2 % / −4.5 % on the committed tree, so this job moved it 0.8 pp across the check's own 5 % tolerance), and **the cause is now named.** A gas-charged tank keeps TWO tracks of the same cushion: its level is read off the node's PRESSURE by the polytropic law (`eTankLvlA`), while its mass is what transport carried, and nothing forces the two to agree. Measured mid-blowdown at t = 2.00 s: the level says the gas swept to **20.356 m³**, the delivered mass at 1000 kg/m³ says **20.067 m³** — 1.4 % apart in volume, which at n = 1.4 is the whole pressure distance, growing as the stroke runs. The same two-track shape already named for the circulating-water tank. It moves with any change to how fast the tank blows down, which is why this job's 0.8 pp is not a defect in either job. **FIXED 22/09/26 (batch L): mass is the state.** The level is `100·m/tankKg` off the node's own mass, the pressure is the polytropic law off that level (`eTankPA` no longer returns the solved field for a charged tank), and every seed of a charged tank's node (`eMassSeed` and the held settle in `eAdvectStep`, one helper `eSeedKgA()`) is `level0·tankKg`, so the store is one Newton step off the true law like every other node. The held settle used to write the node full (`V·ρ`); read through the new law that was 1596 MPa on STOCK's HPI node, found and fixed on the way. Readings (`accum.js`): the law read off a poked mass is exact to 3e-16 at m = 1, 0.8, 0.5, 0.1 m0; the level to 1e-14 %; the seed reads p0; the end-of-stroke pressure against `p0·(V1/V2)^1.4` at the swept volume is **0.0 %** (5.5873 vs 5.5873 MPa, 12 415 kg delivered); the ledger holds to 1.3e-9 kg over the stroke; the `fault` chunk (the field read put back) fails at 2.96. The stroke is now read on the last tick the tank is intact: in this march a compartment blast wrecks the HPI tank at ~7.2 s, and a wrecked tank is an open one. At rest the HPI line is not an absent edge: its check valve passes ~8e-4 kg/s, so the fix moves the whole plant by ~1e-5 at commissioning and ~1.7e-4 in power after 600 ticks on STOCK (accepted by the user 22/09/26); every `presets.js` verdict on all nine presets is unchanged | **MODEL, closed 22/09/26** | high on the polytropic range; high on the measurement |
| ...and the inertia in it | the water in a pipe has mass, so a flow cannot be changed for nothing: `I·dw/dt = Δp − friction`, `I = L/A`. That is what makes a plant resist a slam and what gives water hammer its `ρ·c·v` | **in the tree and LIVE.** Every run edge carries `ed.I` = `max(L/2, NET_COMP_LEN)/areaOf(bore)` and every machine internal path that states a duct carries `partPathI()`. `edgeG()` returns `g/(1+g·In)`, `edgeH()` adds `In·w0`, and `edgeIn()` is gated on `netMarch`: `I·dw/dt` is a TIME derivative and is identically zero in a steady solve, so a settle, a reference solve and a governor walk carry none and only `step()` marches | **all nine presets fly at 5 s and eight of nine at 60 s with the term live**, against nine at HEAD without it: STOCK 373, NUSCALE 201, BWR/4 296, BN-600 380 (362 at HEAD), EPR 296, RBMK-1000 294, WINDSCALE 13, all resting at level 50. MSRE drains to level 8 and DUAL trips its turbine at 53 s. `probe.js ledger` closes to 0.81 kg of 337 500, `nodom-probe.js` read its full 1800 samples until 10/09/26, when the loop-head work took it to **735**: the LOAD FOLLOW scenario on the reference ship fills its generator shell to 100 % level by 50 s at HEAD too, and what changed is only what happens after the turbine trips on top of a full shell (the shell goes solid at 34–48 MPa and the vessel ruptures at 73.5 s, against a relief at HEAD). With the design state alone in it reads 1136; the overfill itself is the parked feed-regulator job, not this one, and every `splice.js` BARE baseline passes. One lump per run, no travelling wave, no pipe-wall compliance, so a surge reads about 20 % high | the two that are left are NAMED, not cut | high |
| how wide a pipe is | a designer sizes it for the flow it has to carry at a design velocity, and no two lines on a plant are the same size for the same reason | since 09/09/26 exactly that: `boreForW(w, rho, v)` on the run's OWN duty (`runDutyKgs()` = the smallest figure either of its two end nodes states) at one of three velocities - `COOLANT[].vLeg` on a primary leg, `V_LIQ` 3 m/s on any other liquid, `V_VAP` 50 m/s on vapour - doubled on a pump's suction face for NPSH. It was a six-row table keyed on the run's KIND | the figures move a long way and they move the plant: on STOCK circulating water 750 to 2940 mm, feedwater 1125 to 615, EFW discharge 1125 to 139, main steam 750 to 645, turbine exhaust 750 to **15 453** (425 m3 of holdup), HPI 187.5 to 107, surge 225 to 101. `ledgerKg` 294 725.9 to 337 375.4 on the wider circulating-water and exhaust bores. A run whose two ends state nothing still falls back to `BORE_REF` 750, and a relief line is the exception - it takes the valve's own bore | the LAW is real; `V_LIQ` and `V_VAP` are one figure each for the whole board, which is the cut | high on the law, medium on the two velocities |
| the circulating water pump | HEI *Standards for Steam Surface Condensers*: a condenser's tube water runs at 1.8-2.4 m/s at design and costs 34-69 kPa tube-side, tubes plus both waterboxes. A circulating-water pump for a short closed circuit develops 15-25 m total head, of which the condenser is the largest single item | **closed 21/09/26.** The cooling path is a duct now: `pathBoreMm()` cuts it from its own duty at the velocity its `ROLE` row states, and `pathK()` prices a path that states a `tube` bore from friction over every pass plus entry, exit and a waterbox turn per pass change. `ROLE.cond` states a 1 in OD 22 BWG two-pass bundle (24 mm bore), `ROLE.radiator` a 21 mm single-pass finned panel. `circHeadOf()` walks the circuit's machines the way `loopHeadOf()` walks the loop's. Measured on all nine presets (`tests/physics/pump.js`): tube velocity **2.076-2.088 m/s**, tube-side drop **58.3-58.7 kPa**, pump passes **1.037-1.042** of its rated flow, total developed head **15.03-15.20 m** | inside every published band. Was 1.19x the bought flow and 0.026 MPa bought against 0.048 delivered | **closed.** The 08/09/26-19/09/26 readings on this row were taken against a 750 mm duct at 41.6 m/s, and the earlier attempt that "trips RBMK-1000 and WINDSCALE and halves MSRE" failed on that area, not on the resistance. A THIRD fault was found in the same job and is what made the duct honest: an unauthored circuit's water was the PRIMARY's row at its own P0 and Tref, 705 kg/m³ at 583 K, so `boreForW()` cut the duct 19 % wide and the tube velocity could not be the one it was sized for. `circIsCw()` now names the circulating-water circuit structurally - no authored coolant, no core, a sink rejecting into it - and `circDesRho()`/`circMu()` read its own state (`cwRho()`, `CW_MU` 6.6e-4 Pa s at 312 K). Injection: halving the stated K drops the drop to 30.9 kPa and the check goes red | high |
| MSRE's heat balance hunts about unity | at steady state what the core makes leaves through the exchangers and the panels, and it stays there | **measured 21/09/26 over 600 s** after the radiator's cooling path got its real resistance: removal/heat reads 0.978 at 120 s, 1.022 at 200, 0.946 at 300, 1.033 at 400 and 1.008 at 600, with T-avg hunting 918.5-922.8 K. It closes ON AVERAGE and oscillates about +-3 %. **21/09/26, later:** its turbine trips on condenser backpressure (over 0.02 MPa) 0.10 s after commissioning with no orders given, found by the new no-red-annunciator check in `presets.js`; seeding a part-filled node's flow-work pressure on the tick's law (row "a part-filled node on its first tick", a correct fix) moved its lowest armed steam-line pressure 0.947 → 0.481 of design, a FAIL now tagged to this row. **22/09/26, after batch D** (blocks with their own temperature, samarium at 88 pcm, 100× clock): heat balance **0.558** at 120 s (was 1.283), core heat 0.883 (was 0.850), the steam line back to 0.755 of design (PASS), TURB TRIP red at 0.10 s, and the Carnot check reads a cycle efficiency of 0 (GAP); still hunting, one run, dice live. **22/09/26, after batch L**, sampled at 120/200/300/400/600 s with dice off, removal/heat: committed tree **1.0025, 1.0026, 0.9752, 1.0903, 1.0944**; batch L tree (loop-head elevation term, standard g) **0.9072, 0.9942, 1.0970, 1.1101, 0.9709**. It still closes on average and still hunts; the swing is wider than the ±3 % first written here, on BOTH trees, so the ±3 % is stale and a single 120 s reading grades nothing on this preset. **24/09/26, batch F**, one 120 s reading per step, dice live: 1.0003 before, 1.0265 on the exact stage law (a rounding-level change on its constant-c_p salt), 1.0000 with heat both ways, 1.0003 with the signed cap, **1.0781** once the turbine reads its inlet (wet at its 17 MPa shell, MWe −1.1 % at rest) | the 2 % check fails wherever the hunt happens to be. Against the committed tree this is a large improvement, not a regression: there removal was **0** at 120 s and the cycle's thermal efficiency read 0.958 against a Carnot ceiling of 0.494, which is gone | **UNCLASSIFIED** - the balance is satisfied on average and nobody has diagnosed the oscillation. | what a pipe costs | friction plus form loss | the same, `fricOf` (Colebrook) plus per-run `K` | no gap worth stating | — | high |
| the thermosiphon reading (`SC_NAT`, cabinet signal `nat`) | a single-phase loop with its pumps stopped carries w = sqrt(B/R), B the buoyancy round the loop, R its losses; with turbulent friction dw/w = 0.56 dB/B, smooth in its inputs (Todreas & Kazimi, *Nuclear Systems I*) | **fixed 21/09/26.** `eNetNat()` solves the core loop with every pump stopped and every store held, every `E_NAT_EVERY` 25 ticks, to `E_NAT_TOL` 1e-3 in at most 8 passes, warm-started off `ST.natPBy`. Until 21/09/26 its passes ran with the march's inertance (`PT.edI`/`NET_DT`, anchored on the pumped flow), so each pass was one 0.02 s coastdown step: 8 passes read **0.55 of rated where the loop's own fixed point is 0.036** (preset 0, 0.5 s after pump stop), every recompute exhausted its 8 passes (presets 0-3, 120 of 120), and the reading sat 3.3x / 2.9x / 1.6x off the fixed point on presets 0 / 1 / 2. Inertance is now stood down inside it: the fixed point in 2 passes on presets 0 and 2 (0.0359 and 0.2047 of rated). Preset 0, 45 s after pump stop (`natcirc.js real,pred`): prediction **509 kg/s** against the loop momentum balance's 497 (+2.5 %), the real flow 497. 1 ulp in one core-circuit enthalpy (`natcirc.js ulp`): worst prediction split **1.6e-10** over 20 s against the 0.56·s + 1e-3 bound. The 18/09/26 "1 % swing" was not reproduced by any rounding-level site run (1 ulp of h on presets 0-3, of m on 2, h under a hot-leg break on 0: worst split 3e-11). One run did split: BWR/4 after pump stop, where 1 ulp of h grows to a 4.4e-2 split of the core circuit's own pressures, and the reading's 1.8e-2 follows it inside the T2 bound; that is the plant amplifying, not the reading, and BWR/4 does not fly (BUILD on the drawing, per its gap.md row; this amplification unclassified) | the 25-tick cadence: a reading up to 0.5 s old. Only preset 0 is judged against a loop balance; presets 1, 4, 8 (also water single-phase) are not yet, and two-phase, sodium, salt and gas presets cannot be by this check. Not run: the rest of the plan's matrix (slice on 3-8, seed, bore, h on 4-8, m on 0, tight on 3-8). Fault injections 21/09/26: the `ulp` check FAILS (4.1e-2 against 1e-3) when the pass count rides the low bits of one enthalpy with no convergence test, and stays inside (3.4e-4) when the test is kept, since a converged loop moves under `E_NAT_TOL` per extra pass. The 45 s `pred` check is blind to the old defect (516 kg/s with the inertance put back, a pass), because by then the real flow has coasted to the thermosiphon; so `pred` also reads at 0.5 s after pump stop, against the loop balance with friction priced at its own flow: **299 kg/s against 331 (-9.6 %, inside 10 %) while the real flow is still 2332**, and with the inertance put back 1962, a FAIL. The -9.6 % is not diagnosed; the balance prices each leg at the pumped flow split | YES for the cadence; the rest as measured | high on the law |
| a pump's casing | a passage of a fixed area; the water's density belongs in the flow law, not in the passage | `pumpCasingC(h, q, rho)` = the resistance costing `PUMP_DROOP` of the stated head at the stated flow, taken ONCE at the circuit's design density when the edge is built | **it was read live off `F.rho`, the EOS density at (p,h), while `flowG()` prices the flow on `F.rhoD`, the donor's own holdup.** The two never cancelled: a suction that flashed priced the casing at vapour and then passed the law liquid, and the pump became a hole - measured 09/09/26, STOCK's feed pump at **23 202 kg/s against a 636 rating** with its suction at −0.15 MPa, and the node balance 23 168 kg/s out | closed 09/09/26 | high |
| a generator's feedwater nozzle | has a check valve, so a shell above its own feed header cannot blow down through it | `ed.diode = 1` on the shell's own feed path (`ROLE.sg.internal[1]`), the same door every standby train uses | **it had none.** Measured 09/09/26 the shell ran backwards through its feed nozzle at 2 867 kg/s at every valve position, and the water then arriving at the nozzle was the shell's own, so `feedInH()` read saturated and the bleed went to zero | closed 09/09/26 | high |

| a pump coasting after a trip | the hydraulic torque goes with the flow, bearings and windage do not, so the shaft stops — a real RCP in minutes. **Sourced 20/09/26.** Published RCP coastdown is reported as flow against time, never as a decomposed friction term: half-flow lands around 5–15 s depending on the flywheel (Qinshan Phase II: over 10 s on loss of power to both pumps, OSTI 20289829), a Westinghouse 13 200 lb flywheel is quoted as extending the total coastdown by 22–30 s (NRC ML11223A213, snippet only — the PDF would not extract), and flywheel design studies take **2 minutes of coastdown as the upper acceptance ceiling**. **No source anywhere gives a bearing-and-windage time constant on its own**, because nobody publishes the decomposition | `dN/dt = −N²/(2·τ_c) − N/PUMP_FRIC_S`, `PUMP_FRIC_S` = 60 s | the friction constant is a plausible order, not a measured machine: it is the only free number here, and it sets how long the tail is, not whether there is one. Measured 07/09/26, stock plant, blackout with the backup supply bypassed, coolant pump speed: 10 s 0.545 → 0.479, 60 s 0.167 → 0.088, 300 s 0.0385 → 0.0011, 600 s **0.0196 → 0.00001**. The old law was exactly `1/(1+t/2τ_c)` (0.03845 measured against 0.03846 predicted at 300 s), so the shaft still turned at 2 % of rated ten minutes in and the plant read that as forced circulation for ever. At 300 s of blackout `flowK` is now 0.0528 against `nat` 0.0528 — the whole of the flow is the thermosiphon, where it used to be 0.0646 against 0.0478 | the term is right; the constant is a stated **FIT**: still a guess, but 20/09/26 the PAIR was cross-checked against the published curves. The two terms split at `N = 2·τ_c/PUMP_FRIC_S` = 0.2: above 20 % speed the N² hydraulic term drives the deceleration, below it the N/60 friction term does, so **60 s sets the low-speed tail only** — it is neither a standstill time nor the 1/e constant of the whole curve. Solving the law as written with `τ_c` 6 s and no demand gives half-flow at **9.3 s** (published 5–15 s) and 1 % of rated at **172 s, 2.9 min** (against the 2 min acceptance ceiling). Both land inside the published ranges, so the constant is not contradicted — but that is the pair agreeing, not `PUMP_FRIC_S` being measured, and the tail is the only place it bites | **medium** that the pair (`τ_c`, `PUMP_FRIC_S`) reproduces real coastdown timescales; **low, unmoved**, that 60 s is the right friction constant — no source isolates one |

| what a feed pump has to suck against | Thoma's cavitation number is PER STAGE: sigma = NPSHr / H_stage (Stepanoff; Karassik, *Pump Handbook*). A boiler feed pump stage develops 200-400 m at 5000-6000 rpm, so a real machine's NPSHr at rated duty is 3-10 m. ANSI/HI 9.6.1 and ISO 9906 define NPSHr as the suction at which total head has fallen 3 %: above it head is flat, below it falls away | **closed 21/09/26.** `pumpStages(id)` is the machine's head over `PUMP_STAGE_H` (400 m of water, the top of Karassik's band), rounded up, floored at 1 - an answer, not a knob - and `pumpNPSH()` divides Thoma's number by it. `PT.pumpNPSHr` is a build column; `eCavStep()` ramps `ST.cavP` on the shortfall of NPSH available (p - p_sat(T) at the machine's own suction) against it, and the old subcooling trigger with its `E_CAV_SPAN` 12 K is deleted. Measured on all nine presets: head per stage **202-398 m**, feed pump NPSHr **9.58-9.99 m** on seven of them, BN-600 and MSRE 11.73-11.94 m. Head is flat above NPSHr to 1e-6, 60.2 % of full at half the requirement, 20.2 % dry. Every single-stage machine - RCP, circulating water, condensate - is unchanged by construction | inside the published 3-10 m on seven presets. BN-600 and MSRE are 17-19 % over it, and that is their own drawings: both carry a 22.98 MPa feed pump, which no real plant has, and both already state it on their own rows (BN-600 steam pressure; MSRE's whole steam cycle is this project's). Stated as GAPs against those rows, not as this law's distance | **closed, and the 09/09/26 half of this row was stale.** No preset has negative NPSH available any more: measured at rest on all nine (`tests/physics/settle.js`, extended from "over saturation" to "over saturation by NPSHr", which is the real criterion) every running pump clears its own requirement, the tightest being CALDER HALL's feed pump at 0.0969 MPa of margin. Injection: standing a machine's NPSHr down removes the derate at the same suction | high |
| a node smaller than one tick | a pipe does not care what time step you march it with | the transport is explicit and the store is a diagonal. The BN-600 collapse this row was written on is CLOSED, and the tick was never the term: a header tee was a SEPARATOR. `gasAt` was set on both ends of every vapour-law run, so a tee handed its whole flow over at `satHg` at its own pressure, charged itself back `−m(hg−h)`, and kept the liquid it had "separated" in a node with no drain. Measured over the settle 09/09/26: `mstee0` seeded dry (41.8 kg, x 1.000) and was pulled monotonically to 2004 kJ/kg and 161.6 kg over 150 passes, never converging — hg RISES as pressure falls in this range, so the node handed out more enthalpy per kg than it was fed. The march then reversed the header at 0.30 s, priced a node whose booked mass and enthalpy disagreed at 76 MPa, and burst six pipe cells | dt was never it. dt 0.005 and dt 0.001 both still collapsed the shell; so did an inflow cap on the node's own pressure step (0.03, 0.1, 0.3) and a minimum run length (1, 2, 4 m). All three failed because all three treated a **conservation** defect as a stability one. The related chatter is closed separately: a run resting on its saturation line read 982 then 0.8 then 982 kg/m³ on alternate ticks off (p,h) while its mass never moved, and `F.rhoD` (a run's donor density is its own holdup) fixed that | — the ERROR is closed, **and 20/09/26 the accident it used to produce was re-run and is gone too**. The 10/09/26 reading — a BN-600 turbine trip with dry shells giving a 115 MPa turbine node, an 849 K condenser and both radiators wrecked on OVERPRESSURE — does not reproduce (`tools/sandbox/net.js` key `bn600DryTrip`: feed pump and turbine stop valve both shut through `act()`, dice on and off, to 90 s). Tripped at 10 s the header climbs 17.96 to 20.10 MPa and plateaus at 20.1-20.3; tripped at 20 s it climbs 13.02 to 14.14, plateaus, then drifts to 17.3 by 90 s as decay heat keeps boiling the isolated header. It never runs away, the condenser stays 270-330 K, and both radiator wreck flags stay 0. That is the physical answer: a tripped turbine sees at worst what is upstream of it, and the relief holds it there. It was downstream of this row's separator defect and of the feed pump's `pumpRhoK` rating leak, both closed | high; the settle drift and the burst timeline are both measured; high on the 20/09/26 re-run |
| what a steam nozzle draws | a drum, a shell and a hotwell have a free surface, so the nozzle over it draws vapour and the water it leaves stays behind and drains. A tee in a header has no surface and no drain: it passes what arrives, mixed, and a drum that runs out of steam passes water | since 09/09/26, two statements. `separates()` (`netEdges`) sets `ed.gasAt` only where the end is a part with a level — a shell, a core, a tank, a thermal sink — asked of the PART, never of the run's kind, so `edgeLaw()` no longer decides who separates. And `advectStep` caps a gas donor at the vapour it holds plus the vapour arriving that tick (`x·m/dt + vIn`); over that it carries at its own mean and the `−m(hg−h)` charge-back is taken on the vapour share only | a shell still hands over exactly dry saturated vapour with no carry-over and no dryer, and its steam is 1.5 % wet by the first tee (`mstee0` settles at 2815 kJ/kg, x 0.985, against hg 2827 at its own 16.75 MPa) because throttling saturated steam above ~3 MPa really does wet it. There is no moisture separator or reheater anywhere on the board, so what the turbine gets is what the header holds. **And since 10/09/26 the liquid half exists.** `ed.liqAt` is the mirror of `ed.gasAt`, set on the vessel end of a non-vapour run off a TANK with a level under full - a drum's downcomer, a pressurizer's surge line - and never on the core node (the riser is a mixture and must reach the drum as one), a shell pool or the hotwell (already priced at its own liquid). The donor hands over `satH` when `x > 0`, charges the surplus back the same way the gas half does, is capped at the liquid it holds plus the liquid arriving this tick, and hands over no hydrogen. `F.rhoL`/`net.liqNodes` mirror `F.rhoG`/`net.gasNodes`; `flowG` and `staticH` take the marker the way they take `gasAt`. Measured on RBMK-1000 at rest: `s.flowNet` 0.79 -> **0.93**, core-average void 0.413 -> **0.146**, exit quality 0.244 -> **0.099** against a real 0.145, and the core inlet 3.7 K subcooled -> **33.0 K** against a real 14.5. The books close (`probe.js ledger --pre=5`, residual 0.000 kg), and STOCK PWR and EPR are inside 0.2 % of HEAD at 60 s, so the surge line handing over `satH` is invisible at rest. **The 33 K is the honest consequence and is not tuned:** the real 14.5 K is cold feed mixed into the drum's water, and a one-node drum whose steam leaves at `hg` and whose water leaves at `satH` has nowhere to keep that mixing - the node reads two-phase, and a two-phase node's liquid IS saturated by definition | — | high on the mechanism, high on the settle measurement; the drum's inlet subcooling is now wrong in the other direction |
| a direct cycle | an RBMK-1000 and a BWR have no steam generator: the channels boil, a drum separates the steam and sends it straight to the turbine, and the separated water goes down to the pumps mixed with the feed. The turbine governor holds the drum at ~7 MPa at every power | `drumIds()` (`pipenet.js`) is a tank in the field, level under full, no gas charge, with a line reaching a turbine, asked of the drawing. Since 19/09/26 a drum's feed may land on the drum or on the suction its downcomer reaches: `drumFence()` finds the regulating valve on a `feed` line landing on either, `runKindFor()` names a pump-discharge-into-pump-suction line `feed` (every preset's condensate line moved `user` → `feed` with it; duty, bore and law unchanged), and `pumpAhead()` stops at the fence so the feed pump is bought to the drum's pressure and not as a booster to the loop pump. `eDrumSteam()` takes the drum's own balance over h_f when no feed lands on it; the rest settle imposes each drum's feed at its steam. On a direct cycle the feed and condensate train is on the core's circuit: its reference enthalpy is no longer the loop's (it flashed in the condenser, row "RBMK-1000 reference solve"), a NaN node off the loop reads its own saturation and not T-avg, and the heat-balance reading counts a drum's steam out less its feed in (`SC_HBREMOVAL`, which counted no drum and read 0) | measured on RBMK-1000 and the drum probe (`drum.js`), rows below | — | high |

RBMK-1000's EFW suction (wall 550 K, water 320 K, standing between a shut tank valve and the pump's check valve) climbed 0.47 MPa a tick and burst at its 75.6 MPa at 1.86 s, armed with dice on, on HEAD as well; the drum probe burst `pipe:56,15` by 28 s.
