| the core inlet, off the field instead of off the mean | the water arrives at the channel at whatever the loop delivers, and on a direct cycle that is saturated drum water plus the pump's own head | **landed 10/09/26, in two halves.** `coreInH(s,id)` (`step.js`) is the one door: `advectStep()` refills `coreInHBy[nid]` off the same donor pass `feedInHBy` rides — the kg-weighted `inH/inM` of the edges arriving at the vessel node, blended onto the node's own water only below `CORE_DT_QMIN` of its commissioned through-flow, where a rise is no longer a rise. `coreStep()` takes `hIn` instead of `Tavg`, opens the channel at it and reads the transport lag's density there; `cs.coreDT` is still the RISE and the display, `sgInertiaK` and `K.sc0` still read it. `TavgOf()` keeps every other reader. **The second half is the term that half one exposed:** `cs.nTc` was algebraic in `heat` with no time constant at all, so with the channel no longer anchored on the slow `s.Tavg` the loop n -> heat -> nTc -> mod -> rho -> n closed inside one tick. The node coolant temperature now takes the SAME transport lag `cs.nV` already takes (`tau` = core height over the coolant's own velocity): a target `cs.nTct` written in the node loop, relaxed in the existing lag block, copied `nTct -> nTc` by the commissioning walk where it already copies `nVt -> nV` (`step.js`, `probe.js`). No new number | **the ERROR is closed and the ALGEBRAIC inlet was the wrong one.** STOCK PWR at rest: the field delivers **573.3 K**, the vessel node reads **602.6 K**, so the field's own rise is **29.3 K** against `cs.coreDT` 30.2 — the two agree on the rise. They disagree on the ANCHOR: the vessel's midpoint is 588.0 K and `s.Tavg` reads **583.0 K**, because `s.Tavg` is mass-weighted over the WHOLE circuit and a steam generator's tube side is one well-mixed node carrying its own OUTLET enthalpy, which drags the mean cold. Centring the channel on it started every core ~5 K cold. 60 s, dice off, seed 1, `n` HEAD → landed: STOCK PWR 0.9411 → 0.9412, EPR (55 s) 0.8840 → 0.8819, WINDSCALE 0.9470 → 0.9475, BN-600 1.0130 → 1.0243. **NUSCALE 0.9305 → 0.4739, BWR/4 0.9131 → 0.6068, MSRE 0.8178 → 0.6101, DUAL (10 s) 1.2176 → 0.5672.** RBMK-1000 breaches at 27.1 s against 25.6 s, and carries the `LWGR` re-pin as well. **The earlier attempt's NUSCALE 0.93 → 0.47 IS reproduced** — an earlier draft of this row said it was not | **YES, and what the first attempt at it cost is stated below.** The four that collapsed were a DEFECT, and the mechanism first written on this row was not what the parts showed. Re-measured 10/09/26 against HEAD by rule 9 (dice off, seed 1, `s.parts`): the falls are carried by `mod`, the coolant-temperature part `aM·(nTc − K.Tref)`, not by `vd`. NUSCALE at 5 s: `mod` **−897 pcm** against HEAD's −123, `vd` only **−249** (not the −929 this row first stated); at 1 s `mod` −1024 and `n` 0.443 against HEAD 1.034. MSRE at 1 s: `mod` **−2212 pcm** where HEAD reads +6, and MSRE has no void to speak of, so "now boils" cannot be the mechanism. `mod` GROWS over the first second (−439 at tick 1 → −1024 at 1 s) while the inlet barely moves (NUSCALE subcooling 46.6 → 49.0 K), so it is a transient in `nTc`, not a fixed datum offset alone. `probe.js presets --pre=1` reads RED HI FLUX at 0 s on NUSCALE where HEAD reads no alarm at 8 s. **The cause was measured, and it was not a stale datum.** Commissioning balances: at tick 1 the parts sum to the same -3565 pcm on HEAD and on the tree, boron taking the difference (HEAD -51, tree +254). What diverged was `nTc` itself, period-2 and growing on NUSCALE (592.44, 592.67, 592.62, 592.94, ... 600.32, 585.57, 607.25), carrying `mod` with it until HIGH FLUX tripped at n 3.23 at 0.34 s. Halving `dt` did not shrink it - the same tick count either way - so the gain was per TICK, which is a zero-lag algebraic loop and not physics. **60 s, dice off, seed 1, unarmed, `n` HEAD -> landed with the lag in (and the `LWGR` re-pin in `design.js`):** STOCK PWR 0.9411 -> 0.9409 (-0.02 %), EPR 0.8942 -> 0.8956 (+0.16 %), WINDSCALE 0.9470 -> 0.9467 (-0.03 %), NUSCALE 0.9305 -> 0.9288 (-0.18 %), BN-600 1.0130 -> 1.0247 (**+1.15 %**, outside the 1 % gate and unmoved by the lag - **named 20/09/26, it is the FIELD INLET term**, see below), MSRE 0.8178 -> 0.7430 (**-9.1 %**, carried by `rod` -374 pcm, the plant's own temperature programme answering a 23 K hotter channel; `mod` IMPROVED by +176), BWR/4 0.9131 -> 0.6578 (**-28 %**, carried by `xe` -692 pcm over the minute with `mod` at -43; its rest core-average void reads **0.381** against HEAD's 0.131 and a real BWR's ~0.40, so the fall is a plant nobody is steering under a xenon build, not the channel), DUAL (10 s) 0.6191 -> 1.5746 (up, not a fall). RBMK-1000 breaches at 28.7 s against HEAD's 27.0 s. `probe.js presets` reads no RED at 0 s that HEAD does not: NUSCALE's HI FLUX is gone and RBMK-1000's LO SUBCOOL with it. On the two drum plants the same read shows the drum handing **8 % quality down the downcomer** (`drum0` x 0.108, `run:cold:drum0b-pump0t` x 0.079): `separates()` gives a vapour run its `gasAt` and there is no liquid counterpart, so a separator's WATER outlet takes the mixture  **BN-600's +1.15 %, named 20/09/26 (`tools/inletsplit.js`).** A controlled ablation from one commissioned start, 60 s, dice off, marching BN-600 with each term held off in turn: nothing changed 1.000155 (+0.0155 %); LAG OFF 1.000155, identical to the sixth decimal, confirming the row's "unmoved by the lag"; **FIELD INLET OFF 1.057523 (+5.75 %, 35 pcm at beta = 640)**, and the trajectory visibly destabilises — it undershoots to 0.928 at 20 s and is still climbing at 60 s. Both off: 1.036260. The preset's OWN drift is 0.0155 %, **74x smaller than the 1.15 %**, so "it is the preset's own residual" is ruled out by measurement; and the term that owns it moves `n` 350x the noise floor when held off. **The caveat, stated:** this is a marginal-term ablation from a COMMON commissioned start, not a re-commission under the old code, so it isolates the term's own sensitivity rather than reproducing the historical HEAD-to-landed number exactly. The term identification stands regardless of that | high on the anchor finding, on the part readings and on the divergence being numerical; **high, as of 20/09/26, that the BN-600 1.15 % is the field inlet term and not the preset's drift** |
| the commissioned rest state | a plant at steady state: every node passes what it takes, and each feed valve passes exactly the steam its shell raises | since 18/09/26 one held field, `eSettleSteady()` (tick.js): direct factorisation, every edge but a pump's at half its secant with half the last solve's flow as a head, solved until no edge flow moves more than 1e-6 of the largest and no pressure of a node that passes flow more than 1e-6 of its own (since 19/09/26: against the largest, the 0.009 MPa exhaust was called settled with its drop still 30 % off). Under `eNetSteadyOn` a run's donor density is its EOS at the node's state, not its mass over volume, so the settled field does not move when `eMassSeed()` rewrites the mass after it. The references, the shell settle, the feed valve fit and the mass seed all stand on it. A feed valve is fitted by imposing its shell's steam through its edge and reading the opening off the drop the network puts across it through the tick's own law (`eFeedFit()`); no search. `tests/physics/settle.js`, eight presets (RBMK-1000 out): first-tick feed within 0.2 % of steam on every one (HEAD: 21-31 % over, STOCK 797 against 649 kg/s; RBMK-1000 7 against 281); settled-field continuity under 1e-9; STOCK settled twice lands its valve within 5e-6, and a 1e-4 turbine nudge moves it 2e-5 (HEAD flipped it 0.84 → 0 on a 1e-6 nudge); commissioning 0.35-1.8 s a preset (HEAD 0.37-2.5 s). Landed valves moved on every preset (STOCK 0.844 → 0.914). Since 19/09/26 only a circuit's own pressure vessel is pinned while held (`eHeldPin()`); a second hold vessel or drum on that circuit is a free node, and a free drum is reseeded at its own solved pressure. Read before tick 1, the tank is at its own zero-flow equilibrium to **1e-11 of its pressure**: its node is a BOUNDARY (`eBound` true, `PT.tankStores` 1, `tankInField` 0), so the settle never had it to freeze — the pressure is imposed by the tank's own level through the polytropic law, and the 0.011 MPa standing between it and the tie node is exactly the static head between them. `eNetSteady()`/`eFieldTankP()` is not what is happening. **What the transient IS: the circulating-water loop warming to its operating temperature, and the surge tank taking the expansion — which is what a surge tank is for.** The loop is a fixed volume, so the mass it gives up is beta.dT of itself, and measured `(dm/m)/dT` is **4.44e-4 (STOCK), 4.13e-4 (NUSCALE), 4.30e-4 (EPR) 1/K against IAPWS-IF97's 4.49e-4** for liquid water at 322 K and 0.6 MPa, all inside 8 % — that is the coefficient of thermal expansion of water, not a number this simulation chose. The SIGN also flipped under the circulating-water pump row's fix: the level now RISES 60.0 → 60.64 % over 170 s where it fell to 59.47 % before, because that job corrected the loop's commissioned density and with it its commissioned temperature. The 47.83 kg is superseded (STOCK now takes 64.40 kg in). One residual remains and is the same one named 20/09/26: the tank is a BOOKED node, so `ST.mBy` is overwritten each tick from its own level instead of integrated from the edge, and the two tracks of the same expansion disagree by **1.5e-6 of the tank's mass** (STOCK; NUSCALE 6.7e-7, EPR 9.5e-7) — a per-node bookkeeping residual that costs the plant nothing, inventory holding to 3.7e-15. The stale Boyle-law form in the level check was also corrected to the polytropic exponent the code actually uses (n = 1.4), which it now matches to 1e-6. **No repair was made and none is owed**: the authored charge stays authored and the level is its consequence. ## Pump sizing — the guess, the reference and the core's own drop

`loopHeadOf()` is the **design-time** guess at how big a pump to draw on the board. It never runs
in the tick. From 10/09/26 it and the reference solve (`eNetRef()`, `PT.nodeRefH`) read ONE design state, `loopDesignH()`: the runs
that still carry what the core sent out are priced as the mixture, and the reference solve is seeded
at the same enthalpies instead of being isothermal. `runHotSide()` answers structurally — a walk
from the core's outlet face to the first vessel that separates, so a drum counts like a generator and
a direct cycle is no longer priced as cold water. The core states its own drop at its inlet face
(`COOLANT[].dpCore`, read through `runK0()`), and `ROLE.core` no longer carries a flat `kEnd:2`.

Nine presets, `probe.js loophead`, dice off, seed 1, at 5 s. HEAD is the tree immediately before this
change (it already carried another session's uncommitted work).

| what the loop costs at rated flow | a Westinghouse RCP develops 0.6–0.7 MPa; a BN-600 primary pump 0.7–0.9; an RBMK-1000 MCP 1.5–2.0; the MSRE fuel pump 0.33 | **the guess and the solved loop agree.** Scaling each pump's solved rest dp to its own rated flow by (rated/q)², 10/09/26 at 5 s: STOCK 1.1233 against a guess of 1.1203 (0.3 %), NUSCALE 2.3 %, BN-600 3.5–3.9 %, EPR 3.4 %, WINDSCALE 5.3 %. MSRE is 25 % (0.2325 against 0.1861) and was 23 % before this work. Two presets cannot be read this way at all — BWR/4 and RBMK-1000 rest far off rated flow and their loop resistance moves with the void, so (rated/q)² is not their system curve | **the “a third light” figure is WITHDRAWN.** 1.4849 against 1.97 was never reproduced: measured on all nine presets the solved rest dp is the guess plus 5–10 % on every single-phase loop, which is the pump sitting a little up its own droop. **20/09/26 both open items are answered (`tools/loopsplit.js`).** MSRE's 25 %, leg by leg at q = 2623 kg/s against a rated 2600, guess vs solved in MPa: `cold:coreb-pump0b` 0.0735 / 0.1133; `hot:corer-tee0l` 0.0351 / 0.0786; `hot:sg0l-tee0r` 0.0523 / 0.1153; `part:sg0` 0.0249 / −0.0035; `cold:pump0t-sg0b` 0.0022 / −0.0652; total 0.1879 / 0.2386. **No single leg is wrong — every leg is off and most of it cancels.** The cause is a MISSING TERM, not a mispriced K: `loopHeadOf()`'s `dpOf()` is friction only, `K·w²/2ρA²`, with **no elevation term at all**, while the solve carries one per edge (`eStaticHA()`). MSRE's loop has real height — `sg0` at grid y = 7, `pump0` at y = 20 — and molten salt is dense (2085–2108 kg/m³), so each leg carries 0.04–0.07 MPa of hydrostatic head. Because the hot and cold legs differ in density the round-loop total does not cancel: it nets **+0.051 MPa, which is the whole residual**. **The two boiling presets now have their first readings**, taken by driving the pump until the solved flow equals the rated flow and reading the loop dp there, never by scaling: **RBMK-1000 converges cleanly** (demand 0.986, q 2010 against a rated 2006) and reads **2.352 MPa** against a real MCP's 1.5–2.0, consistent with the drawn risers adding their own ~1.2 MPa. BWR/4 could not be driven to rated flow at all: the flow pinned at 1162–1168 kg/s, 57 % of its rated 2025, up to five times the demand, and the first number was taken at that ceiling, 1.517 MPa. **20/09/26 the ceiling is GONE and the cause is named**: it was the riser sized at liquid density (row "...once it IS drawn as a direct cycle"), which choked the loop. Re-measured on the same sweep: at its own demand the plant carries **4456 kg/s, 1.10 of a rated 4050**, and more pump speed keeps buying flow — 1.5x demand 6593 kg/s, 2x 8080, 3x 9459, 5x **11 718 kg/s = 2.89 of rated**, pump dp 0.53 to 4.31 MPa. There is no ceiling. The RBMK reading of 2.352 MPa above was taken with the same undersized risers and is **stale**; the MCPs now buy 1.236 / 1.253 MPa | closed for eight of nine. **22/09/26 (batch L), MODEL, closed for MSRE — and the 20/09/26 reading above was half wrong.** `loopHeadOf()` now carries `Σ ρ·g·dz` along the flow path, discharge to suction plus the pump's own casing (`loopWalk()` over the loop's legs, `nodeZ()` heights, the density friction already gives each leg, `G_MPA`). Round a closed loop that is the thermosyphon head only, and it is SMALL: MSRE **−0.984 kPa** (hot 2408.7, cold 2435.5 kg/m³), STOCK −2.561 kPa. The "+0.051 MPa, the whole residual" was the tool comparing the guess, a pump head, against `p_dis − p_suc`, which carries the casing's own lift (MSRE's suction face 2.33 m above its discharge face: −0.0557 MPa). With the casing counted and only the friction scaled by (rated/q)², `tools/loopsplit.js msre` reads solved head 0.1998 MPa at rated against a guess of 0.2000: **0.13 %** (the old guess 0.2010 reads about +0.6 % the same way). Leg by leg the guess (friction + height) now sits within 0.001 MPa of the solved drop on all five legs: `cold:coreb-pump0b` 0.1240 / 0.1245, `hot:corer-tee0l` 0.0898 / 0.0906, `hot:sg0l-tee0r` 0.1320 / 0.1329, `part:sg0` −0.0145 / −0.0139, `cold:pump0t-sg0b` −0.0755 / −0.0754. Loop pump heads before/after (MPa): STOCK 1.2472/1.2446, NUSCALE 0.8792/0.8767, BWR/4 0.5284/0.5078 and 0.5080/0.4874 (−3.9 %), BN-600 0.6138/0.6130, 0.5817/0.5809, 0.6012/0.6004, EPR four pumps −0.0026 each, RBMK-1000 1.2357/1.2053 and 1.2521/1.2216 (−2.5 %), MSRE 0.2010/0.2000, CALDER HALL 0.7292/0.7291, DUAL 0.8792/0.8767 each; every one smaller, because a light hot leg gives head back. `tests/physics/loophead.js` judges it: the walk closes to 0 m, the term equals the hand sum at 1e-9, one density owes 0, and a flipped leg fails. `presets.js` and `pump.js` verdicts on all nine are unchanged except MSRE's 120 s heat balance, PASS 1.0025 → GAP 0.9715, which is one sample of an oscillator (row "MSRE's heat balance hunts about unity"). **`G_MPA` was 9.81e-6 and is standard gravity 9.80665e-6 since 22/09/26** (0.035 % heavy before; the user took the change). The same literal in `eChfZuberA()` is `E_G_MS2`, which was already standard. The preset verdict counts did not move with it; MSRE's 120 s sample went 0.9715 → 0.9072, still inside its own hunt. `tests/physics/hydrostatic.js` and `torricelli.js`, which write 9.80665 by hand, now read the same g the solve does. **BWR/4's flow ceiling is CLOSED and it was MODEL**: `runBoreSuggest()` handed the boiling riser the circuit's liquid density, so the recirculation loop was drawn about four times too small in area. Neither of the two guesses on the table (undersized drawing, overstated two-phase multiplier) was right | high on the MSRE leg table and on the BWR/4 sweep; **the RBMK 2.352 MPa figure above is stale** and the MCP row carries the current one |
| the core states its drop | a real machine spends real pressure between its own nozzles: a Westinghouse 4-loop vessel about 0.30 MPa at rated flow; a BWR/4 bundle with its inlet orifice about 0.15; an RBMK-1000's ZRK throttle, lower water line, channel and steam-water line most of a 1.5–2.0 MPa pump head; a BN-600's wire-wrapped assemblies most of 0.7–0.9; the MSRE core a small part of 0.33; a pebble or prismatic core on a blower of about 0.1 | **`COOLANT[].dpCore` from 10/09/26**, one figure per coolant, MPa at rated flow, placed entirely at the core's INLET face and turned into a `K` on the run that lands there (`coreEndK()` inside `runK0()`), so the design guess, the reference solve and the tick all read it through the one door. PWR **0.30**, BWR **0.15**, LWGR **1.00**, SFR **0.50**, MSR **0.04**, HTGR **0.06**; `coreDpOf()` = `coreD().dp0 ?? coreDpSuggest()`, one inspector row, nothing baked. It REPLACES `ROLE.core`'s old flat `kEnd:2`, which is why two pumps get smaller | it moves what the pumps are bought for towards the real machines on three presets and away on two. BN-600 0.2409 → **0.6200** MPa against a real 0.7–0.9; WINDSCALE 0.0748 → **0.1005** against ~0.1; RBMK-1000 0.7495 → **2.3463** against a real 1.5–2.0, now ABOVE it because the drawn risers carry 1.20 MPa of their own at the design state; STOCK PWR 1.0937 → **1.1203** against a real 0.6–0.7, still nearly twice it. Against those: MSRE 0.2234 → **0.1861** against a real 0.33, because 0.04 MPa is less than the `kEnd:2` it replaced, and BWR/4 as in the row above | the six figures are the decision; the two that move the wrong way are named, not tuned | PWR medium-high, BWR medium, LWGR and SFR low-medium, MSR and HTGR low |
| a boiling loop's cold leg | comes back saturated | priced on the saturation line since 06/09/26 | closed | — | high |
| two-phase friction multiplier | a real and large term | tried, **measured, and it made BWR/4 worse** | not the missing term | — | high |

## Mass, water and books

| what | real machine | this model | distance | on purpose? | sure |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| water is conserved | exactly | the books close to 0.0 kg at rest and through a safety lift; no residual over 0.5 kg on any book over 600 s. The `advect` floor books at most 0.03 kg over 60 s at rest, and 1e-11 kg through a 15 s pipe burst on any preset now the donor limiter is swept to a fixed point (`COURANT_PASSES`) instead of priced once off the raw solve — one pass booked 285 kg on a BWR/4 burst, 1.6e6 kg on the `netTrivial` rig | none | — | high |
| how much water the plant admits to holding | every kilogram in it | every kilogram in it: the run holds its own water, so none of it lands on a node whose book would hide it. Stock 316 242 kg | none | — | high |
| ...on the first tick | mass is conserved or it is not | **the ~2 kg does not exist on the current tree, chased 20/09/26** (`tools/probe.js ledger`, extended to book tick 1 at full precision on all nine presets, dice on and off). `SC_MASSRES` on tick 1: STOCK −5.82e-11 kg on 371 889 kg held, NUSCALE 0, BWR/4 −1.16e-10, BN-600 0, EPR 0, RBMK-1000 +5.82e-11, MSRE +1.75e-10, CALDER HALL −2.91e-11, DUAL +2.33e-10 on 1 800 117 kg. That is `inventory × 2^-52` — double-precision roundoff, nothing else. `eLedgerOut()` is **exactly 0.0** on tick 1 on every preset and not one `E_BK_*` code fired, so there is no clamp, no overdrawn donor, no double-counted `eMassSeed()` stub and no book credited before a limiter. An independent residual computed outside `eLedgerA()` reproduces `SC_MASSRES` bit for bit, so the number is not an artefact of its own arithmetic | 1e-16 of inventory, which is the floor | **ERROR CLOSED.** Mass closes on tick one to machine precision on every preset. What removed the 2 kg was NOT identified; the 19/09/26 `eEdgeGH()` fix on the row above is the likely candidate and was never cross-referenced back here, and that attribution is unconfirmed | high on the measurement; none on the attribution |
| a machine's duty on a tick nothing flowed | a machine's heat is a fact about the machine, not about that instant's flow | `advectStep()` takes `src[]` on a node with no inflow as `q·dt/mass`, since 09/09/26; a settle pass still takes none, because it is a steady-state sweep and not a march | **it took none at all.** The control volume was written as a relaxation toward `(inH + src)/inM`, so the whole term was skipped when `inM` was 0. A steam path at the condenser's vacuum holds a few kilograms and the Courant limiter meters it through in PULSES - every other tick - so the condenser was getting half its cooling and ran 15 K hot into a turbine trip at 3 s. **Amended 10/09/26:** those pulses were the TURBINE'S, and the exhaust hood is where they were declared spent — see the row below | closed 09/09/26 | high |
| a light vapour node in an explicit transport | an exhaust hood holds its own steam and its pressure follows the condenser smoothly | it does, since 10/09/26. `turbb` (11.025 m³) holds 0.257–0.259 kg over ticks 1000–1500 and never reaches zero; the duct behind it holds 10.22–10.29 kg instead of sloshing 4.31–16.60 kg on alternate ticks; both solve at 0.00428 MPa or above, off the `COND_P0` floor. STOCK MWe sd over 20–30 s 7.78 → 6.64, max step 0.253 → 0.098 MW a tick, books 0.0 kg residual on every book over 600 s | the old row named the cause as a one-tick-lagged field against an explicit mass integral. **That was wrong** — the solve's own store balance `C·(p − p0)` reproduces the duct's 12 kg swing tick for tick. Two terms were priced wrong and each owned one symptom. (1) `advectStep()`'s `kIn` capped a vessel at its EOS mass `V·ρ(pMax,h)`; at the condenser's vacuum 0.8 kJ/kg of pool enthalpy moves `condt` from 86 t to 16 t against 41 t held, so on alternate ticks the limiter refused every arrival (250 of 500 ticks) and the hood's 590 kg/s piled into the duct for one tick. A node with `F.void` now states its capacity liquid-full, the read `poolLvlOf()` already uses. (2) `staticH()` weighed a column at the MEAN of its two ends' mixture densities, so 1.4 m of steam between `condt` and the exhaust run priced at 0.0013–0.0068 MPa — more than the whole vacuum span — and drove the edge below the floor, where `netPStar()`'s clamp made the hood's store a permanent source and `kOut` clipped its integral to zero. A `gasAt` end now weighs at `F.rhoG`; the gas test is `gasEnd()`, the one predicate `flowG()` also calls | closed 10/09/26 | high; each term isolated, each removal removed exactly its own symptom, reproduced per tick, books closed |
| what the presets lost when the hotwell joined the field | — | measured 09/09/26, dice off, seed 1, against HEAD. 5 s / 60 s MWe: STOCK 386/372 (was 384/373), NUSCALE 207/191 (213/201), BWR/4 359/303 (295/296), EPR 423/287 (328/296), MSRE 318/321 (336/414), WINDSCALE 14/13 (15/13), DUAL 684/534 (452/425) | **BN-600 is closed** — see the separator row below. Re-measured 09/09/26 with the separator on vessels only, dice off, seed 1, 5 s / 60 s MWe against the same tree with a separator on every steam node: STOCK 386/357 (390/371), NUSCALE 216/193 (206/0), BWR/4 296/299 (380/315), **BN-600 429/371 (0/0)**, EPR 387/309 (313/310), RBMK-1000 337/0 (355/0), **MSRE 320/266 (115/2443, SG BURST 8 s)**, WINDSCALE 15/14 (12/15), DUAL 604/426 (609/432). A 60 s figure printed against a `died` note is the reading AT the trip, and `s.turbTrip` is condenser back-pressure, which resets. **RBMK-1000 still loses its generators** (shell 0.29 MPa against 4.07, level 0 by 38 s) and nobody has looked; the separator law did not move it. BWR/4 is the one preset that lost, 380 to 296 at 5 s. **CLASS: one of the nine is classified.** BN-600's collapse was a MODEL defect — `gasAt` on every vapour-run end, in a shared builder, moving five presets at once. Every other reading in this row is **UNCLASSIFIED**: nobody has said whether RBMK-1000's shell going to 0.29 MPa, BWR/4's 380 to 296, or any preset's shortfall against its own rating is the physics being wrong or the drawing being wrong, and the two are not the same finding. **Re-measured 10/09/26** after the commissioning rating gate (the row on what a pump is rated against, below), dice off, seed 1, with the probe now stopping on the first red `ANN` row: **BN-600 370/373, alive at 60 s** and MODEL, against 429/371 that ended SG DRY at 8 s. Three others were freed by the same gate and are read with the red stop off, before → after at 60 s: EPR 220 (SG HI PRES 9 s) → 305 alive, WINDSCALE 22 (SG DRY 48 s) → 14 alive, BWR/4 405 (TURB TRIP 15 s) → 307 alive, RBMK-1000 59 (TURB TRIP 22 s) → 287 alive. STOCK 384, MSRE 301 and DUAL 0 did not move; NUSCALE 208 → 207. **RBMK-1000 no longer loses its generators over 60 s** — shell 3.951 MPa against 4.07, level 50 — so the 0.29 MPa reading above is withdrawn; nothing beyond 60 s has been re-measured and the four freed presets are UNCLASSIFIED. **Re-measured 10/09/26** after the condenser pool reader (row below), same conditions, 60 s MWe before → after: STOCK 395.4 → 386.3, NUSCALE 211.5 → 207.9, BWR/4 313.7 → 307.6, BN-600 378.3 → 371.7, EPR (30 s) 315.4 → 310.5, RBMK-1000 311.7 → 306.0, MSRE 273.3 → 296.3, WINDSCALE 13.8 → 13.6, DUAL (10 s) 0.0 → 0.0. That move is a MODEL defect closed on all nine; DUAL's own 0 MWe is a BUILD defect on that preset and did not move | ERROR on RBMK-1000 withdrawn at 60 s, the rest unclassified | high on the readings, none on the classes |

Hole mid-`cold:coreb-pump0b`: core → run +902 kg/s, pump → run +1 544 kg/s, out of the hole 2 477 kg/s — both ends flow IN | — | high |
| a wall heating the water in a nearly-empty pipe | the wall wins; they equalise | the exchange is capped at `m·|h(T_wall) − h|/dt` — the wall may not drive the water past itself in one tick | an explicit-integration guard, not an implicit coupling. It binds only where the steel outweighs the fluid by orders, which on this plant is short stagnant lines | YES | high |
| **a pump running on what is in its casing** | `dp = rho·g·H`, so a pump on vapour makes about a thousandth of its rated pressure rise | `pumpRhoK()` — suction density over the density that pump was commissioned at, straight onto the head, no threshold | none in the law; the reference is the commissioned point rather than a stated design density, so a plant reads exactly 1.000 at its own design point by construction | YES | high |

Until 10/09/26 each settle was rated on the previous settle's answer. BN-600 runs four settles; its feed suction seeds at the shell's own saturation (h 1940 kJ/kg, 45 % steam at the 1.2 MPa suction), so a pump rated at 993 kg/m³ ran on 14 kg/m³ and kept 1/70 of its head. Settle runs 2 to 4 delivered 0 kg/s, the tick started with the shells dry, and the plant made 0 MWe by 36 s. With the gate BN-600 holds 373 MWe, 16.52 MPa and level 50 at 60 s, and STOCK is unmoved at 384 | closed 10/09/26; **class MODEL** — shared commissioning code, one figure, wrong scope | high; measured pass by pass in the settle and reproduced by injection |
| a steam generator's shell | a real vessel, water and steam | **ONE two-phase node**, the machine's own steam face (`shellNode()`), the same law a pressurizer already had: water and steam are one holdup, the level is that node's void fraction (`holdLvlOf` × `SG_DOME`), the pressure is solved, and the feed valve, the steam nozzle, the tube leak and the burst opening are all real edges on it. Re-measured 09/09/26: every preset commissions at its design pressure and holds level 50.0, and the ledger closes with no book over 0.5 kg | no gap worth stating | — | high |

No erosion between x 0.05 and 0.88 (Baumann 12 %): a chronically wet machine still runs forever (gap.md row) | **FIT** on the threshold pair: `E_TURB_WET_X` 0.05, `E_TURB_WET_S` 2 — "liquid wrecks it in seconds" is published, the exact second is not | high that a real one passes water and breaks; low on the exact pair |
| a condenser | a real heat exchanger with its own hydraulics | **a vessel in the field**, since 09/09/26. The exhaust space and the pool under it are ONE node holding the ship's stated condensate (`condVesNode()`, `condPoolVol()`); the condensate nozzle in its floor is the other. The pressure is solved, the water is `s.mBy`, the level is a read (`poolLvlOf()`), a wreck and the operator's drain are one break edge, and `s.tank.hotwell` is gone. `condRejOf()` is a source on the node and `s.condTBy` reads back off it | the backpressure now moves with duty, load and circulating water instead of following `condT` alone. | ...and where its vacuum comes to rest | a sink's pressure is the saturation of the water in it, and its temperature is set by the circulating water it rejects to | the pool's temperature is its own energy. `condPoolT()` (`step.js`) bisects the saturation curve for the T whose enthalpy — saturated liquid plus the vapour fraction the VOLUME forces at that T — is the node's `h`; `condTRead()` is that on a `condVacuum()` sink and `netTempAt()` on one spliced into a primary leg. `condSatP` stays `psat` of the pool's T and the `COND_P0` floor is kept. A node whose mass cannot be liquid at any saturation the volume allows falls back to `netTempAt()`, so a drained hotwell reads as before | measured 10/09/26, STOCK, dice off, seed 1, with that day's uncommitted `gasEnd`/`staticH`/advect-void-cap work in the tree. **The pair now agrees**: `\|Tsat(p) − condT\|` ≤ 0.16 K and `\|h − hOfT(condT)\|` 0.09 kJ/kg at 5, 10, 30 and 60 s, against the 28 K above. STOCK rests at **305.87 K / 0.00492 MPa** at 60 s and moves with load (307.59 / 307.92 / 306.36 / 305.87 K at 5 / 10 / 30 / 60 s) instead of pinning at 302.11 K. `cwIn` 293.98 K, so the approach is **11.89 K**, against the plan's derived `cwIn + 13`. `F.x` at the node 1.8e-4 (was 0.028), `F.rho` 163.6 kg/m³ (was 0.9). Books close (`none`). Tick cost is inside the noise: 1.640 / 1.815 / 1.809 ms before, 1.844 / 1.573 / 1.535 ms after. MWe at 60 s, before → after: STOCK 395.4 → 386.3, NUSCALE 211.5 → 207.9, BWR/4 313.7 → 307.6, BN-600 378.3 → 371.7, EPR (30 s) 315.4 → 310.5, RBMK-1000 311.7 → 306.0, MSRE 273.3 → 296.3, WINDSCALE 13.8 → 13.6, DUAL (10 s) 0.0 → 0.0. No preset trips inside the window that did not trip at HEAD. **CLASS: the MODEL defect is closed on all nine.** Eight rest off the floor; DUAL is the ninth and its sink never holds a pool (hotwell 0.147 % at 10 s, `turbTrip` and `condLost` at 0.00 s, SG burst at 0.04 s, identical before and after), so it takes the fallback by design — DUAL's failure is a **BUILD** defect on that preset and is untouched here. The fallback was seen to fire: emptying the node by hand makes `condTRead` equal `netTempAt` exactly, with no NaN. Whether a real sink SHOULD rest at 4 kPa is still a separate question nobody has answered here | **closed**, with one gap: nothing beyond 60 s was measured, because 600 sim-seconds is ~50 s of wall against the project's 10 s node budget | high on the readings; none on the 600 s rest |
| ...and a condenser's compliance at the vacuum | a hotwell's pressure is its own saturation, and it is very stiff: condensing a little steam hardly warms 40 t of water | `condStoreC()` states it - the steam space over the pool plus the pool and its steel following saturation - the same shape the SG shell used before its own pressure got high enough not to need one | **it cannot be a plain (p,h) store.** At 0.01 MPa the whole span from a full hotwell to an empty one is 3e-5 MPa of saturation shelf and 0.17 kJ/kg of enthalpy, so the void fraction is a knife edge, the level has to be read off the MASS, and the donor density has to be stated: `F.rhoD` at a pool node is liquid, because the drain is under the water, and `F.void` says a nozzle in the space above draws steam | YES - the vacuum is the reason, and it is written down | high |
| ...and a condenser spliced into a primary leg | a vessel in a line either passes what is in the line or fails as a pressure boundary | **it passes and the books close**: 6167 kg/s forward and 5942 reverse of a 7301 kg/s leg, no burst, **0.0 kg** out (09/09/26, `tools/splice.js cond`). Its node reads 16.54 MPa, 576.9 K, x 0, 3334 kg — an ordinary vessel on the leg. At HEAD it was 2271/6593 kg/s at 515/546 K with 159/169 kg out. Three things were wrong and all three are the same rule: `condVacuum()` now names the set for the pool seed (`poolSet()`), the holdup (`nodeVol()` hands `condPoolVol()` to sinks at the vacuum only, so splicing one in no longer halves the stock hotwell) and the plant's readings (`condSinks()` is what `condPRead()`, `condTMean()` and `cwInMean()` average) | the leg runs **4.5 K hotter** than stock, not colder — 587.2 against 582.7. The machine is holdup and resistance in the line, flow falls 16 %, and the core's rise over it grows; `condRejOf()` takes about 2 K back out (576.9 K in, 574.5 K out), because the circulating-water side of a sink standing in a hot leg is stagnant | closed 09/09/26. The old row's 515 K forward was not the machine cooling anything: it was 45 m³ of hotwell seeded as a vacuum void, collapsing the loop to 0.3 MPa on tick 1 | high |
| the circulating water outside it | a closed cooling circuit has an expansion vessel: water that warms has to go somewhere, and the vessel sets the circuit's pressure | **a real circuit, real pump and a gas-charged surge tank**, since 09/09/26: `cwtank` (`buildStockPlumbing()`, 10 m³, 60 % full, `gas:{p0:0.6}`) on the CONDENSER's circulating-water outlet nozzle. Before that it was a rigid ring `netReadP()` floated so its lowest node sat at `Pcont` | closed. Rigid, the nodes went over-full as the water warmed, `netPStar()` priced that at tens of MPa and the store row demanded thousands of kg/s the transport refused: at 30 s the solve read 17 346 kg/s into a run and 14 849 out of it. With the tank, STOCK reads 18 149 / 18 150 / 18 149 / −18 150 kg/s round the loop against a transport of 18 153 / 18 153 / 18 150 / −18 153, and the 3.6 kg/s difference is the tank's own branch. The tank hangs on the condenser and not on the pump's suction face because a tank a suction walk reaches is a RESERVE (`pumpResOf()`) and commissions that pump stopped | closed 09/09/26 | high |
| ...and what it cost | — | the pump now delivers its curve instead of fighting a spurious store: 18 150 kg/s against 14 940, +21 % | **that is the ERROR row above biting harder.** MWe at 60 s, before → after: STOCK 372.1 → 372.6, NUSCALE 191.4 → 174.7, BWR/4 302.9 → 299.0, BN-600 3.0 → 0.0, EPR 286.5 → 313.6, RBMK-1000 0.0 → 0.0, MSRE 289.0 → 15.4, WINDSCALE 12.8 → 0.0, DUAL 0.0 → 432.2. No preset breaches in 60 s either side. The charge is 0.6 MPa and not 0.3 because at 0.3 NUSCALE and DUAL rupture their vessels inside 60 s, and at 0.15 the loop cavitates (the pump passes 10 kg/s) | ERROR, inherited — the circulating-water circuit still has no resistance a real one is made of | high |
| the radiator panels against the duty | a heat sink is bought for the rejection at its design temperature | **re-sized 20/09/26.** `SINK_MARGIN` is 1.0: T^4 is convex, so `sum(T_i^4) > N·T_mean^4` and a series string sheds MORE than the same area at the mean — the old margin's stated reason had its sign backwards (`radiator.js series`: the pair-to-mean ratio against its analytic value to 1e-12). Measured on STOCK, dice off: the fleet rests at 313.7 K at 20 s, ~7 K over `RAD_TDES`, within a ±2.1 K series spread only as a stated gap; condenser 330.6 K; MWe 375.7 → 354.1 at 120 s (−5.7 %). BN-600 and MSRE commission past the turbine trip on the smaller sink (`condP0` 0.026 / 0.035 MPa against `TURB_TRIP_P` 0.02) and trip at tick 0: BN-600 survives derated (~0.89), MSRE collapses to ~0.56 with the turbine mostly offline | the sized duty undershoots actual rejection ~12 % (the turbine delivers 0.285 of heat against 0.360 sized; MSRE worse on its 0.697 FIT `eff`) | YES on the cut — the analytic check decided it; **MODEL** on the duty shortfall | high on the readings; medium on the sizing |
| a turbine's work | the isentropic enthalpy drop of its inlet steam to the exhaust pressure times a stage efficiency, 0.85–0.90 across a large machine's wet LP stages; the generator loses ~1.5 % | **since 24/09/26 (batch F) from the inlet state.** `eNetReadEdges()` sums flow × the donor's h on the work edges (`SC_TURBH`); `eTurbDhA()` takes the entropy of that (p, h) off the dome when wet and off IF97 region 2 above h_g (`if97R2()` now returns s, 2e-9 against IF97 table 15), expands it to the condenser, × 0.85 (`E_TURB_ETA`), × 0.985 at the generator (`eMWe()`). The design-time caller passes h_g at its design pressure. Throttling moves the inlet: above ~3 MPa h_g falls with pressure, so saturated steam throttled from a 6.9 MPa shell reaches the stop valve slightly WET (STOCK 2773.9 against h_g 2776.3 kJ/kg at 6.70 MPa); below it (CALDER HALL, 1.35 MPa) slightly superheated. Rest MWe, old → new: STOCK 295.45 → 295.04, NUSCALE 162.82 → 162.62, EPR 228.32 → 227.97, DUAL 319.52 → 319.10, RBMK-1000 150.21 → 150.05, BN-600 512.93 → 506.41, MSRE 198.31 → 196.16 (both 17 MPa shells), BWR/4 175.98 → 175.88, CALDER HALL 27.13 → 27.14. `hx.js sh`: a superheated inlet against IF97 by hand 1.1e-5 (fault, the saturated reading: off 6.5 %); a saturated inlet equal to the saturated formula to 1e-9. Since 18/09/26 before that: saturated vapour at the stop valve's pressure, whatever arrived | no reheat, no moisture separator, no extraction work: one expansion from the inlet state; an exhaust that would be superheated is priced at h_g, which over-reads the drop (no preset exhausts dry) | YES on the single expansion; 0.85 is the low end of the published band | medium |
| a turbine exhaust's enthalpy | a hood passes what the last stage left in the steam, superheated or wet | it does. `turbb` reads h 3076.9 kJ/kg at 30 s and 3078.9 at 60 s on STOCK, x 1.0, and what lands on the condenser reads the same 3079 | **the ERROR is closed, and `c8ddde0` closed it.** The runaway was `advectStep()` crediting a `gasAt` donor `−m·(hg − h)`, which at superheat is a source feeding itself; `c8ddde0` narrowed the `gasAt` set on a vapour run from BOTH ends (`ea.gasAt = u; eb.gasAt = v`) to an end that `separates()`, and a hood is not a vessel with a free surface, so no edge on `turbb` carries `gasAt` any more (verified on the built net, 10/09/26). The credit itself is unchanged and still applies to a real separator | closed 10/09/26 | high on the reading and on the mechanism |
| the feed train at commissioning | a standby EFW line is at the header's pressure and does not ring | the whole header rings for about 0.3 s on the first ticks: `run:feed:efwpl-efwteer` peaks at **16.580 MPa**, 1.42× its own 11.66 MPa rating, and `efwtee` swings 6.83 MPa. The stub commissions at 558 K (the shell's saturation, reached by the seed walk over the header) and is flushed to 318 K by tick 8 | it no longer bursts and it no longer depends on the frame rate: ten seeds with the dice ON give 16.580 MPa every time, no `PIPE BURST`, no damage, 60 s. The peak is still over the rating and only the 17.49 MPa burst point keeps the run whole. Seeding the stub cold is not available: it sits on the HEADER side of the EFW pump's check valve, so no walk reaches it from the tank. **RE-MEASURED 20/09/26 on the clean tree (`tools/ringprobe.js`, preset 0, per tick to 0.3 s and again to 2 s): THE RING IS GONE.** The header sits flat at 10.3990 MPa out of the settle and peaks 10.3995 MPa at tick 8, **0.89× its rating**, not 1.42×. All three candidates ruled on with numbers: the INERTIA term is present (`edI` 31.200 on both bordering edges) but never excited — the pump is in standby and `ST.edW` stays inside ±0.016 kg/s all window; the SOLVE is settled — the pre-tick field already reads 10.3992 MPa and the tick-to-tick swing is 0.0002–0.0003 MPa; and the SEED is falsified outright — the run node measures **309.03 K, subcooled, x = 0** at t = 0, where the row says 558 K. The shell itself still reads 558.00 K, so that half of the old measurement was real; the seed walk simply no longer carries it onto this node. **THE MECHANISM IS CLOSED 21/09/26.** `eAdvectSeed()` now takes the flow law's own diode test — one predicate off `PT.adjEdge`/`PT.edU`, no new state, nothing allocated — so the walk crosses a check valve only the way the water could. Water does not arrive where it cannot flow to. What the test actually changes, measured on all nine presets (the same walk with it against without it): the main feed pump's suction face `feedt` on eight of nine, **387-405 K blind against the condensate header's own 316-339 K walled**, plus `run:feed:cpumpb-feedr` on three of them. RBMK-1000 moves nowhere. Nothing else moves, so the walk was reaching the feed pump's SUCTION backwards through its own check valve and seeding it at feedwater-outlet temperature | **CLOSED.** The 09/09/26 ring is gone and its cause is still not known — that half of this row stands as written. What is closed is the diode-blind walk itself: it can no longer put an unreachable state behind a check valve on any drawing. `tests/physics/seed.js` is the rig (a 400 K boundary, a checked pressurizer pointing back at it, a stub nothing anchors): walled the stub commissions at 406.42 K off its own circuit, and with the diode stood down the walk carries the unreachable 400 K across, so the check is seen to fail | high on the new measurement; **none** on the cause of the 09/09/26 ring's disappearance |
| **an intermediate exchanger's second stream (`ROLE.ihx`)** | a real second circuit with its own pump, inventory and pressure | **two real internal paths**, the declaration `ROLE.sg` already makes: both streams are network nodes on their own circuits, `ihxQAt()` is counterflow NTU with both inlets read off the field, and the pot is gone | no gap worth stating on the stream itself | — | high |
| an exchanger's own tube rupture | a leak between two sodium circuits | `ROLE.ihx.sgtr` is `false` — the tubes cannot let go | no exchanger leak event at all | YES, deliberately out of scope 06/09/26 | high |
| **how many exchangers in series** | as many as the plant has | **any number, structurally.** `ihxFeeds()` is a circuit walk, `sgActive()` asks whether the core reaches the tube face, and nothing relates a stage to a stage by name | **FLOWN 20/09/26, and the walk holds; closed 24/09/26 (batch F).** `tools/sandbox/net.js` key `netIhx3`: a stock PWR core tapped by `ihx1`, relayed `ihx1 → ihx2 → ihx3` at UA 800 / 600 / 400 kW/K. It lays clean and all three stages pass heat at once. `hx.js chain` builds it through the profile's own builder and marches 25 s in snapshot slices, dice off: every stage's law against a counterflow integral typed in the test, 5.4e-7 worst over 72 samples; per tick and per circuit (L1, L2, tertiary), held energy moves by the boundary enthalpy flows and the stage heats the tick applied, closed to 6e-9 kJ against the engine's own worst \|`SC_ENRES`\| 8.6e-7. **The overshoot's pass is named:** the take-up cap `eTakeCapA()` summed absolute values, so a node already past its surface still read capacity toward it. Signed now, asked in the direction of the offered heat, for every source it caps. Every tick a stage passes heat, both outlets stay inside the two inlets, worst 6.8e-4 K; with the old cap injected (`chain,fault`) stage 3's outlet runs 1.53 K past its far inlet at 21.7 s. The 20/09/26 reading compared a face node with a face node: the giving side's inlet face holds that stream after half the stage's heat has left it, so it is not the inlet; the test reads each stream's own inlet (`SX.stgN`) and its downstream face. Worth recording about the rig: `eStageFed()` only counts a stage's hot side as fed if it reaches the core's own loop or another stage's cold face; a pumped relay ring with no expansion tank cavitates itself to zero head | none left on the walk, the law, the closure or the second law | **high** |
| a generator's tube-side heat capacity rate | its own flow and its own fluid | `eStageLoadA()` loads the tube stream at its inlet node's own h and pressure and its own solved flow (`SX.stgW`), on its own circuit's curve (`eNodeSat()`), and the law prices it on that curve's own T(h) (row "Steam generator effectiveness"); the secant `SX.stgC` is read only by `eSettleUA()`'s NTU ceiling, where a two-phase stream is unbounded | it was `fl·P.netRef/n·P.sat.cp`, the PRIMARY's reference flow and the PRIMARY's `cp`, on a generator that may stand on another circuit. Swapped 07/09/26. **No shipped preset exercises it**: every `PLANTPRE` row puts its generators on the core's own circuit, so the two figures agree — stock plant 20 s, turbine flow moved 622.747 → 622.745 kg/s and `P.sgUA` 45894.8 → 45896.3. It bites only on a bench plant with an exchanger spliced in, which is the plant BN-600 is supposed to be | no gap worth stating | high |
| **BN-600 has an intermediate sodium loop** | three circuits. The intermediate loop is why the plant is safe — a sodium-water reaction in the steam generator cannot reach radioactive primary sodium | **two circuits. Primary sodium goes straight into the generator tubes**, and `ROLE.sg.sgtr` is true, so a tube rupture is primary sodium against water. The MACHINE can now be built — the exchanger's second stream is real and a hand-drawn three-circuit plant forms three circuits with the barrier intact — but the preset still ships two | the plant does the one thing an SFR is built never to do | ERROR | high |

## Compartment, damage and crew

| what | real machine | this model | distance | on purpose? | sure |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| air heat capacity | the mass actually in the cell, at its own species mixture, on c_p(T): dry air 1.005 kJ/kg/K at 300 K, 1.141 at 1000 K, 1.249 at 2000 K, and hydrogen's c_v is 14× air's per kilogram | **since 20/09/26 exactly that.** `ROOM_CGAME` is deleted. `eRoomGasA()` reads m·c_v over `ST.roomM`, `ST.roomVap` and `ST.roomH2`, each species' c_p(T) off its own NIST WebBook Shomate row through `shoCpA()` — the one polynomial the coolants already use. NIST has no row for air, so c_p,air is mass-weighted over N2/O2/Ar/CO2 at 0.7553/0.2315/0.0129/0.0005, which is how the published air tables are built. c_v = c_p − R/M per species, mixed by mass. A uniform 2034-point table from T_SPACE to `ROOM_TMAX` interpolates it, and u is the trapezoid integral of that same interpolant so du/dT is exactly the c_p the lookup answers. Outside 298.15–6000 K c_p is held at the end value: past 6000 K the hydrogen row's own polynomial turns c_v negative. Measured (`tests/physics/roomheat.js cp cv`): every species within 0.013 % of `shoCpA` across the whole grid; dry air 1.00505/1.14099/1.25038 against the published 1.005/1.141/1.249; γ 1.39989 at 300 K and 1.33623 at 1000 K; a half-air half-hydrogen cell holds 7.59× the all-air capacity to 1e-16; a cell vented to half its mass takes twice the rise for the same kilojoule (1.99764 against 2) | **none on the law.** What is still bought: the 298.15 K floor and the 6000 K ceiling on the fits, and Shomate extrapolated between 6000 K and `ROOM_TMAX` where real air would dissociate | NO — the ×50 cut is spent | high |
| structure that stores heat | a compartment holds air and it holds steel, joined by a surface conductance: the air responds on seconds, the steel on minutes to hours | **since 20/09/26 a second node, `ST.roomTS`, per cell.** 8 mm mild plate (7850 kg/m³, c_p 0.49 kJ/kg/K, `ROOM_PLATE_C` 30.77 kJ/K/m²). **Since 23/09/26 over the cell's real surfaces** (`roomGeomBuild()`, `PT.rFloor/rDeck/rSide/rHull`, `PT.rStrA`): front and back 2·MPC² = 0.4356 m² in every cell (the board is one 4 m slice; those walls are interior structure, never hull), plus `ROOM_A_FACE` = MPC·`ROOM_DEPTH` = 1.867 m² for each floor, deckhead or side whose neighbour is off the board or gas-tight paint. A machine cell beside is not a wall (its skin is `ROOM_HK`'s); a wall later wrecked keeps its plate. Until then the "floor and deckhead" were 2·MPC², the front and back walls' area: a real floor is 8.6× that. An open interior cell carries 13.40 kJ/K as before; a cell on a liner deck 2.30 m², 70.8 kJ/K; a cell walled on four sides 7.90 m², 243 kJ/K. Hull faces are the faces toward off the board: an edge cell 1, a corner 2 (it counted the border ring's own cells too, 3 and 4). `ROOM_H` 6 W/m²/K over the same plate. Measured 23/09/26: `roomheat.js area` 0 of 2232 cells off the drawing; `hull` an edge cell radiates 2.3032 kW against ε·σ·A·(T⁴ − T_space⁴) 2.3032 at 400 K, a corner 4.6064 against 4.6065 (the old count reads 6.91 and 9.21); `struct` (one cell walled all round) 16.05 s against 16.10; `mix` 0.05670 1/s against 0.05674 once each cell's own plate is projected on the mode. Stock PWR after 60 s at rest, mean over open cells: air 295.497 → 295.457 K, plate 292.967 → 292.973 K. A machine's own skin is NOT counted — `ROOM_HK` already exchanges with it. **And the hull plate is what radiates**: the Stefan-Boltzmann term moved off the air onto `roomTS`, taken backward about the plate's own temperature, because the air reaches space by convecting to the plate and never past it. Measured 20/09/26 on the old areas (`roomheat.js struct`): 284.53 s against the analytic 284.66 s. **What the old ballast turned out to be:** `ROOM_CGAME` 50 implied 51.2 kJ/K per cell, i.e. 104.5 kg of steel over 0.4356 m² — **30.6 mm of plate**, three to five times a real machinery-space flat. The ballast was not a real structure. The real 8 mm plate is 13.40 kJ/K per interior cell, so the compartment's long-run capacity is 14.2 kJ/K against the old 52.3 | stiffeners, girders and the condensation on them are not counted, so 13.40 kJ/K is a FLOOR on the real structure, not an estimate of it | NO | medium on the plate thickness, high on the law |
| the pressure of a cell holding steam or hydrogen | an ideal-gas mixture: p = n·R·T/V over the moles of every species; a kilogram of hydrogen is 14 times the moles of a kilogram of air | since 18/09/26 the moles of air, steam and hydrogen (`eRoomMolFill()`); before, every kilogram was priced at air's gas constant, so a hydrogen-laden cell read about a fourteenth of its partial pressure | exact at the ideal-gas level (`tests/physics/roomgas.js`, 0.001 %) | — | high |
| hydrogen autoignition | **two different quantities, and the literature keeps them apart.** BULK autoignition in air: 773–858 K (NFPA 497 500 °C, IEC 60079-20-1 560 °C, ISO/TR 15916 585 °C). HOT-SURFACE ignition, where a small hot body lights the gas passing it, is higher, because the gas beside it is only there briefly: measured 1000–1170 K (Mevel, Melguizo-Gavilanes, Boeck & Shepherd, *Int. J. Heat Fluid Flow* 2019, on a 9.3 × 5.1 mm SS316 glow plug — 1010 K at 5 % H2 to 1170 K at 74 %; Morreale et al., WHEC 2010, 1033 K on a steel coil; Tamm et al. 1987 via NUREG/CR-6530, 1050 K turbulent and 1123 K quiescent in 50 mol % steam) | **since 20/09/26, two constants.** `H2_IGN` 773 K is read at the GAS temperature (`eIgnites()`, bulk); `H2_IGN_SURF` 1050 K is read at a part's SKIN. Before, one constant did both and a hot pipe lit hydrogen 277 K earlier than any experiment says it would | `H2_IGN` stays at the low, conservative end of its own 85 K band, **decided 20/09/26:** a room cell is a large volume, where quenching is least and the bottom of the band is the physically appropriate end as well as the conservative one. `H2_IGN_SURF` is a single number standing for something that is not one: the threshold moves 128 K on cylinder orientation alone and up to 50 K on surface chemistry, and the same Caltech group's 2011 thesis reports 808–910 K for lean H2 where its own 2019 work reports 1010–1017 K. 1050 K is where three independent sources overlap | DRIFT closed 20/09/26; NO on the split — one constant was standing in for two mechanisms | high on the bulk band, medium on the hot-surface figure, and the literature itself says no single constant exists |
| what a jet, a vent or a boiling pool may heat the air TO | a stream cannot drive a gas above its own temperature. A flashing break delivers a two-phase mixture pinned at the saturation temperature of the space it flashes into; a relief passes steam at the shell's own temperature; a boiling pool gives up its latent heat at T_sat and no higher | **since 20/09/26, capped at exactly that** (`eJetQCapA()`), the same rule `eFireStep()` already took on a burning pool and `eAdvectSrc()` takes on a wall. Three callers: the break jet (`eJetLiqQ()`, donor = T_sat at the cell's own pressure), the relief and shell-vent plumes (donor = the hottest shell's steam), and the boiling term in `eWaterHeat()` (donor = T_sat). **This is not a tuning knob; without it job 1 is wrong.** The deposit is sensible heat with no donor temperature behind it, and on the ×50 ballast that was invisible — on a REAL air capacity a plume cell ran to thousands of kelvin no source in the plant can reach. Measured on the stock hot-leg break: the plume cell reads **363–457 K over the first 0.26 s** where before the cap it read 679–1195 K and the compartment ran away to `ROOM_TMAX` and 30 MPa within 5 s. Over 60 s the guard was touched **3 cell-ticks on a hot leg and 0 on a steam line** (which peaked at 2046 K), against **1 514 800 and 3 962 592** on the tree before the cap. **21/09/26: 0 cell-ticks on both** (`roomgas.js breakhl breaksl`, after the gas and the liquid were solved against each other), and 0 on the committed tree's hot leg the same day, so the 3 were never chased to a cell; with this cap stood down the hot leg touches the guard 341 cell-ticks and the check fails. **Withdrawn as stated: "no gas hotter than its hottest donor"** - a squeezed gas runs above its donors by law (air taken isentropically from 0.1 to 4 MPa goes from 293 to about 840 K), and the worst cells over 60 s, 1699 K on the hot leg and 1223 K on the steam line, are pockets the water pumps; measured, not decomposed. **Re-measured 21/09/26 after the water got its own volume:** 0 cell-ticks at the guard on both breaks again; the steam line's worst room cell reads 6.62 MPa against a 6.66 MPa drive (PASS), the hot leg's 35.5 MPa against 11.76 (FAIL, a squeezed pocket: row **...water pushed into a full body**). The hot-leg figure read 21.9, 25.3, 716, 54.6 and 35.5 MPa at successive stages of the same day's work, so it is a record of one run, not a distance. **22/09/26, CLASS: MODEL, fixed:** a flooded cell's evicted gas (`eGasDisplace()`) booked the floor volume `ROOM_VG_MIN*ROOM_VCELL` (0.0087 m3) as squeeze work on its receivers on every eviction, whatever the mass, so a trace of steam boiled into flooded cells paid ~p·0.0087 m3 a tick into the pocket above; it now books the gas's own n·R·T/p at the donor's pressure, capped by the space held. `roomgas.js evict`: 10 mg of air evicted books 8.301184e-6 m3 against m·R·T/p 8.301184e-6 (identity, 1e-9), the box's gas gains 2.92294e-3 kJ against Δm·h 2.92295e-3 (first law, 2 %); the old line reads 8.71e-3 m3 and 0.876 kJ and fails both. Guard cell-ticks on the hot leg: 95 → 0 on the interim tree (coarse grid on the water solve, `E_FUEL_NEWT` 4), 10 → 0 on the committed tree | the energy the cap withholds is discarded from the compartment's books rather than left in the stream — the mass it rode in on is already accounted separately, so nothing is double counted, but the plant's own ledger does not see the difference | NO | high on the law, medium on where each donor temperature is read |
| the compartment's gas field at rest | a compartment with hot machinery in it has buoyant circulation: the air over a warm pump really does rise and be replaced | **since 20/09/26 it does.** With the air at its real capacity the machine skins raise their cells by kelvins rather than by 1/50 of that, so the cell-to-cell pressure differences clear `WAVE_P_LO` and the implicit gas solve runs. Measured on the stock plant at rest: **the solve is live on 173 of 500 ticks at 84 CG iterations**, where before the field sat still | **this is where the tick cost went.** 2.50 ms a tick against 1.05 ms on the committed tree, 2.4×; with the gas solve forced off the same tree runs at 1.16 ms, BELOW the old figure, so the new physics itself costs nothing and the circulation costs everything. Allocation moved the other way: 13.3 kB a tick quiet against 72.4 kB before (5.4× better), 101.5 kB on a burst against 42.8 | YES, weighed and taken 20/09/26: the circulation is real physics and the plant pays for it. The alternative was raising `WAVE_P_LO` to stand the solve down at rest, which buys 1.3 ms a tick by pretending a compartment with hot machinery in it has still air. The knob is left where it is | high on the measurements |
| air heating a machine's contents | happens | only through one skin conductance | a fire wrecks boxes long before it moves a loop | YES | high |
| crew in hot air | die | work at full rate in air at 2000 K | total | YES, a stated scope cut | high |
| a radiator panel | degrades, articulates, sees the sun | alive or destroyed, nothing between | no partial damage | YES | high |
| a tripped turbine | trips on high backpressure and is **re-latched** once the exhaust is clear. A real machine also trips on a **load rejection**: losing the grid shuts the control valves fast, at full power, in milliseconds | the same, since 06/09/26: re-latches under `TURB_TRIP_P × 0.75` with vacuum never lost, the exhaust whole and the machine repaired. There is still no manual trip — the only setter is backpressure. So a blackout reaches the turbine only through the condenser warming up: measured 08/09/26, BWR/4, blackout at 10 s, `condP` passes `TURB_TRIP_P` at 35 s with the diesel lost and never with it up | none. The real chain is grid → turbine → scram, and this cabinet reaches the same end from the first term: `SIGNAL.dark` is a block-readable BLACKOUT reading and `SINK.scram` is the sink, so a plant that scrams on losing the bus is a graph the player draws at design time. Hard-wiring it would take that choice away | YES | high |

It is coloured the mock's way (`paint()`), not in bands: one ramp `C.wave` to `C.waveHi` on `(s/ref)²`, alpha `min(0.92, √(s/ref))`, culled under 2 % and, since 11/09/26, wherever neither slope component reaches `WAVE_P_LO` 0.05 kPa - the gas step's own rest gate, below which it stops solving and leaves the step standing, so a 0.017 kPa step left by water pushing air aside drew as a patch that never faded, against `waveRef`, the loudest slope since the plant was built, which never falls and is floored at `PNOW_LO` 0.5 kPa per cell so float noise in a still room cannot become the reference. **The BLAST tool's charge is the mock's Gaussian**, `roomBlastCharge()`, `BLAST_SIG` 2.4 cells, peaking at the figure in the box, into open air only: a one-cell charge was a grid-scale source and, measured 11/09/26, 17-27 % of lit cells rang as a checkerboard against 0.0 % for the Gaussian; at 5 MPa in one cell 56-84 cells also sat on the vacuum floor. The box defaults to 0.1 MPa, the mock's charge, where it was 5 MPa. A charge throws the mock's 160 sparks and 24 puffs (`blastFx()`, off `s.blastEv`), and every spark and puff falls at 9.81 m/s² with a 0.6 s drag and is 0.3 cell across. BLAST is on by default, so scars and hits draw with the wave as they do in the mock. BLAST no longer fills cells: the `s.roomPPk` cell fill, its zone colours, band strokes and pulse are gone from the layer and live on only in the tooltip's BLAST PEAK row. It draws SCARS, one per passage: `roomScarStep()` books `peak − HIT_LO` (5 kPa) into `s.roomScar` each time a cell's bang has fallen to half its own peak, never decayed, and each machine face is bitten to `half a cell + 0.7 × depth × min(1, scar/45 kPa)`, smoothed 1-2-1, in half-cell blocks, four shades of `C.scar`. Measured 11/09/26, `probe.js blast`, three charges 1 s apart beside `pump0`, on the Gaussian charge: 100 kPa, scar sum 7510 / 15222 / 22894 kPa, ratio 1 : 2.027 : 3.048; 500 kPa, 182025 / 369846 / 557880, ratio 1 : 2.032 : 3.065. On the one-cell charge it was 1 : 1.995 : 2.990 at 500 kPa, and at 2000 kPa the ratio broke (1 : 1.008 : 1.312) because the plant bursts and the room is no longer linear. It also draws HITS, a 0.3-cell bar on every shut face of a cell carrying more than 5 kPa, held 0.12 s. The expanding rings `burnRings` are GONE: they were a Rankine-Hugoniot circle with a fitted slowdown, never the field. Sparks and smoke add the solved air (`s.roomPU`/`s.roomPV`) to their own velocity. History: 10/09/26: `BLASTFX` states ONE colour, `C.red`, for both blast layers, and the opacity is a continuous ramp, `BLASTFX.a0` 0 to `a1` 0.55. **`a0` is ZERO and that is not cosmetic**: the layer has a threshold, so any opacity AT the threshold is a hard edge, and a decaying field walks every cell in the room across that edge one at a time - which draws as speckle, not as a wave. Measured at `a0` 0.10, a 400 kPa source on the stock board: 22 to 37 % of the lit boundary was a lone cell against a dark one, and the fraction ROSE as the field decayed, because more of the room was sitting on the threshold. At `a0` 0 the worst alpha step between neighbours falls from 0.10-everywhere to 0.051 by 0.40 s and the picture fades out instead of switching off. The speckle was NOT the checkerboard: sign alternation between neighbours ran 6-23 % and FELL with time while the speckle rose, and raising `WAVE_D4` from 0.35 to 1 did not move it. **Both ramps are LOGARITHMIC and both ends are a real pressure.** PRESSURE NOW ramps the live field from `PNOW_LO` 0.5 kPa, the tooltip's own floor, to `BLASTFX.now` 70 kPa, the rung that takes heavy rotating plant; BLAST ramps `s.roomPPk` from `BLASTFX.lo` 15 kPa to `BLASTFX.scar` 200 kPa, the rung that takes a pressure vessel, on top of its soot fill. A linear ramp was tried first and **thrown out on measurement**: it was pinned on the p99 of a 5 MPa blast, 35 kPa, and the field this game actually draws spans two decades - measured over 2 s in an empty room, the median is 0.64 kPa on a 100 kPa charge and 1.94 on a 5 MPa one, with maxima 1.40 and 70.17 - so on anything but the biggest charge every cell drew within 5 % of the alpha floor and the picture had no contrast at all. Alpha at 0.64 / 1.76 / 5.61 / 21 / 70 kPa is now 0.119 / 0.197 / 0.286 / 0.388 / 0.480, against 0.101 / 0.114 / 0.156 / 0.24 / 0.48 linear. **PRESSURE NOW draws MAGNITUDE, `|P|`, not the signed gauge.** Gauge pressure is signed and half of an acoustic wave is a rarefaction, so drawing the positive half alone left the ring hollow and made every cell blink as the sign alternated: measured one tick after a 400 kPa source, 356 cells drew against 600 that carry a field, and about 40 % of the live wave was invisible at any moment. The cost is that a compartment vented to space, which sits at -101.3 kPa gauge and whose `roomPGauge()` floors at 0, now draws at full opacity and stays there. **The magnitude then has to be SMOOTHED, and this was found by looking at the running page rather than by measuring**: `|p - mean|` has a ZERO at every wavefront, a wave is nothing but wavefronts, so the raw magnitude puts a black hole in every cell whose pressure is passing through its compartment's mean - which is about half the room at any instant. On screen that is a uniform red field with random black holes punched through it, and it is what the user called spotted cheese. `BLASTFX.smooth` was 0 with a comment saying it added no information; it is 1 and it is not decoration, it is what fills the amplitude's own zeros. One 5-point pass (`blastSmooth()`) over the drawn scalar, not over `s.roomP`, so nothing in the physics sees it. Confirmed by eye on `plantPreset(0)`, a 1500 kPa blast at cell 30,28: holes gone, one smooth cloud. **The layer is drawn against the region MEAN and then smoothed, so it is no longer a per-cell reading of anything** - the tooltip is the reading and the layer is a picture. The five thresholds survive as `BLASTFX.zones`, which is now a LABEL ladder only and is read by `roomCellTip()`: 20 kPa a cabinet, 70 heavy rotating plant, 120 a pipe, 200 a pressure vessel, and those are the machines' own limits. The band-edge strokes both layers drew are gone, and both tooltips in `src/render/layers.js` were rewritten to describe what is drawn. `BLASTFX.full` 600 kPa stays and is now the EVENT's own scale only, read by `evFx()` for the particle count; `BLASTFX.smooth` stays 0 | **the colour no longer says which machine the pressure breaks.** Before this, a cell drawn amber meant "enough to take heavy rotating plant" at a glance, over the whole board at once. Now only the tooltip says it, one cell at a time, and reading which of five bands a cell is in costs a pointer move. That was asked for and it is a real loss | YES - decision 6 of the plan, taken by the user on 10/09/26 on the grounds that a wave walking up and down a colour staircase does not look like a wave | high - the ramp figures are p99s of a measured field and are stated here with the run they came off |

What the uncapped law gave, measured 11/09/26 on the stock plant, charge 3 cells left of `pump0`, largest elastic target over 1 s before the ×2 kick: 100 kPa, 1.47 cells on the 1x1 `rv0`; 500 kPa, 7.36 cells on `rv0` and 0.94 on the 2x2 `cwtank`; 2000 kPa, 99.5 cells on `tee0`. | **water on the floor** | it lands where it comes out, falls accelerating, runs toward level at the speed a shallow sheet runs (sqrt(g*h), ~2 m/s half a metre deep), stands where it stands, and a machine it rises round is drowned | **since 12/09/26 one law for both liquids: a pressure at every cell's floor and a speed on every face** (`liqStep()`, room.js; `liqSettle()` is its name at the two call sites). Each tick, per liquid, one implicit face-momentum solve of `roomGasStep()`'s shape (`roomCgSolve()`, the same SGS-preconditioned CG, `LIQ_CG_TOL` 1e-9), then the mass moves on the faces it solved and its energy rides upwind with it (`roomAdvect()`). The unknown is the pressure at the cell's own floor: a cell with room left is a free surface (`p = pGas + ρgh`, compliance A/g); a full cell carried by the floor with liquid or a ceiling over it is stiff (compliance cap/K, `WATER_BULK` 2.2e9 Pa, sodium `COOLANT.bulk` 5.8e9) and its pressure is STATE (`s.roomWP`, `s.roomPoolP`) - and since 12/09/26 "liquid over it" is a DEPTH, `h[i-GW] > LIQ_H_LO` (1 mm), never a bare `M > 0`; a full cell with gas over it is a free surface at its own ceiling, and goes stiff for a second pass the tick it is fed from the side; a full cell in the air is a lump that presses on nothing beside it. Gravity is in p. Horizontal drive = the hydrostatic force over the wetted face, over the mean depth of the water between the two centres, plus the gas difference: the shallow-water u-equation's g·dh/dx (**21/09/26**; it was over the deeper side's depth, `ρg(h_i²-h_j²)/(2·max h)`, which halved the drive at a front); vertical drive = the floor pressure over the receiver's ceiling pressure, or over its gas when it has room. Friction: Manning `LIQ_MANNING` 0.012 on the face's own depth, and the orifice loss `LIQ_CD` 0.6 where a standing body leaves through a hole shot in a wall. Upwind momentum advection on every face, so a stream falls at sqrt(2gz) and not at g·t; **since 21/09/26 the momentum is carried INTO a face from its upwind neighbours, v_up·|v_up|/L**, where it was advected at the face's own speed, so a face at rest - every new face at a front - took none of the water pushing into it. `faceLimit()` cuts a cell's outflow to what it holds and its inflow to the room it has, iterated to rest and then shaved exact by at most 16 tail passes, so no cell is ever filled past its cap by a face and no clamp makes mass up afterwards. Since 21/09/26 every cell is capped, by volume, and since 23/09/26 a stiff cell over its cap carries the excess as a source in its own row of the solve, which raises its pressure until its faces pass it (row below; from 21/09/26 the excess was walked to the nearest cell with room before the solve, and from 12/09/26 burped up its own column); two cells both in the air have no face between them unless they move sideways faster than they fall (`lz`), so a stream has no side to leak out of; a stream landing on a body turns into the side faces of the cell it lands in at its own speed (`lat`; since 21/09/26 as momentum, where it was a force on the face, which drove a face of grams to the `LIQ_V_MAX` guard); a source lands at the speed it left its opening at (`liqLand()`'s `v0` - the pressure difference for a break, the stated rate over one cell face for the INJECT tool, the spray's own speed for metal) rather than at rest; and a body or a band under one device pixel is not drawn (`liqDraw()`, render/room.js). A source lands through `eLiqLand()` at the nearest cell with room that the liquid reaches (since 21/09/26; it walked up its own column and built a full column in the air under a 10 t/s break), and its volume is a source in the gas solve rather than a push into the ring. The per-face gas swap is gone since 21/09/26 except where a liquid leaves a cell that held no gas: the gas it falls into, and since 23/09/26 the gas above or beside it, takes the room it leaves (`eLqSwap()`); elsewhere the liquid's volume reaches the gas as a squeeze the gas step relieves (`the implicit step's front`). `s.roomPool`/`s.roomPoolE` went to double. The ideal inviscid dry-bed front is **2·√(g·h₀)**, not √(g·h₀) (Ritter 1892, *Z. Ver. Deutscher Ing.* 36(33) 947-954; confirmed in Castro-Orgaz & Chanson, *Environ. Fluid Mech.* 2017, and the USGS FEQUTL manual — the same solution gives h = 4/9·h₀ and u = 2/3·√(g·h₀) at the dam line, which also checks out). A real frictional front decelerates from 2·√(g·h₀) toward √(g·h₀) with time (Whitham, *Proc. R. Soc. A* 227, 1955; Dressler, *J. Res. NBS* 49, 1952), and Dressler's own 1954 laboratory dam breaks measured **1.54–1.74·√(g·h₀)** a few seconds after release. At the probe's h₀ = 0.467 m that is an ideal 4.28 m/s and a real 3.30–3.72 m/s. The measured front ran 10 cells in 3.20 s = 1.46 m/s, **0.34 of the ideal solution and 0.39–0.44 of the real one**. **21/09/26, which term cost it, measured by standing each down alone on the probe's rig: neither Manning nor the upwind advection (1.33 → 1.35 and 1.21 m/s without them).** Two laws were wrong and are fixed: the drive over the deeper side's depth (half of g·dh/dx at a front) and momentum advected at the face's own speed (a face at rest took nothing from the water pushing into it). A third defect sat in the gas: a pool cell uncovering a sliver of room read its own lagging fill as a suction of -20 to -30 kPa and held the water back; a surface now feels its gas space's volume-weighted pressure. **`tests/physics/liquid.js dam`, one cell deep (h₀ 0.467 m): the front runs 2.07 m/s over its first second and 1.93 m/s over 2.4–4.8 s, 0.48 of Ritter and 0.55 of Dressler's band mean; it decelerates, 0.95 → 0.89 → 0.80 √(g·h₀); the dam line reads 0.233 m against 4/9·h₀ = 0.207 (+12 %) and 1.09 m/s against 2/3·√(g·h₀) = 1.43 (−24 %).** **22/09/26, the test's truth was wrong again, and the stacked-cell lead is withdrawn.** The check detects the front at a 1 % contour, and Ritter's depth-h contour moves at 2·√(g·h₀) − 3·√(g·h), so at h = 0.01·h₀ the truth is **1.70·√(g·h₀)** (3.64 m/s), not 2·√(g·h₀); the test now computes it from its own detection depth. MODEL, stock only measured. **22/09/26, the limiter's own leak is closed: `eFaceLimit()` carries a pocket scale.** A sealed pocket's gas volume changes only by what the solve that priced its pressure delivered, so a limiter that cuts its outflows must cut its inflows with them. Beside `k` (a cell's outflow) and `ki` (a cell's inflow) the iteration now carries `kp[r]` per pocket, starting at 1 and only falling, multiplying every face whose receiver is in sealed pocket `r` and whose donor is not; a face between two cells of the same pocket is never scaled, so a compartment's own floor water still moves inside it. The allowance is `A_r = max(0, Σ_{i∈r} net_i/ρ_i)` m3, the per-cell `lcap` statement lifted from the cell to the pocket, and unsealed pockets (`gc.D[r] = ∞`) are never scaled. A face scaled by `kp` is scaled in the `k` and `ki` sums too, so all three constraints see each other and the fixed point holds; every scale multiplies a whole face, so nothing is booked off (`SC_MASSRES` 1.1e-15/kg against 5.4e-16 unscaled). That the relocated water landed in the violating pocket is measured only by coincidence of tick, not confirmed. (1) The compliance is `vg/(γ·R·T)` at the cell's OWN mixture and temperature, not `vg/(R·T)`. (2) **Energy rides the mass**: what crosses a face carries h of its donor and lands in a U at fixed volume, so a compressed cell really heats and there is no separate p·dV term. Implicit upwind, the same walk `eRoomAdvect()` runs on the species — a donor exports at its own NEW h, so every update is a convex blend and the step is bounded however much a cell passes; the sweeps settle what each donor leaves at and one conservative face pass moves it. (3) The temperature is a read off the energy (`eRoomTofUA()`, Newton on u(T) whose slope is c_v(T)), never a relaxation. **21/09/26, the gas and the liquid are solved against each other.** The liquid's face solve carries each gas component's adiabatic compliance (the cells joined by open gas faces, eliminated exactly so the solve stays SPD), rejoins gas the pass sealed and steps a squeezed pocket's compliance toward the secant of its isentrope (`LIQ_GAS_IT` passes), and a pocket takes no more than that solve gave it when the limiter runs; a surface feels its gas space's volume-weighted pressure. Each is first order in the tick's dV; what is left is the pricing's second-order ½·γ·p·dV²/V per cell per tick. | ...whether the room's transport conserves mass | a transport operator relocates what a cell holds and creates none of it, to the arithmetic's own precision | **since 20/09/26 exactly conservative by construction.** `eFaceTail()` grows one factor per donor cell from the always-safe start `k = min(1, M/out)`, alternating forward and backward Gauss-Seidel sweeps of `k[i] = min(1, (M[i] + sum of inflow x k[donor])/out[i])`, and applies it once to the entry-state faces. The map is monotone and bounded by 1, and a cell's true final inflow is never below the inflow its own bound was taken against, so **every iterate is feasible and any stopping point conserves**; the pass count `FACE_TAIL` 16 is a through-flow quality knob, not a correctness one. Before, the tail iterated DOWNWARD from k = 1, so every iterate before convergence was still infeasible, and its last pass computed a correction and returned without applying it. New state `ST.sc[SC_FACERES]`: the kilograms `eFaceMove()`'s `Math.max(0, ...)` creates, zeroed at the head of `eRoomStep()`, so all three calls in a tick are covered. **Measured 20/09/26**, `plantPreset(0)`, dice off, hot-leg break at `pipe:28,15` + floor hole at `mat:20,30` + 10 t/s at 12,15 over 6 s (`tools/liqprobe.js conserve`): **34.908 kg invented, worst tick 31.586 kg, against 0.000000 kg now.** In `tests/physics/roomgas.js move`, 200 ticks of the same scenario: 4.02e-13 kg, against a double-ulp floor of 1.49e-10 kg; the room's gas total over a 5 MPa BLAST holds to 1.43e-9 relative against an f32 storage floor of 100 x 2^-24; a 5 kg/s hydrogen source over 50 ticks raises the total by 5.0000133 kg against 5; no cell holds negative gas in any of them.
