|  what the BLAST fault injects | a charge releases ENERGY. What it is worth as a pressure is that energy over the volume it is released into, and what it leaves behind is a hot compartment that vents through whatever it opened | **21/09/26, the judge counts a front, not a shared rise.** A cell is judged on the part of its sudden change its compartment did not share: `bang = (P - Q) - (P̄ - Q̄)`, the bars the gas-volume-weighted means over the region's gas cells on the same tick (`eBang()`, `eRegExcess()`, `SX.regExc`); blast, scar and `RR_PMAX` all read it. A uniform rise has no front and loads a structure by its pressure difference, dynamic load factor 1 for a rise long against its period (Biggs 1964, ramp loading): measured, the core's compartment raised 2 MPa/s for 0.5 s through `roomBang()`'s own law wrecks **0** parts on the blast path (`blastdmg.js ramp`), where the old `P - Q` judge wrecked 111 (seen to fail). Steam-line break, stock, dice off: **0 blast-path wrecks in the first second**, the worst intact part `reltk` at 5.27 of its 25 kPa; before, `reltk` was wrecked at 0.04 s and `rods` at 0.06 s on a 35 → 110 kPa compartment-wide rise the one-pole baseline trailed by 28-44 kPa, which jammed the rods. `late`, `stale` and `charge` read as before. No `pburst` moved. **20/09/26, the damage-timing half is closed.** A structure fails on the peak side-on overpressure it sees, WHENEVER it sees it. `eBlastStep()`'s `burn` gate is deleted and `SC_ROOMBANG` with it: every part is judged every tick against its own cell's excess over a QUASI-STATIC baseline, `ST.roomPQs`, a one-pole lag whose time constant is DERIVED — `max(GW,GH)·MPC/sqrt(γ·R·T_HULL)` = 0.0843 s, the time sound takes to cross the board, so it is slower than the acoustic traverse and faster than anything a plant does. No fitted number enters it. `eScarStep()` reads the same baseline; `eRoomRegMean()` and `SX.rPStat` are deleted — one baseline, one concept. Measured (`tests/physics/blastdmg.js`): over 222 blast-limited parts, **every one is wrecked on exactly the tick its own cell first met its own limit, and 220 of them on a tick AFTER the one the charge was placed on** — under the old gate those 220 were never judged at all. A second charge 5 s after a first wrecks exactly the set that met its limit on that tick, 0 wrecked without and 0 met without being wrecked. A sustained 200 kg/s steam release leaves a dynamic excess of **0.94 % of the static rise it causes** and wrecks nothing on the blast path; frozen, the same baseline leaves 100 % and the check fails. **The 11/09/26 reading of 11 of 24 wreck sets pointing at the charge is now 19 of 24** (2000 kPa, 24 placements, re-measured). **11/09/26: nothing posts; the charge is heat into its cells (`roomBang()`, `dT = kJ/ROOM_CVAIR`) and the pressure is the read.** Measured, stock, 5 MPa beside `pump0`: the region's mean temperature goes to 835.8 K, whose constant-volume worth is 187.6 kPa gauge, and the read is 29.3 kPa at 1 s, 82.1 at 10 s, 105.7 at 20 s - no wall breaks (row: the gas is isothermal). What follows is the 10/09/26 text. Since 10/09/26 the figure typed stays MPa and is read as the constant-volume rise it is worth in the cell it is placed in: `roomBang()` does `dT = kJ/ROOM_CVAIR` and posts `ROOM_P0*dT/T_HULL`, exactly the two lines the hydrogen deflagration and the metal fire already had, which now call the same door. Measured: the deflagration reads 12.7127 kg burned, 15640.0 K peak and 6231.6 kPa peak wave through either spelling, to every digit | **the timing and the `ROOM_TMAX` dial cap are closed; the near field is not.** **before 10/09/26 the tool carried neither mass nor energy**: it posted a pressure onto a field that relaxed back to an attractor it had never moved, and 5 s after a 300 kPa blast the cell was at 293.06 K with nothing left. After, the same 300 kPa leaves the cell at 326.44 K. **5 MPa is now about 10.9 MJ, near 2.6 kg of TNT: 14 460 K in one 0.87 m3 cell, and 5.3 kPa quasi-static once it is shared over a 760 m3 compartment.** One thing follows and is stated rather than hidden: the near field is thrown away - the source cell is left at 14 755 K with its density unchanged, so the impedance step traps most of the impulse in the cell and the front that leaves it is small; measured, the whole 5 MPa gives a region peak of 10 kPa. **Re-measured 10/09/26 when the fourth difference replaced the second, and it MOVED - the wrong way.** Method, stated because it is not the one that gave the 10 kPa: the stock board's geometry with a hand-made empty room at 300 K and a zero gauge, `roomBang()` fed the same `kPa/ROOM_P0*T_HULL*ROOM_CVAIR` the tool feeds it, 150 ticks. The source cell reaches 14 762 K either way. The worst excess over gauge anywhere is **36.1 kPa at 0.06 s against 55.2 kPa at 0.12 s** on the second difference, and the worst cell other than the source one tick in is **16.9 kPa against 36.3**. Less dissipation makes the far field BIGGER on a cold post - 166.0 kPa against 53.4 one tick after a bare 5 MPa `roomBlastPost()` - and the near field SMALLER on a hot one, because the impulse now rings inside the hot cell instead of being smeared out of it by the damping. The trapped near field is therefore a stiffer gap than it was, not a softer one. **CLASS: MODEL on both.** The shield lift keeps `roomBlastPost()` and gains no heat, because a shield being thrown off is mechanical work. **11/09/26: the charge became a Gaussian (`roomBlastCharge()`, `BLAST_SIG` 2.4 cells) and lost its heat again** - a bare post. Every cell of the Gaussian now goes through `roomBang()` at its own share, so the heat is the pressure's constant-volume worth cell by cell. Measured on STOCK, 1 MPa at 21,28: 3139 K in the centre cell on the first tick, region mean gauge +68.2 kPa at 5 s and +27.6 kPa at 35 s. The dial starts at 1 MPa | **22/09/26, the dial cap is closed: a bigger charge is a wider one, never a hotter one (Sachs scaling).** Until now the source cell's air saturated at `ROOM_TMAX` and the energy over it was discarded (the cap read 6.8134 MPa on a bare STOCK cell, 9.929 MPa beside `pump0`). The cap is now read per act off the source cell's own air (`eRoomBangCapA()`, the exact inverse of `eRoomBangP()`); past it the peak stays at the cap and the width is bisected until the dial's Brode energy lands in the reach on the charge's side of every wall (`eRoomBlastLay()`), so a charge wider than its room still puts all its energy into that room. At or below the cap the charge is the old one, every figure in `charge`, `late`, `stale`, `ramp` unchanged to the digit. Measured in the sealed liner box (`blastdmg.js cap`, cap 9929 kPa there): landed/asked **1.000000000 at 20 MPa and at 100 MPa** (before: 0.844 and 0.329, seen to fail; a plain σ ∝ √(dial/cap) widening reached 0.931 at 100 MPa, the box walls clipping it); hottest cell at the act 20 000 K, 0 cell-ticks at the guard over the first second; the box's peak overpressure 1225 kPa at 20 MPa, 7639 kPa at 100 MPa. `tools/blastweigh.js` (the 20/09/26 weighing below) is deleted; its two answers live in this row. **20/09/26: the near field is NOT trapped in the source cell.** On the row's own case — 5 MPa beside `pump0`, nine Chebyshev rings out to the Gaussian's reach — the DEPOSIT is concentrated (14 755 K at ring 0 falling to 349 K at ring 8), but the PRESSURE is not: peak `ST.roomP` over the first 0.1 s reads 43.1–44.5 kPa on rings 0 through 7, with rings 2–4 slightly ABOVE the source cell, and only ring 8 lower at 37.3. The source cell's share of the summed ring peaks is **0.111, which is 1/9 — statistically uniform**, unchanged between 0.1 s and 1 s. Since the 11/09/26 Gaussian rewrite the pressure is already near-uniform across the near field inside one tick. The row's trapping claim described the superseded single-cell model. **21/09/26, weighed against Baker, in an empty sealed liner box so nothing but the charge acts** (`tests/physics/blastdmg.js charge`): the room gains the charge's Brode energy to 1e-9 (393.8 MJ at 5 MPa, the Gaussian cut at 3σ as the tool cuts it); 0 cell-ticks at `ROOM_TMAX`, the hottest cell 10 469 K. The near-field peak one cell outside the 1/e² radius is **407 kPa against Baker et al. 1983's starting shock of 2171 kPa** for p1/p0 50.3 and a 10 469 K source (0.19), and the peak falls from 616 kPa at the centre to 149 kPa 20 cells out. Energy all there, pressure not: the implicit solve spreads a charge over about 7 m inside one 0.02 s tick, which is decision 8's unresolved front (`the implicit step's front`), so the near field is a consequence of that cut and not a separate defect. The 20/09/26 ring table above is stale: fired beside `pump0` the charge now breaks the primary and the rings read the blowdown (6.6-10 MPa in 0.1 s; 4.0-7.8 MPa and one 2.7 GPa cell on the committed tree) | high on the energy, none on any real charge; the width law is Sachs scaling, not a measured fireball; the near field against Baker is YES, decision 8's, confirmed by the user 21/09/26  |
| the flux signal | a detector counts discrete neutron arrivals, so its scatter is 1/sqrt(N) and N goes with power: visibly noisy at low power, quiet at high. It is why a real startup is slow and why plants carry redundant channels | one smooth analytic number at every power. No reading anywhere carries measurement scatter | no measurement noise at all, so a low-power hold is exactly as easy as a full-power one and redundant instrument channels have nothing to vote against | DRIFT — nobody chose it, cut 24/09/26: every channel is single with no wired voter, so noise would move no trip and no control. The INSTRUMENT CHANNELS purchase stays deleted | high |
| an instrument channel | a transmitter fails, and it fails in ways that read: stuck at its last value, or slammed to full scale. It is the whole reason a plant carries three of them and votes 2-out-of-3 | every `source` block reads the plant directly and is always right. Nothing can fail one | no instrument fault exists, so three sources through a `sel`/median - which that block offers as how a plant votes a liar out - protects against nothing, and the instrument-cabinet hit can only stop the cabinet computing, never make it lie | DRIFT — nobody chose it, cut 24/09/26: no preset wires redundant channels, so a fault has nothing to vote against; the cabinet hit stays a computing stop, never a lie | high |
| what a blast breaks (`ROLE[].pburst`, `PIPE_PBURST`) | side-on overpressure damage is published (Clancey 1972, in Lees, Loss Prevention): 1 kPa typical window glass fails; 7 partial demolition of houses; 7 to 14 corrugated steel and aluminium panel fastenings fail, then the panels buckle; 9 steel frame of a clad building slightly distorted; 14 to 21 unreinforced block walls shattered; 21 heavy machines of about 1.4 t in an industrial building take little damage, steel frame building distorted and pulled off its foundations; 21 to 28 frameless steel panel building demolished and OIL STORAGE TANKS RUPTURE; 28 cladding of light industrial buildings ruptured; 35 wooden utility poles snapped, an 18 t hydraulic press slightly damaged; 48 loaded rail wagons overturned; 62 loaded boxcars demolished; 69 probable total destruction of buildings, heavy machine tools of about 3 t moved and badly damaged, very heavy ones of about 5.4 t survive. There is NO published overpressure rung for an electrical cabinet, a pipe wall or a pressure vessel: a vessel rated for MPa inside is not crushed by 200 kPa outside, and pipe fails at its supports and by displacement. Those are killed by missiles, by what they are bolted to moving, and by the fire, not by the pressure itself. **Searched 20/09/26** for a rung covering any of the unsourced classes: Clancey 1972 (in Lees), DoE-STD-3014, DoD 6055.09, the ASCE petrochemical blast guide, Baker et al. *Explosion Hazards and Evaluation*, Glasstone & Dolan, UK HSE. None carries an overpressure threshold for an electrical cabinet, a finned coil, a control-rod drive, a shell-and-tube exchanger, a pipe wall or a pressure vessel. The absence is now a searched result, not an assumption | one kPa per role, no source stated when written: radiator 15; ctrl, bkp, vent, inert 20; rods, cond 35; pump, turb 70; tank 100 (per instance override on `D.tanks[].pburst`); fitting, pipe 120 (the fitting row IS `PIPE_PBURST`); core, sg, ihx 200. `minPburst()` is the weakest fitted one and gates the bang event; `hurtStep()` ramps a sustained squeeze against the same figure. **Re-pinned 11/09/26: tank 100 to 25**, the storage-tank rupture rung. Pump and turbine at 70 sit on the 69 kPa heavy-machine-tool rung and were left where they were. **Re-pinned 12/09/26: fitting 70 to 120**, written as `PIPE_PBURST` itself rather than a copy of it: a tee, a throttle and a relief valve are bodies in the pipe run, and a valve that let go before the pipe either side of it put the weakest thing on the board inside the run rather than at its supports. It is a consistency argument, not a measurement - the 69 kPa rung it leaves was the sourced figure and 120 is not sourced at all. Everything else was left as it was and is listed here as unsourced | pump, turb: on a published rung. Tank: now on one. Fitting: no rung since 12/09/26 - it inherits pipe's, which is the wrong quantity, and the trade was a sourced figure for a self-consistent run, and the 69 kPa it left was sourced to the wrong OBJECT (a 3 t machine tool, not a pipe fitting), so the trade gave up a real citation without gaining one. Radiator 15 is under the 7 to 14 cladding rung but a radiator panel is not building cladding; no rung. Cabinets 20, rods 35, condenser 35: no rung. Pipe 120 and the three vessels at 200: no rung exists and the failure mode is not pressure, so any figure here is the wrong quantity. The label ladder `BLASTFX.zones` in `render/room.js` (20, 70, 120, 200) is a copy of this table and inherits every gap | **YES - DECIDED 21/09/26 by the user, on the 20/09/26 search.** The sources named above carry no overpressure rung for an electrical cabinet, a finned coil, a control-rod drive, a shell-and-tube exchanger, a pipe wall or a pressure vessel, and for most of those the real failure is missiles, mounting failure and fire rather than side-on overpressure. The figures are therefore a ladder chosen to read well, and they stay as they are: an unsourced number swapped for another unsourced number buys nothing. A decision, not an oversight; `BLASTFX.zones` carries the same decision | high on the Clancey rungs; high that the other five classes have no published rung at all, after the 20/09/26 search; none on the figures themselves, which have no rung to be sure of |

## Water and steam properties

Checked by `tests/physics/props.js` against the IAPWS-IF97 verification tables (tables 5, 15, 33, the B23 line) and its saturation table, through an independent test-side IF97 (`tests/physics/lib.js`: regions 1, 2, 3, B23, `if97pT()`) that is itself checked against those tables to 1e-8 (19/09/26). Since 19/09/26 all water is IF97: region 4 for the saturation line, regions 1, 2 and 3 for everything else, including the dome's own h_f, h_g, rho_f and rho_g at psat (Wagner & Pruss and the Clapeyron latent heat are gone). Single-phase water is one table over (p, h) and (T, p), built once at load (`WT_*` in `pipenet.js`); a node reads T and rho at its own pressure. No water `COOLANT` row states a figure: `coolFig()` derives rho, Ts, h_fg and the rise's c_p from IF97 at the row's own P0 and Tref.

| what | real machine | this model | distance | on purpose? | sure |

The saturated line `hOfT()` is kept for a T that is a saturation temperature | 115.33, 184.14 and 975.54 against the same (19/09/26); c_p 4.173, 4.010, 4.655 against 4.173, 4.010, 4.656 | — | high |

At rest, dice off: RBMK-1000 exit quality **0.140** at drum pressure (`rbmk.js boil`), inlet subcooling **14.9 K** below the drum's own saturation (PASS against the published ~14 K). BWR/4 **0.497** at 0.545 of rated flow with a saturated inlet (GAP, row "BWR/4 cycle"). Seen to fail on HEAD with `xOut` on the rows: subcooling 30 vs 9.28 K, flow 12.74 and 5.49 kg/s per MWt, RBMK exit quality 0.082 | BWR/4 -5 % per MWt against the published figure; RBMK-1000 **+2.3 %**, where it was +15 % and all of that was the feed temperature | YES on the law. The **DRIFT** on the RBMK's feed temperature is closed; `FEED_T0` stays the one fallback for a plant that states none. BWR/4 at rest: **BUILD** (row "BWR/4 cycle") | medium on the published flows |
| heat capacity rate of a water stream across an exchanger | the stream's h(T) between its inlet and what it can reach | **since 24/09/26 (batch F) the stream's own T(h), piece by piece.** At a fixed pressure the model's T(h) is straight between the water table's h nodes and the dome's two edges (`hxLineA()`/`hxTA()`/`hxNextA()`, pipenet.js; a stated c_p is one piece a side of its dome, a Shomate gas a piece every 5 kJ/kg), and the law prices each piece exactly as q/LMTD, so the profile along the tube is the model's own and no single exponential. `eStageSecant()` is left only for the settle's NTU ceiling | none: the next row | — | high |
| Steam generator effectiveness | a stream against a boiling shell (Cr = 0) whose cp varies: the integral of dh/(T(h) − Ts) over the stream is UA/w; in general, counterflow with both c_p varying, UA = ∫dq/(T_a(q) − T_b(q)) (Incropera & DeWitt ch. 11; Kays & London); heat flows from whichever side is hotter | **closed 24/09/26 (batch F): one exact counterflow law for every stage.** `hxLawA()` (pipenet.js) on each stream's own T(h) (row "heat capacity rate of a water stream across an exchanger"): against a side held at one temperature (a generator's shell) the stream is marched from its inlet until its NTU is spent, the last piece solved in closed form; between two streams (an exchanger, a superheater) the heat is the root of `hxReqA()`, the UA a heat needs, summed exactly over the union of both streams' pieces, in u = −ln(1 − q/q_pinch). A stream inside its dome is one piece at its own temperature, so a condensing primary and a boiling shell need no special case. `eSgQ()`/`eIhxQ()` load both streams (`eStageLoadA()`) and call it; `sgUASuggest()` asks `hxReqA()` at the rated streams. Heat either way: the inlet is the neighbour of the face the flow enters by (`PT.stageNbrMid`), the stage runs with the roles swapped when the taking inlet is hotter, the film reads each stream's actual direction, and the take-up caps carry the sign. The shell side is the shell node's own h at the shell pressure, saturation only when it is saturated. `hx.js`: constant c_p against Kays & London at five (NTU, Cr) to 1e-9; water/water and CO2 against superheating steam against the test's own two-stream quadrature, 4e-11 and 1.4e-7 (the old secant law with steam as an infinite capacity rate: 66 % off); STOCK PWR, NUSCALE, EPR generators against the fine quadrature, 3e-10, 4e-11, 5e-10 (was −2.4 %, −2.55 %, −2.77 %); a condensing stream in its dome q = UA·ΔT to 1e-9; STOCK PWR's primary 20 K under its shell gives heat back, 6e-11 against the quadrature heating the stream, first law across the tubes exact (the old max(0, ·): 0); a shell 15 K subcooled takes heat at its own temperature (read at saturation: 25 % off). Cost: 3.3 µs per generator per tick, 0.15 % of a warm STOCK tick and 0.27 % for BWR/4's two (was 1.5 µs, 0.07 %); the 11 ms of 20/09/26 was the fine quadrature, not the law. Rest: the settle fits the UA to the same heat, STOCK −4.6 %, NUSCALE −4.9 %, EPR −5.0 %, DUAL −4.8 %, heat, loop T and shell pressure inside the settle's own 1e-4; CALDER HALL's generator sits on its NTU ceiling and its loop stands 0.18 K colder. **BWR/4 no longer flies: BUILD, row "BWR/4 cycle".** Accident, recorded: STOCK PWR hit on pipe:28,15 at 1 s, the generator starts giving heat back at 6.46 s (primary inlet 548.0 K against its shell's 548.1) and gives up to 120 MW at 6.82 s, the energy residual 3e-11 of the core heat over 60 s | none | — | high |
| two-phase film on an exchanger | a stream's film follows its own quality and pressure: a condensing stream transfers better than liquid (Shah 1979), an evaporating one by its convective enhancement (Chen 1966), both toward the vapour-only film as x goes to 1; a stream that is liquid is untouched by any void elsewhere | **built 24/09/26 (batch K phase 3).** One film function, `eFilmA()` (machines.js), for the generators, the intermediate exchangers and the radiators, the ratio over the liquid-only film at the same mass flux: giving stream Shah (1979), read at Shah's own restatement (HVAC&R 15:5, 2009), h_TP/h_LT = (1 − x)^0.8 + 3.8 x^0.76 (1 − x)^0.04 / p_r^0.38; taking stream Chen (1966)'s convective part (1 − x)^0.8 F, F = 2.35 (1/X_tt + 0.213)^0.736, 1 when 1/X_tt ≤ 0.1, X_tt and Re_L on G(1 − x) read at Thome, Engineering Data Book III ch. 10 eqs 10.3.4–10.3.8 (whose F prints without the 2.35, the factor that makes F = 1 at the 0.1 threshold), its nucleate part not counted; both floored at (k_g/k_f)(μ_f/μ_g)^0.8(Pr_g/Pr_f)^0.4, on saturated water through IF97 and the IAPWS transport doors. A generator's UA is stageUA/(φ/r + 1 − φ) on its tube stream, φ 0.5; an exchanger 1/(½/r_a + ½/r_b); a radiator its fluid's r. The old `1 − 0.85·x` on the stages and radiators and the generator's `filmK` off the CORE's void are gone, from the tick and from commissioning's UA fit alike. `hx.js tp`: Shah and Chen against the formulas typed there at x 0.1/0.5/0.9, 7 and 15 MPa, 0; a condensing stream at 7 MPa, x 0.1–0.9, at least 1.94× liquid (fault: 1 − 0.85 x reads 0.235); STOCK PWR's generator the same heat at a core void of 0 and 0.5 (fault: the film off the core's void moves it −0.293). Rest: STOCK PWR's fitted UA moved 7e-5 (the settle's own path to the same point); 120 s flights before and after: STOCK PWR, NUSCALE, EPR, DUAL pass every check, moves ~1e-4; BWR/4 (two-phase water through its generators) passes every check, heat balance 1.0045 → 1.0009, core heat 0.957 → 0.962 | φ, the tube film's share of a clean generator's resistance, 0.5 is a FIT: no published split was found. Chen's nucleate part is not counted, and no exchanger carries tube geometry of its own (stageUA is fitted) | φ: a FIT inside a PWR generator's minority-to-half | high on Shah and Chen (read at restatements); medium on φ |
| Drum at rest | a drum at rest passes what arrives: riser in equals steam and downcomer out, in mass and in enthalpy, and the core's heat is the steam out less the feed in | **closed on both plants, 19/09/26.** The drum probe (`drum.js probe`): mass 6.5e-5 of its steam, first law 1.9e-4, circuit 1.0002; 60 s march (`drum.js march`): mass 4e-11, energy 4e-12, pressure, level and steam all within 1.4e-4 of commissioned. RBMK-1000 (`drum.js 5`): drum0 mass 3.6e-5 and first law 3.6e-5, drum1 1e-13 and 2e-9, circuit 1.00001, each loop's channels heat = w(h_out − h_in) to 4e-6 of their share. What closed RBMK-1000, beyond the probe's fixes: one core water node per loop (row "a tube core's water, per loop"), and the governor fit compared the nozzle flow through a bleed read taken before `eInHSeed()` moved it (15.7 kg/s short on drum0); the condenser and feed settle now re-read the inflow and the turbine every pass. Injected: the split core folded back fails drum0 mass (−0.46) and drum1 first law (−0.18) | none | — | high |
| a tube core's water, per loop | a pressure-tube core has no plenum: each loop's group headers feed its own half of the channels (INSAG-7 annex II: "two parallel loops each of which cools half of the fuel channels") | **since 19/09/26** a tube core piped into several loops is one network node per loop (`coreLoops()`, `coreLoopNode()`, `runEndNode()` in `pipenet.js`): loop k > 0 is the core's id and one mark, so every reader that slices a face off a name still finds the core; a run's loop is `loopMap()` of its far end, an unclaimed run (HPI) lands on the first. Each node takes 1/n of the heat, the vessel, floor and cavity openings are one edge per node (cavity channels split by n), `coreInH` is inflow-weighted over the nodes and `eCoreWaterA()` (core.js) is the one door for mass, volume and pressure. The design graph (`nodeGraph()`) keeps one core node | the neutronic mesh is r-z and cannot tell the halves apart: every mesh node reads the flow-weighted mean of the halves' water, so a real core's halves can differ and this one's cannot, neutronically. Loop flows 1802 / 1865 kg/s (the drawn loop 0 goes through the surge tee) | **YES** on the mesh; a vessel core is untouched | high |

## Steady state against its family

A preset is inspired by its namesake (row "Plant size"). What counts here is BEHAVIOUR its family
shows (a direct cycle boils in the core, a sodium plant keeps its water off the primary); a
pressure, flow or MWe off the namesake's own sheet is a deliberate cut and is recorded, not worked.

`tests/physics/presets.js`, 120 s from commissioning, 18/09/26. Mass and energy close on all nine (mass under 6e-10 of inventory, energy under 7e-11 of core heat; re-run after the reference solve moved onto the engine).

| RBMK-1000 power regulator | the RCPS automatically maintains the preset power level (INSAG-7 annex II) on in-core and ex-core chambers; the turbine holds the drum | `buildPowerAuto()` (`ctl.js`, section POWER REG) on every direct cycle in place of the T-avg controller: `nfr` against a CONST setpoint (the operator's demand, 1), times `TPROG_SPAN` so the plant's own rod tune applies, clamped ±6 K, PID with blank gains (the plant's tune) and no RATE, i.e. rod speed proportional to the error; dead band 0. `rbmk.js`: at rest power held to 4.5e-5 of rated over 60 s, drum to 2e-5, core heat = steam − feed to 1e-5; injected, rod sink off, 0.98 of rated off (fails). The rods run at the RBMK's own 0.4 m/s: `ARCHPRE` RBMK states `rodSpd` 0.4/7, `archPreset()` sets it or deletes it like `tube`, `P.rodRate` and `PT.coreRodRate` follow, `autorodTune()` does not read it; `rbmk.js scram`: normal stroke **17.5 s** and scram rate = drive rate, one servo (PASS; 83.3 s and 0.012 against 0.057 on the tree before, fails). The turbine governor (`loadDem`'s PID, `PRESS_KP` 1000, `PRESS_TI` **67 s**, `step.js`) holds the drum in an outer loop around the rods. No published RBMK governor tune was found (19/09/26: OSTI abstracts of *Power Technology and Engineering* 49(1) "Improvement of Algorithms for Pressure Maintenance Systems in Drum-Separators of RBMK-1000 Reactors" and ETDEWEB 6558032 "Tuning parameter choice for the level and pressure regulators in the RBMK-1000 steam-drum" name the subject and give no figure; OSTI full text refused the connection; the Kyoto Malko PDF did not read), so Ti is derived: 3× the rod regulator's Ti 22.2 s (`autorodTune()`), the low end of the 3–5× separation for nested loops. `rbmk.js stepoff` checks the other side: governor off after the step, the drum drifts as p∞ + a·e^(−t/τ) with τ **97 s** (fit on 30/130/230 s) toward 6.19 MPa, slower than Ti (PASS). A −10 % demand step (19/09/26, `rbmk.js step`): power **0.44 % off** the setpoint over the last 20 s (PASS, limit 0.9 %), **0.20 % peak to peak** (PASS, limit 1 %), 0.9024–0.9044; worst undershoot 0.042 of rated; last outside 1 % at **29.8 s** (19.8 s after the step). Drum over the 90 s **6.713–6.913 MPa**, flown on to 180 s once 6.713–6.949 (peak 0.7 % over) and settling back toward 6.90, against the drawing's own limits read at run time: safety valve lift 7.314 MPa (0.365 MPa clear) and low-pressure trip 5.934 MPa (0.779 MPa clear); no published drum pressure band was found. Injected (`rbmk.js stepdeep`): governor off and a −20 % step leave the trip at 118.1 s (fails). Governor off at −10 % never leaves: with a fixed turbine valve steam flow goes with pressure, so the drum falls about as far as the power, 6.90 → 6.19 = −10.3 %, inside the 14 % trip | with `AUTOROD_DB` (0.8 K, 4.4 % of power through the slope) the power wandered to the band's edges and escaped at 110 s, so the band is 0: the chambers carry no noise here. Before 19/09/26 the governor's Ti was 5 s, inside the rods' band, and the step swung round 0.90 at a ~40 s period (1.15 % off, 1.48 % peak to peak); the rods were not why (0.012 and 0.057 of travel/s read the same). The drum recovers slowly: 90 s after the step it is still 0.2 % over 6.90 MPa | **BUILD**, RBMK-1000: the governor's tune, closed by the derived Ti; the rod speed is BUILD too and closed.
